\Gamma(U,u_{X/\Sigma_{e\inf}*}\mathcal{G})
is defined as the
0
-th cohomology
\Gamma(U/\Sigma_{e\inf},\mathcal{G})
,
and similarly for its filtered analogue.
So to show the vanishing of
Ru_{X/\Sigma_{e}*}(\mathcal{J}_{X/\Sigma_{e}}^{j}\mathcal{F}\otimes\Omega_{X/\Sigma_{e\inf}}^{i})
, it suffices to do this locally and assume
X
is affinoid, together with a closed immersion into a smooth rigid space
Y
over
\Sigma_{e\inf}
.
We then notice that by Proposition
4.1.3
,
Ru_{X/\Sigma_{e}*}(\mathcal{J}_{X/\Sigma_{e}}^{j}\mathcal{F}\otimes\Omega_{X/\Sigma_{e\inf}}^{i})
is computed by the following cosimplicial complex:

J^{j}\mathcal{F}\otimes\Omega_{X/\Sigma_{e\inf}}^{i}(D)\longrightarrow J(1)^{j}\mathcal{F}\otimes\Omega_{X/\Sigma_{e\inf}}^{i}(D(1))\longrightarrow J(2)^{j}\mathcal{F}\otimes\Omega_{X/\Sigma_{e\inf}}^{i}(D(2))\longrightarrow\cdots,

which by Lemma
4.1.4
is homotopic to zero when
i>0
.
So we get the vanishing of
Ru_{X/\Sigma_{e}*}(\mathcal{J}_{X/\Sigma_{e}}^{j}\mathcal{F}\otimes\Omega_{X/\Sigma_{e\inf}}^{i})
for each
i>0
and
j\in\mathbb{N}
; in other words, the filtered sheaf of complexes
Ru_{X/\Sigma_{e}*}(\mathcal{F}\otimes\Omega_{X/\Sigma_{e\inf}}^{i})
vanishes for
i>0
.
By induction, the latter in particular shows that the truncation map

Ru_{X/\Sigma_{e}*}(\mathcal{F}\otimes\Omega_{X/\Sigma_{e\inf}}^{\leq i})\longrightarrow Ru_{X/\Sigma_{e}*}\mathcal{F}

is a filtered quasi-isomorphism for each
i>0
.
As a consequence, since the filtered complex
Ru_{X/\Sigma_{e}*}(\mathcal{F}\otimes\Omega_{X/\Sigma_{e\inf}}^{\bullet})
is the derived limit of
Ru_{X/\Sigma_{e}*}(\mathcal{F}\otimes\Omega_{X/\Sigma_{e\inf}}^{\leq i})
, we get the isomorphism as claimed in the second half of the statement.
∎

Now we can generalize Theorem
4.1.1
to the global case, without assuming the affinoid condition:

Theorem 4.2.2
.

Let
X\rightarrow Y
be a closed immersion of
X
into a smooth rigid space
Y
over
\Sigma_{e}
.
Let
\mathcal{F}
be a coherent crystal over
\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma_{e}}
, and let
\mathcal{F}_{D}=\varprojlim_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{F}_{Y_{m}}
be the restriction of
\mathcal{F}
at the envelope
D=D_{X}(Y)=\varinjlim_{m\in\mathbb{N}}Y_{m}
, with its de Rham complex
\mathcal{F}_{D}\otimes\Omega_{D}^{\bullet}
.
Then there exists a natural isomorphism in the filtered derived category of sheaves of abelian groups over
X

Ru_{X/\Sigma_{e}*}\mathcal{F}\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{D}\otimes\Omega_{D}^{\bullet}.

Before the proof, we want to mention that the strategy is to produce a natural map between those two complexes of sheaves of abelian groups, where the isomorphism will follow from the affinoid computation.

Proof.

By Proposition
4.2.1
, the truncation map of the de Rham complex
\mathcal{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma_{e}}}\Omega_{X/\Sigma_{e\inf}}^{\bullet}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}[0]
produces a canonical filtered isomorphism in the derived category of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules

Ru_{X/\Sigma_{e}*}(\mathcal{F}\otimes\Omega_{X/\Sigma_{e\inf}}^{\bullet})\rightarrow Ru_{X/\Sigma_{e}*}\mathcal{F}.

On the other hand, we recall that the envelope
D=D_{X}(Y)
is defined as the direct limit
\varinjlim_{m\in\mathbb{N}}h_{Y_{m}}
of representable sheaves, where
Y_{m}
is the
m
-th infinitesimal neighborhood of
X
into
Y
.
In the infinitesimal topos
\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{e\inf})
, the map from the envelope
D
to the final object
1
induces a map of derived functors

R\Gamma(X/\Sigma_{e\inf},-)\longrightarrow R\Gamma(D,-)=R\varprojlim_{m}R\Gamma(Y_{m},-).

Similarly for its filtered analogue.

We apply the natural transformation to the filtered de Rham complex
\mathcal{F}\otimes\Omega_{X/\Sigma_{e\inf}}^{\bullet}
, and get

\displaystyle R\Gamma(X/\Sigma_{e\inf},\mathcal{F}\otimes\Omega_{X/\Sigma_{e\inf}}^{\bullet})

\displaystyle\rightarrow R\varprojlim_{m}R\Gamma(Y_{m},\mathcal{F}_{Y_{m}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y_{m}}}\Omega_{Y_{m}/\Sigma_{e}}^{\bullet})

\displaystyle=R\Gamma(X,R\varprojlim_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{F}_{Y_{m}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y_{m}}}\Omega_{Y_{m}/\Sigma_{e}}^{\bullet})

\displaystyle=R\Gamma(X,\mathcal{F}_{D}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{D}}\Omega_{D}^{\bullet}),

where the last equality follows from the observation that the inverse system
\{\mathcal{F}_{Y_{m}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y_{m}}}\Omega_{Y_{m}/\Sigma_{e}}^{\bullet}\}_{m}
admits a finite filtration, where each graded piece
\{\mathcal{F}_{Y_{m}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y_{m}}}\Omega_{Y_{m}/\Sigma_{e}}^{i}\}_{m}
is a pro-coherent system satisfying the sheaf vertion Mittag-Leffler condition (
[
BO78
, Lemma 7.20]
).
Similarly for the subcomplex
\mathcal{J}_{X/\Sigma_{e}}^{m-\bullet}\mathcal{F}\otimes\Omega_{X/\Sigma_{e\inf}}^{\bullet}
.
Notice that the map is functorialial with respect to all locally closed immersions
(X,Y)
into smooth rigid spaces.
In particular, by varying
X
among all open subsets
U
of
X
and considering the above map for locally closed immersions
(U,Y)
, we could enhance the above into the sheaf version filtered morphism

Ru_{X/\Sigma_{e}*}(\mathcal{F}\otimes\Omega_{X/\Sigma_{e\inf}}^{\bullet})\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{D}\otimes_{\mathcal{D}}\Omega_{D}^{\bullet}.

Thus by composing with (the inverse of) the filtered isomorphism at the beginning, we get a natural map in the filtered derived category of sheaves of abelian groups over
X
:

Ru_{X/\Sigma_{e}*}\mathcal{F}\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{D}\otimes_{\mathcal{D}}\Omega_{D}^{\bullet}.

At last, to show the filtered isomorphism, we note that the evaluation functor defined in the second paragraph of the proof is compatible with restriction onto open affinoid subspaces of
Y
and
X
.
In particular, as the map is analytic local with respect to
X
, the compatibility allows us to reduce to the case when both
X
and
Y
are affinoid.
In the latter case, we know by Theorem
4.1.1
that the map is a filtered isomorphism, which finishes the proof.
∎

As a consequence, we get a change of bases formula quite easily.

Proposition 4.2.3
.

Let
X
be a rigid space over
\Sigma_{e}
, and
e^{\prime}\geq e
be an integer.
Let
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
be a crystal in vector bundles over
X/\Sigma_{e^{\prime}\inf}
, and
\mathcal{F}
be the pullback of
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
along the map of infinitesimal topoi
\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{e\inf})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{e^{\prime}\inf})
.
Then there exists a natural isomorphism of complexes of sheaves of
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR},e^{\prime}}^{+}
-modules as below

(Ru_{X/\Sigma_{e^{\prime}}*}\mathcal{F}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR},e^{\prime}}^{+}}^{L}{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}\longrightarrow Ru_{X/\Sigma_{e}*}\mathcal{F}.

Proof.

We first notice that the natural morphism of infinitesimal sites
X/\Sigma_{e\inf}\rightarrow X/\Sigma_{e^{\prime}\inf}
induces a canonical map in the derived category of sheaves over
X

Ru_{X/\Sigma_{e^{\prime}}*}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\longrightarrow Ru_{X/\Sigma_{e}*}\mathcal{F}.

Moreover, as the target is
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
-linear, by the adjunction for the forgetful functor (from
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
-modules to
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR},e^{\prime}}^{+}
-modules) we get a natural map of complexes

(Ru_{X/\Sigma_{e^{\prime}}*}\mathcal{F}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR},e^{\prime}}^{+}}^{L}{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}\longrightarrow Ru_{X/\Sigma_{e}*}\mathcal{F}.

So it suffices to show this adjunction map is an isomorphism.

As the statement is analytic local with respect to
X
, by shrinking
X
if necessarily, we can assume that
there exists a closed immersion
X\rightarrow Y^{\prime}
of
X
into a smooth rigid space over
\Sigma_{e^{\prime}}
.
By Theorem
4.2.2
, we have the following natural isomorphisms

\displaystyle Ru_{X/\Sigma_{e^{\prime}}*}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{D^{\prime}}\otimes\Omega_{D^{\prime}}^{\bullet};

\displaystyle Ru_{X/\Sigma_{e}*}\mathcal{F}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{D}\otimes\Omega_{D}^{\bullet},

where
D^{\prime}
is the envelope of
X
in
Y^{\prime}
, and
D
is the envelope of
X
in
Y=Y^{\prime}\times_{\Sigma_{e^{\prime}}}\Sigma_{e}
where the latter is smooth over
\Sigma_{e}
.
Notice that
\mathcal{O}_{Y^{\prime}}
is flat over
\Sigma_{e^{\prime}}
, and the structure sheaves
\mathcal{O}_{D^{\prime}}
is flat over
\mathcal{O}_{Y^{\prime}}
(for it is defined as the formal completion of
\mathcal{O}_{Y^{\prime}}
along
X\rightarrow Y^{\prime}
).
In this way, by the assumption that
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
is a crystal in vector bundles, the complex
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{D^{\prime}}\otimes\Omega_{D^{\prime}}^{\bullet}
is a
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR},e^{\prime}}^{+}
-linear bounded complex of sheaves of flat
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR},e^{\prime}}^{+}
-modules.
Thus we get the isomorphisms

\displaystyle(Ru_{X/\Sigma_{e^{\prime}}*}\mathcal{F}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR},e^{\prime}}^{+}}^{L}{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}

\displaystyle\simeq(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{D^{\prime}}\otimes\Omega_{D^{\prime}}^{\bullet})\otimes_{\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR},e^{\prime}}^{+}}^{L}{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}

\displaystyle\simeq(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{D^{\prime}}\otimes\Omega_{D^{\prime}}^{\bullet})/\xi^{e},

which is then isomorphic to the complex
\mathcal{F}_{D}\otimes\Omega_{D}^{\bullet}
as the envelope
D=D_{X}(Y)
is equal to the pullback of
D^{\prime}=D_{X}(Y^{\prime})
along the surjection
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR},e^{\prime}}^{+}\rightarrow{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}=\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR},e^{\prime}}^{+}/\xi^{e}
.
∎

5.
Derived de Rham cohomology over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}

In this section, we introduce the derived de Rham cohomology of a rigid space over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
, and prove the comparison between the derived de Rham cohomology and the infinitesimal cohomology.

Before we start, we want to mention that we will use mildly the language of
\infty
-category throughout this section.
The main reason is to globalize the affinoid constructions and get a good theory of “sheaf of derived objects”, using the
\infty
-categorical cohomological descent.

Remark 5.0.1
.

The construction of the analytic derived de Rham complex in this section can be applied to more general class of analytic Huber rings, which includes for example rigid spaces over an arbitrary
p
-adic non-archimedean field, and perfectoid spaces.

The results of this section hold true for rigid spaces over a general
p
-adic fields.
Moreover, in an upcoming work
[
GL20
]
by Shizhang Li and the author, we show that the analytic derived de Rham complex of perfectoid rings is isomorphic to the de Rham period sheaves in
[
Sch13
]
.

Derived
\infty
-category and filtered
\infty
-category.

We first setup the convention of derived
\infty
-category and its filtered version in this section.

Let
\mathscr{A}
be a Grothendieck abelian category (
[
Sta
, Tag 079A]
).
We can associate to
\mathscr{A}
a natural
\infty
-category
\mathscr{D}(\mathscr{A})
, called the
derived
\infty
-category of
\mathscr{A}
(
[
Lu17
]
, 1.3.5).
This is the
\infty
-categorical enhancement of the classical derived
\infty
-category, and the homotopy category
\mathrm{h}{\mathrm{Ch}}(\mathscr{A})
of
\mathscr{D}(\mathscr{A})
is the usual derived category
D(\mathscr{A})
.
Here we want to mention that the derived
\infty
-category
\mathscr{D}(\mathscr{A})
is a stable presentable
\infty
-category.
In the special case when
\mathscr{A}
is the category of modules over an ring
R
, we use
\mathscr{D}(R)
to denote
\mathscr{D}(\mathscr{A})
, which is equipped with a symmetric monoidal structure by the derived tensor product of complexes.
As a convention in this section, we will call
\mathscr{D}(R)
the derived category.

For a presentable
\infty
-category
\mathscr{C}
, we recall the
filtered
\infty
-category in
\mathscr{C}
is defined as the
\infty
-category

{\mathscr{DF}}(\mathscr{C}):=\textrm{Fun}(\mathbb{N}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathscr{C}).

Moreover,
{\mathscr{DF}}(\mathscr{C})
admits a full sub-
\infty
-category
\widehat{\mathscr{DF}}(\mathscr{C})
, called
filtered complete
\infty
-category in
\mathscr{C}
, consisting of objects
C_{\bullet}
such that
\lim C_{\bullet}\simeq 0
.
The natural inclusion functor
\widehat{\mathscr{DF}}(\mathscr{C})\rightarrow{\mathscr{DF}}(\mathscr{C})
admits a left adjoint, called the
filtered completion
.
When
\mathscr{C}=\mathscr{D}(R)
is the derived
\infty
-category of
R
-modules, we use
{\mathscr{DF}}(R)
and
\widehat{\mathscr{DF}}(R)
to denote
{\mathscr{DF}}(\mathscr{C})
and
\widehat{\mathscr{DF}}(R)
respectively.
Here we note that by to their homotopy categories (and induced functors), we recover the ordinary filtered derived category.

Hypersheaves

We then give a quick review about sheaves of complexes.

Let
X
be a site, and let
\mathscr{C}
be a presentable
\infty
-category.
The
\infty
-category of presheaves in
\mathscr{C}
, denoted as
\mathrm{PSh}(X,\mathscr{C})
,
is defined to be the
\infty
-category
\textrm{Fun}(X^{\mathrm{op}},\mathscr{C})
of contravariant functors from
X
to
\mathscr{C}
.
The
\infty
-category
\mathrm{PSh}(X,\mathscr{C})
admits a full sub-
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Sh}(X,\mathscr{C})
of
(infinity) sheaves in
\mathscr{C}
,
consisting of functors
\mathcal{F}:X^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}
that send coproducts to products and satisfy the descent along Čech nerves:
for any covering
U^{\prime}\rightarrow U
in
X
,
the natural morphism to the limit below is required to be a weak equivalence

\mathcal{F}(U)\longrightarrow\lim_{[n]\in\Delta}\mathcal{F}(U^{\prime}_{n}),

where
U^{\prime}_{\bullet}\rightarrow U
is the Čech nerve associated with the covering
U^{\prime}\rightarrow U
.
Here we note that this is the
\infty
-categorical analogue of the classical sheaf condition in ordinary categories.

There is a stronger descent condition which requires
(\ast)
above to hold
with respect to all
hypercovers

U^{\prime}_{\bullet}\rightarrow U
in the site
X
.
Sheaves satisfying such stronger condition are called
hypersheaves
.
For example, given any bounded below complex
C
of ordinary sheaves on a site
X
,
the assignment
U\mapsto\mathrm{R}\Gamma(U,C)

gives rise to a hypersheaf.
The collection of hypersheaves in
\mathscr{C}
forms a full
sub-
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Sh}^{{\mathrm{hyp}}}(X,\mathscr{C})
inside
\mathrm{Sh}(X,\mathscr{C})
.

Let
\mathscr{C}=\mathscr{D}(R)
be the derived
\infty
-category of
R
-modules.
Then the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X,\mathscr{C})
of hypersheaves over
X
is in fact equivalent to the derived
\infty
-category
\mathscr{D}(X,R)
of classical sheaves of
R
-modules over
X
, by
[
Lu18
, Corollary 2.1.2.3]
.
As an upshot, the underlying homotopy category of
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X,\mathscr{C})
is the classical derived category of sheaves of
R
-modules over
X
.
In particular, given a hypersheaf
\mathcal{F}
of
R
-modules over
X
, we can always represent it by an actual complex of sheaves of
R
-modules.

5.1.
Topological algebras over
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}

As a preparation, we first setup basics around the topologically finite type algebras over
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}:={\mathrm{A_{inf}}}/\xi^{e}
, and the construction of the analytic cotangent complex, generalizing the discussion for
e=1
in
[
GR
]
Section 7.

In this subsection only, we make the convention that
M^{\wedge}
is the classical
p
-adic completion of
M
, where
M
is a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-module.

Definition 5.1.1
.

Let
R
be an
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}
-algebra.

(1)

We call
R
is
topologically finite type over
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}
if there exists a surjection of
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}
-algebras
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}\langle T_{1},\ldots,T_{m}\rangle\rightarrow R
for some
m\in\mathbb{N}
.

(2)

We call
R
is
topologically of finite presentation over
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}
if
R
admits a surjection from
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}\langle T_{1},\ldots,T_{m}\rangle\rightarrow R
with kernel being a finitely generated ideal.

We denote
{\mathrm{Alg}_{\mathrm{tfp},e}}
the category of
p
-adically complete
p
-torsionfree algebras
R
over
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}
that are of topologically finite presentation, where the morphisms are defined as maps of
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}
-algebras.

Similarly, we can extend these notions to the relative situation, replacing
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}
by any
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}
-algebra.

Here we list some basic properties about modules over a given
R\in{\mathrm{Alg}_{\mathrm{tfp},e}}
.

Lemma 5.1.2

(cf.
[
GR
]
, 7.1.1)
.

Let
R
be an algebra in
{\mathrm{Alg}_{\mathrm{tfp},e}}
.
Then we have

(1)

Every finitely generated
p
-torsionfree
R
-module is finitely presented.

(2)

The ring
R
is coherent.

(3)

Let
N
be a finitely generated
R
-module,
N^{\prime}\subset N
a submodule.
Then there exists an integer
c\geq 0
, such that

p^{k}N\cap N^{\prime}\subset p^{k-c}N^{\prime}

for every
k\geq c
.
In particular, the subspace topology on
N^{\prime}
induced from the
p
-adic topology on
N
agrees with the
p
-adic topology of
N^{\prime}
.

(4)

Every finitely generated
R
-module
M
is
p
-adically complete and separated;
namely every such
M
is isomorphic to its
p
-adic completion
M^{\wedge}
.

(5)

Every submodule of a finite type free
R
-module
F
is closed for the
p
-adic topology of
F
.

Proof.

(1)

This is proved in the proof of
[
BMS
, 13.4. (iii.b)]
; for completeness, we record it here.
We do this by induction, and note that for
n=1
the case is given in
[
BoLü
]
1.2.

Let
\overline{M}
be the image of
M
in
M/\xi[\frac{1}{p}]
, and let
N
be the kernel of
M\rightarrow\overline{M}
.
The image
\overline{M}
is finitely generated
p
-torsionfree
R/\xi
-module, which by induction is a finitely presented
R/\xi
-module.
Note that this also implies the
R/\xi
-module
\overline{M}
is a finitely presented
R
-module.
So by
[
Sta
, Tag 0519]
,
N
is finitely generated over
R
, and to show the finite presented-ness of
M
it suffices to show the finite presentedness of
N
.
But note that for
x\in N
, there exists some
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
p^{k}x\in\xi M
.
This implies that
p^{k}\xi^{n-1}x=0
in
M
as the element is contained in
\xi^{n}M=0
, and by the
p
-torsionfreeness of
M
we have
\xi^{n-1}x=0
.
So
N
is a finitely generated
p
-torsionfree
R/\xi^{n-1}
-module, and by induction we get the result.

(2)

By definition, a ring
R
is coherent if every finitely generated ideal of
R
is finitely presented.
So by the
p
-torsionfreeness of
R
and (i), we get the result.

(3)

Let
M
be the kernel of the map
N\rightarrow N/N^{\prime}[\frac{1}{p}]
.
Namely we have the following short exact sequence

0\longrightarrow M\longrightarrow N\longrightarrow N/N^{\prime}[\frac{1}{p}].

Then since the image of
N
in
N/N^{\prime}[\frac{1}{p}]
is finitely generated and
p
-torsionfree, by (i) we know the image is finitely presented, and thus
M
is finitely generated (
[
Sta
, Tag 0519]
).
Note that the quotient
M/N^{\prime}
is
p^{\infty}
-torsion, so by the finitely generatedness there exists some
c\in\mathbb{N}
such that
p^{c}M\subset N^{\prime}
.
Besides, for
x\in N
such that
p^{k}x\in M
, the image of
x
in
N/N^{\prime}[\frac{1}{p}]
is also zero.
So the definition of
M
implies that
x\in M
and
p^{k}x\in p^{k}M
.
In this way, for
k\geq c
, we have

\displaystyle p^{k}N\cap N^{\prime}

\displaystyle\subset p^{k}N\cap M

\displaystyle\subset p^{k}M

\displaystyle\subset p^{k-c}N^{\prime}.

(4)

We can fit
M
into the following short exact sequence of
R
-modules,

0\longrightarrow N\longrightarrow R^{\oplus n}\longrightarrow M\longrightarrow 0,

We apply the
p
-adic completion to the sequence.
Then note that since the subspace topology on
N
is the isomorphic to the
p
-adic topology by (iii), while the quotient topology on
M
is the same as the
p
-adic topology, by
[
Mat86
]
Theorem 8.1, we get an exact sequence of
p
-adically complete
R
-modules with continuous maps

0\longrightarrow N^{\wedge}\longrightarrow R^{\oplus n}\longrightarrow M^{\wedge}\longrightarrow 0.

Compare with the above two exact sequences, we see the natural map
N\rightarrow N^{\wedge}
is injective while
M\rightarrow M^{\wedge}
is surjective.

We then assume the
R
-module
M
is finitely presented.
By the
[
Sta
, Tag 0519]
, we know
N
is finitely generated.
In this way, since the surjection of
M\rightarrow M^{\wedge}
is true for any finitely generated
R
-module, we see
N\rightarrow N^{\wedge}
is an isomorphism.
In particular, we get
M\simeq M^{\wedge}
.
This finishes the (iv) for
M
being finitely presented over
R
.

In general, let
M
be any finitely generated module over
R
.
Take
\overline{M}
to be the image of
M
in
M[\frac{1}{p}]
.
Then since
\overline{M}
is finitely generated and
p
-torsionfree, we know
\overline{M}
is finitely presented and hence the kernel
N=\ker(M\rightarrow\overline{M})
is finitely generated by loc. cit.
Notice that by definition
N
is
p^{\infty}
-torsion.
So there exists some
m\in\mathbb{N}
such that
p^{m}N=0
.
Now by the
p
-torsionfreeness of
\overline{M}
, the base change of the exact sequence
0\rightarrow N\rightarrow M\rightarrow\overline{M}\rightarrow 0
along
R\rightarrow R/p^{s}
is exact. Moreover, since the inverse system
\{N\otimes_{R}R/p^{s}\}_{s}
is essentially constant, the inverse limit of the short exact sequence of inverse system is exact, and we get

0\longrightarrow N^{\wedge}=N\longrightarrow M^{\wedge}\longrightarrow\overline{M}^{\wedge}\longrightarrow 0,

which by the isomorphism
\overline{M}\simeq\overline{M}^{\wedge}
we get the result

M\simeq M^{\wedge}.

So we are done.

(5)

Let
N
be a submodule of a finite free
R
-module
F
, and let
M:=F/N
be the quotient.
By (iv), since
M
is finitely generated, we have the canonical isomorphism
M\simeq M^{\wedge}
.
As in the proof of (iv), the
p
-adic completion induces the following short exact sequence

0\longrightarrow N^{\wedge}\longrightarrow F\longrightarrow M^{\wedge}\simeq M\longrightarrow 0.

Hence we get the isomorphism
N\simeq N^{\wedge}
.
In particular, since
N
is complete and its
p
-adic topology is isomorphic to its subspace topology, we get the closedness of
N
in
F
by standard topological argument.

∎

Corollary 5.1.3
.

Let
R
be a topologically finite type algebra over
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}
.

(1)

The ring
R
is
p
-adically complete and separated.

(2)

The ring
R
is topologically finitely presented over
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}
if it is
p
-torsionfree.

(3)

Assume
R
is in
{\mathrm{Alg}_{\mathrm{tfp},e}}
, and
I
is an ideal of
R
.
Then
I
is finitely presented over
R
if
R/I
is
p
-torsionfree.

Proof.

(1)

Note that
R
is the quotient of
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}\langle T_{1},\ldots,T_{m}\rangle
for some
m
, with the latter being in
{\mathrm{Alg}_{\mathrm{tfp},e}}
.
In particular,
R
is a finitely generated
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}\langle T_{1},\ldots,T_{m}\rangle
-module.
So the result follows from Lemma
5.1.2
.(iv).

(2)

By (i), we know
R
is
p
-adically complete and
p
-torsionfree.
So it suffice to check that for a surjection
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}\langle T_{1},\ldots,T_{m}\rangle\rightarrow R
, the kernel is finitely generated.
This then follows from Lemma
5.1.2
.(i), since
R
is a finitely generated
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}\langle T_{i}\rangle
module that is
p
-torsionfree.

(3)

By Lemma
5.1.2
.(i) applied at the
p
-torsionfree
R
-module
R/I
, we know
I
is a finitely generated ideal in
R
.
So thanks to
5.1.2
.(ii), we know
I
is in fact finitely presented.

∎

Lemma 5.1.4
.

Let
R
be in
{\mathrm{Alg}_{\mathrm{tfp},e}}
, and
F
be a flat
R
-module

(1)

The functor
M\mapsto(M\otimes_{R}F)^{\wedge}
is exact on the category of finitely presented
R
-modules.

(2)

Given a finitely presented
R
-module
M
, the following canonical map is an isomorphism

M\otimes_{R}F^{\wedge}\longrightarrow(M\otimes_{R}F)^{\wedge}.

(3)

The
R
-module
F^{\wedge}
is flat over
R
, and is
p
-torsionfree.

Proof.

(1)

Let
0\rightarrow M^{\prime}\rightarrow M\rightarrow M^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow 0
be a short exact sequence of finitely presented
R
-modules.
By assumption, the tensor product with
F
over
R
is exact, so it suffices to show that the
p
-adic completion is flat on
0\rightarrow M^{\prime}\otimes F\rightarrow M\otimes F\rightarrow M^{\prime\prime}\otimes F\rightarrow 0
.
By Lemma
5.1.2
.(iii), there exists an integer
c\geq 0
such that
p^{k}M\cap M^{\prime}\subset p^{k-c}M^{\prime}
.
Applying this inclusion with the tensor product functor
-\otimes_{R}F
, and notice that the flatness of
F
implies
(p^{k}M\cap M^{\prime})\otimes F=(p^{k}M\otimes F)\cap(M^{\prime}\otimes F)
, we see the
p
-adic topology on
M^{\prime}\otimes F
is isomorphic to the subspace topology induced from
M\otimes F
.
In particular, by
[
Mat86
]
Theorem 8.1, we get the exactness

0\longrightarrow(M^{\prime}\otimes F)^{\wedge}\longrightarrow(M\otimes F)^{\wedge}\longrightarrow(M^{\prime\prime}\otimes F)^{\wedge}\longrightarrow 0.

(2)

Assume
M
has the following presentation

R^{\oplus n}\longrightarrow R^{\oplus m}\longrightarrow M\longrightarrow 0.

The tensor product of this with
F
gives

F^{\oplus n}\longrightarrow F^{\oplus m}\longrightarrow M\otimes F\longrightarrow 0.

We then take the
p
-adic completion.
By (i), we get an exact sequence,

(F^{\wedge})^{\oplus n}\longrightarrow(F^{\wedge})^{\oplus m}\longrightarrow(M\otimes F)^{\wedge}\longrightarrow 0.

On the other hand, we replace
F
by
F^{\wedge}
in the second exact sequence above, and get

(F^{\wedge})^{\oplus n}\longrightarrow(F^{\wedge})^{\oplus m}\longrightarrow M\otimes F^{\wedge}\longrightarrow 0.

The canonical map from (2) to (1) are identities on
F^{\wedge,\oplus n}
and
F^{\wedge,\oplus m}
.
Thus we get the isomorphism.

(3)

It suffices to show that for any injective map of finitely presented modules
M^{\prime}\rightarrow M
, the tensor product with
F^{\wedge}
is still injective.
This then follows from (ii) and (i).

∎

Corollary 5.1.5
.

Let
f:A\rightarrow B
be a map of algebras in
{\mathrm{Alg}_{\mathrm{tfp},e}}
.
Then the kernel of any surjective
A
-homomorphism
\rho:A\langle T_{i}\rangle\rightarrow B
is finitely generated over
A
.
In particular,
B
is a topologically finitely presented
A
-algebra.

Proof.

By assumption, we can write
A
as
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}\langle U_{j}\rangle/I
for some finitely presented ideal
I
.
This allows us to write the surjection
\rho
as
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}\langle U_{j},T_{i}\rangle/I\rightarrow B
, and it suffices to show that the ideal
J:=\ker({\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}\langle U_{j},T_{i}\rangle/I\rightarrow B)
is finitely presented.
Notice that the quotient ring
B
by assumption is
p
-torsionfree.
So by Corollary
5.1.3
.(iii), the finite presentedness of the ideal
J
follows if we can show that the ring
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}\langle U_{j},T_{i}\rangle/I
is in
{\mathrm{Alg}_{\mathrm{tfp},e}}
.
The latter by Corollary
5.1.3
.(ii) is equivalent to showing that the ring
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}\langle U_{j},T_{i}\rangle/I
is topologically finite type over
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}
and is
p
-torsionfree.

Finally, to check the latter conditions, we notice that that the ring
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}\langle U_{j},T_{i}\rangle/I
is by construction topologically finite type over
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}
.
On the other hand, since
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}\langle U_{j}\rangle/I=A\langle T_{i}\rangle
is the
p
-adic completion of the flat
A
-module
A[T_{i}]
, by Lemma
5.1.4
.(iii) we know it is
p
-torsionfree.
∎

5.2.
Analytic cotangent complex: affinoid case

We then introduce the analytic cotangent complex, for algebras in
{\mathrm{Alg}_{\mathrm{tfp},e}}
and affinoid rigid spaces over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
in this subsection.

Derived
p
-adic completion

We recall the basics of derived
p
-adic completion.

Let
R
be a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-algebra.
For a complex
C=C^{\bullet}
of
R
-modules, recall that the
derived
p
-adic completion
of
C
is defined as

{\mathrm{R\varprojlim}}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}(C\otimes^{L}_{R}\mathrm{cofib}(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.83507pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.83507pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{R\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.18587pt\raise 6.26584pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.90474pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{p^{m}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 22.29916pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 22.29916pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{R}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces)),

as an object in the derived category
\mathscr{D}(R)
of
R
-modules.
Here the object
\mathrm{cofib}(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.83507pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.83507pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{R\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.18587pt\raise 6.26584pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.90474pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{p^{m}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 22.29916pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 22.29916pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{R}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces)
is the cone of the map
p^{m}:R\rightarrow R
.
An object
C\in\mathscr{D}(R)
is called
derived
p
-complete
if
C
is isomorphic to its derived
p
-adic completion.
The subcategory
\mathscr{D}_{p}(R)
of derived
p
-complete objects is a full subcategory (
[
Sta
, Tag 091U]
) of
\mathscr{D}(R)
, and the derived
p
-adic completion forms a left adjoint functor to the inclusion functor
\mathscr{D}_{p}(R)\rightarrow\mathscr{D}(R)
(
[
Sta
, Tag 091V]
).

There exists a natural isomorphism of complexes of
R
-modules

R\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathrm{cofib}(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 8.36673pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-8.36673pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 7.71753pt\raise 6.26584pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.90474pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{p^{m}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 23.83083pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 23.83083pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces)\simeq\mathrm{cofib}(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.83507pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.83507pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{R\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.18587pt\raise 6.26584pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.90474pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{p^{m}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 22.29916pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 22.29916pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{R}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces).

From this, the derived functor
C\mapsto C\otimes_{R}^{L}\mathrm{cofib}(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.83507pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.83507pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{R\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.18587pt\raise 6.26584pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.90474pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{p^{m}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 22.29916pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 22.29916pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{R}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces)
in
\mathscr{D}(R)
can be rewritten as

\displaystyle C\longmapsto

\displaystyle C\otimes_{R}^{L}R\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{L}\mathrm{cofib}(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 8.36673pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-8.36673pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 7.71753pt\raise 6.26584pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.90474pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{p^{m}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 23.83083pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 23.83083pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle C\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathrm{cofib}(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 8.36673pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-8.36673pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 7.71753pt\raise 6.26584pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.90474pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{p^{m}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 23.83083pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 23.83083pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces).

Here we note that since
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
is
p
-torsionfree, the complex
\mathrm{cofib}(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 8.36673pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-8.36673pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 7.71753pt\raise 6.26584pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.90474pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{p^{m}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 23.83083pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 23.83083pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces)
is isomorphic to the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-module
\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{m}[0]
living at the degree
0
.
In the case when
C
is a
p
-torsionfree
R
-module, by the flatness of
C
over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, its derived
p
-adic completion is exactly its classical
p
-adic completion
\varprojlim_{m}C/p^{m}
.
This in fact holds true in full generality for complexes as follows.

Lemma 5.2.1
.

Let
C
be a cochain complex of
p
-torsionfree
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-modules.
Then the derived
p
-completion of
C
can be represented by a cochain complex
\widetilde{C}\in{\mathrm{Ch}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, which is obtained by the term-wise classical
p
-completion of
C
.

Proof.

We first notice that
\widetilde{C}
is derived
p
-complete, as the derived
p
-completeness can be checked by cohomology (
[
Sta
, Tag 091N]
) and each
\mathrm{H}^{i}(\widetilde{C})
is derived
p
-complete.

When
C
is bounded to the right, the derived tensor product
C\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{L}\mathbb{Z}/p^{m}
is represented by the cochain complex
C/p^{m}\in{\mathrm{Ch}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, obtained via term-wise quotient by
p^{m}
.
In this case, the claim follows from via
[
Sta
, Tag 09AU]
and can be checked by taking the mod
p^{n}
reductions, as
\widetilde{C}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{L}\mathbb{Z}/p^{m}=C/p^{m}
.

In general, consider the naive truncation

\sigma^{>n}C\longrightarrow C\longrightarrow\sigma^{\leq n}C.

By
\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{m}
is quasi-isomorphic to a perfect complex over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, the derived tensor product functor
-\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{L}\mathbb{Z}/p^{m}
commutes with derived limit functor.
In particular, we have

C\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{L}\mathbb{Z}/p^{m}\simeq R\varprojlim_{n}\left((\sigma^{\leq n}C)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{L}\mathbb{Z}/p^{m}\right).

Hence we have

\displaystyle R\varprojlim_{m}C\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{L}\mathbb{Z}/p^{m}

\displaystyle\simeq R\varprojlim_{m}R\varprojlim_{n}\left((\sigma^{\leq n}C)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{L}\mathbb{Z}/p^{m}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq R\varprojlim_{m}R\varprojlim_{n}\left((\sigma^{\leq n}C)/p^{m}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq R\varprojlim_{n}R\varprojlim_{m}\left((\sigma^{\leq n}C)/p^{m}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq R\varprojlim_{n}\sigma^{\leq n}\widetilde{C}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{C}.

∎

Analytic cotangent complex for affine formal schemes.

Now we introduce the definition and the basic properties of analytic cotangent complexes, for a map of algebras over
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}
.
The analogous discussion for topologically finite type algebras over
K
can be found in
[
GR
, Section 7.1]
.

Construction 5.2.2
.

Let
f:A\rightarrow B
ba a map of
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}
-algebras in
{\mathrm{Alg}_{\mathrm{tfp},e}}
.
Namely both
A
and
B
are
p
-adically complete
p
-torsionfree algebras over
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}
that are quotients of
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}\langle T_{1},\ldots,T_{m}\rangle
for some
m\in\mathbb{N}
.
As an
A
-algebra, the ring
B
admits a standard simplicial resolution

P_{\bullet}\rightarrow B,

where each
P_{i}
is a polynomial over
A
(
[
Sta
, Tag 08PM]
).
This allows us to give a simplicial
P_{\bullet}
-modules
\Omega_{P_{\bullet}/A}^{1}
, where each
\Omega_{P_{i}/A}^{1}
is the algebraic differential of
P_{i}
over
A
.
Recall that the
algebraic cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{B/A}
is the image of the cochain complex
\Omega_{P_{\bullet}/A}^{1}\otimes_{P_{\bullet}}B
in the derived category over
A
.
The
analytic cotangent complexes
\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}
for the
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}
-algebras
B\rightarrow A
is then defined as the image of the derived
p
-adic completion of the
\Omega_{P_{\bullet}/A}^{1}\otimes_{P_{\bullet}}B
, in the derived category of
A
-modules.

Remark 5.2.3
.

As the polynomial resolution is functorial with respect to the pair
(A,B)
, by Lemma
5.2.1
the analytic cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}
can be represented functorially by the cochain complex in
{\mathrm{Ch}}(B)
, produced by the term-wise
p
-adic completion of
\Omega_{P_{\bullet}/A}^{1}\otimes_{P_{\bullet}}B
.

There exists a canonical map from the algebraic cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{B/A}
to the analytic cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}
.
This is given by the counit map of the adjoint pair for the derived
p
-completion and the inclusion functor
\mathscr{D}_{p}(A)\rightarrow\mathscr{D}(A)
.

Here are some useful results for the analytic cotangent complex of
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}
-algebras.

Proposition 5.2.4
.

Let
f:A\rightarrow B
be a map of
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}
-algebras in
{\mathrm{Alg}_{\mathrm{tfp},e}}
.
Assume
f
is formally smooth.
Then we have a canonical isomorphism

\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}\longrightarrow\Omega_{B/A}^{1,{\mathrm{an}}}[0],

where the right side is the module of (
p
-adic) continuous differential forms.

Proof.

Since
f
is formally smooth, by Elkik’s algebraization result of formally smooth adic algebras (for this specific situation, see
[
BS
, Page 11, Footnote 6]
, where the noetherian assumption is not needed),
B
is isomorphic to the
p
-adic completion of a smooth
A
-algebra.
In particular,
f
is flat (Lemma
5.1.4
) and the map
f_{n}:=(A/p^{n}\rightarrow B/p^{n})
is smooth.
So by the derived base change formula for the algebraic cotangent complex (
[
Sta
, Tag 08QQ]
), since
B/p^{n}=B\otimes_{A}^{L}A/p^{n}
, we have

\mathbb{L}_{B/A}\otimes^{L}_{A}A/p^{n}=\mathbb{L}_{(B/p^{n})/(A/p^{n})}.

Moreover, the smoothness of
f_{n}
gives a canonical isomorphism

\mathbb{L}_{(B/p^{n})/(A/p^{n})}\simeq\Omega_{(B/p^{n})/(A/p^{n})}^{1}[0]=\Omega_{B/A}^{1}\otimes_{A}A/p^{n}[0].

In this way, by taking the derived
p
-adic completion of
\mathbb{L}_{B/A}
and notice the
p
-torsionfreeness of
B
and
A
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R\varprojlim}}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{L}_{B/A}\otimes^{L}_{A}A/p^{n}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R\varprojlim}}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\Omega_{B/A}^{1}/p^{n}[0]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Omega_{B/A}^{1,{\mathrm{an}}}[0].

∎

In the next result, we show that the analytic cotangent complex for a finite morphism coincides with the associated algebraic cotangent complex.
Recall that for an object
L^{\bullet}
in the derived category of
R
-modules, it is called
pseudo-coherent
if it is isomorphic to a upper-bounded complex of finite free
R
-modules.

Proposition 5.2.5
.

Let
A\rightarrow B
be a map of two topologically finitely presented
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}
-algebras in
{\mathrm{Alg}_{\mathrm{tfp},e}}
, such that
B
is a finitely presented
A
-module.
Then the algebraic cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{B/A}
is pseudo-coherent.
In particular,
\mathbb{L}_{B/A}
is derived
p
-complete and we have a canonical isomorphism

\mathbb{L}_{B/A}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}.

Proof.

We first show that it suffices to assume
A\rightarrow B
is a surjection.
Too see this, we first pick a polynomial algebra
A[x_{1},\ldots,x_{r}]
that maps surjectively onto
B
.
By the finite presentedness assumption of
B
over
A
, each
x_{i}
satisfies a monic polynomial
f_{i}(x_{i})
of
x_{i}
in
A
, and the induced map
B^{\prime}=A[x_{1},\ldots,x_{r}]/(f_{1},\ldots,f_{r})\rightarrow B
is also surjective.
Here we note that the ring
B^{\prime}
, as a finite algebra over
A
that is
p
-torsionfree, is automatically
p
-complete and is also in
{\mathrm{Alg}_{\mathrm{tfp},e}}
.
Moreover, notice that since the sequence
\{f_{1},\ldots,f_{r}\}
is a regular sequence in
A[x_{i}]
,
by the distinguished triangle of algebraic cotangent complexes for
A\rightarrow B^{\prime}\rightarrow B
we get

\mathbb{L}_{B^{\prime}/A}\otimes^{L}_{B^{\prime}}B\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B/A}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B/B^{\prime}}.

Here we note that as
B^{\prime}
is relatively of local complete intersection over
A
, by
[
Sta
,
Tag 08SL
]
we know
\mathbb{L}_{B^{\prime}/A}
is a perfect complex over
B^{\prime}
.
Thus to show the pseudo-coherence of
\mathbb{L}_{B/A}
, it suffices to show this for
\mathbb{L}_{B/B^{\prime}}
, where
B^{\prime}\rightarrow B
is a surjective map of algebras in
{\mathrm{Alg}_{\mathrm{tfp},e}}
.

Recall that by assumption
B
is a finite
A
-module that is
p
-torsionfree.
So Lemma
5.1.2
implies that
B
is a finitely presented
A
-module and there exists an exact sequence of
A
-modules as below

\textstyle{A^{\oplus m}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{A^{\oplus r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

Moreover, as the image of
f
is a submodule of
A^{\oplus r}
, which by Lemma
5.1.2
.(i) is finitely presented, we know
\ker(f)
is also finitely generated (hence finitely presented as it is inside of
A^{\oplus m}
).
This procedure allows to give a finite free
A
-module resolution of
B
.
In particular, this shows that
B
is pseudo-coherent over
A
.

We then take
P
to be a simplicial polynomial resolution of
B
over
A
, and let
J
be the kernel of the map
P\otimes_{A}B\rightarrow B
.
Then by the finite presentedness of
B
over
A
, the simplicial
A
-algebra
P
is also pseudo-coherent over
A
.
So by taking a base change along
A\rightarrow B
, we see
P\otimes_{A}B
is a simplicial
B
-algebra that is pseudo-coherent over
B
.
Moreover, since the map
P\otimes_{A}B\rightarrow B
has a natural section, the kernel
J
is also pseudo-coherent (
[
Sta
, Tag 064X]
).
Notice that the cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{B/A}
fits into the distinguished triangle (cf.
[
Ill71
, Chap. III. 3.3.2]

J\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B/A}\longrightarrow J^{2}[1].

To show the pseudo-coherence of
\mathbb{L}_{B/A}
, by
[
Sta
, Tag 064U]
it suffices to show by decreasing induction that
\mathbb{L}_{B/A}
is
n
-pseudo-coherent for each
n\leq 1
.
When
n=1
, the result is clear as
\mathbb{L}_{B/A}
has cohomological degree
\leq 0
.
Suppose the result is true for
n\leq 0
.
Since
A\rightarrow B
is a surjection, the induced surjective map
P\otimes_{A}B\rightarrow B
is isomorphic on
\pi_{0}
with kernel living in cohomological degree
\leq-1
.
Thus by
[
Ill71
]
Chap III, 3.3, we have

\mathrm{H}^{m}(J^{i})=0,~for~i>-m.

This implies that when
i>-n
,
J^{i}
is
n
-pseudo-coherent.
On the other hand, by
[
Ill71
]
Chap III, 3.3.2, there exists an isomorphism.

J^{j}/J^{j+1}\longrightarrow L\mathrm{Sym}^{j}_{B}(\mathbb{L}_{B/A}),~j\geq 0.

The derived symmetric product preserves the
n
-pseudo-coherence (
[
GR
]
7.1.18), so
J^{j}/J^{j+1}
is
n
-pseudo-coherent for any
j\geq 0
.
Thus the fiber sequence for the quotient
J^{i}\rightarrow J^{i}/J^{i+1}
allows us to deduce the
n
-pseudo-coherent of every
J^{i}
.
In particular, when
i=2
, by taking the cohomological twist we know
J^{2}[1]
is
(n-1)
-pseudo-coherent.
So combining with the quasi-coherence of
J
, we get the
(n-1)
-quasi-coherence of
I/I^{2}=\mathbb{L}_{B/A}
by
[
Sta
, Tag 064V]
.

∎

Corollary 5.2.6
.

Let
A
be a
p
-torsionfree topologically finitely presented
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}
-algebra, and
I
be a finitely generated regular ideal in
A
such that
B:=A/I
is
p
-torsionfree.
Then we have a canonical isomorphism

\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}\longrightarrow I/I^{2}[1].

Proof.

Since
B
is
p
-torsionfree, by the Corollary
5.1.3
we know
B
is in
{\mathrm{Alg}_{\mathrm{tfp},e}}
.
So the result follows from Proposition
5.2.5
and the case for algebraic cotangent complex.
∎

Here is another useful result about the distinguished triangles for triples:

Proposition 5.2.7
.

Let
A\rightarrow B\rightarrow C
be maps in
{\mathrm{Alg}_{\mathrm{tfp},e}}
.
Then we have

(1)

The analytic cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}
is a pseudo-coherent object in the derived category of
B
-modules.

(2)

there exists a natural distinguished triangle of pseudo-coherent objects in the derived category of
C
-modules

\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}\otimes_{B}^{L}C\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{C/A}^{\mathrm{an}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{C/B}^{\mathrm{an}}.

Before the proof, we make the following claim:

Lemma 5.2.8
.

Let
A\rightarrow B
be a map of algebras in
{\mathrm{Alg}_{\mathrm{tfp},e}}
.
Let
K
be a bounded above complex of
A
-modules, and
K^{\prime}
be its derived
p
-completion.
Then the derived
p
-completion of
K\otimes_{A}^{L}B
is isomorphic to the derived
p
-completion of
K^{\prime}\otimes_{A}^{L}B
.

Proof of Lemma.

It suffices to check by applying the derived tensor product functor
-\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{L}\mathbb{Z}/p^{m}
, which is then clear as
K\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{L}\mathbb{Z}/p\simeq K^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{L}\mathbb{Z}/p^{m}
.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

5.2.7
.

(1)

By the Corollary
5.1.5
, we may write
B
as the quotient
P/I
, where
P=A\langle T_{i}\rangle
is a convergent power series ring over
A
, and
I
is a finitely generated ideal by the Corollary
5.1.3
.(iii).
We take the distinguished triangle of algebraic cotangent complexes for
A\rightarrow P\rightarrow B
, and get

\mathbb{L}_{P/A}\otimes_{P}^{L}B\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B/A}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B/P}.

Note that
\mathbb{L}_{P/A}^{\mathrm{an}}
is isomorphic to the finite free
P
-module
\Omega_{P/A}^{1,{\mathrm{an}}}
(Proposition
5.2.4
), so after applying the derived
p
-completion and using the lemma above, we get a distinguished triangle

\Omega_{P/A}^{1,{\mathrm{an}}}\otimes_{P}B[0]\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B/P}^{\mathrm{an}}.

Here the
\mathbb{L}_{B/P}^{\mathrm{an}}
is pseudo-coherent by Proposition
5.2.5
.
Thus we are done.

(2)

For (ii), take the distinguished triangle for algebraic cotangent complexes, we get

\mathbb{L}_{B/A}\otimes_{B}^{L}C\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B/A}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{C/B}.

So the result follows from the lemma above and the pseudo-coherence of each analytic cotangent complex.

∎

Analytic cotangent complex for affinoid rigid spaces.

We then introduce the basics of the analytic cotangent complex for a map of affinoid algebras over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
, using the integral construction given in the last paragraph.
The analogous discussion for topologically finite type algebras over
K
can be found in
[
GR
, Section 7.2]
.

Construction 5.2.9
.

Let
f:A\rightarrow B
be a map of topologically finite type affinoid algebras over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
.
Namely both
A
and
B
are quotients of
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}\langle T_{1},\ldots,T_{m}\rangle
for some
m\in\mathbb{N}
.
Denote by
\mathcal{C}_{B/A}
the category of pairs of rings
(B_{0},A_{0})
, where
A_{0}
and
B_{0}
are rings of definition of
A
and
B
respectively, such that both of them are in
{\mathrm{Alg}_{\mathrm{tfp},e}}
, and
f(A_{0})\subset B_{0}
.
The morphism among pairs is defined by inclusion maps on each entry respectively.

Assume
(B_{0},A_{0})
is an object of
\mathcal{C}_{B/A}
.
By the construction of the last paragraph, we can construct the analytic cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{B_{0}/A_{0}}^{\mathrm{an}}
for
B_{0}/A_{0}
, as the derived
p
-completion of the algebraic cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{B_{0}/A_{0}}
.

Definition 5.2.10
.

The
analytic cotangent complexes for affinoid algebras
B/A
is defined as the colimit

\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}:=\underset{(B_{0},A_{0})\in\mathcal{C}_{B/A}}{{\mathrm{colim}}}(\mathbb{L}_{B_{0}/A_{0}}^{\mathrm{an}}[\frac{1}{p}]),

as an object in the derived category of
B
-modules.

Remark 5.2.11
.

As there exists a canonical actual complex representing
\mathbb{L}_{B_{0}/A_{0}}^{\mathrm{an}}
(by the term-wise
p
-adic completion of
\Omega_{P/A_{0}}^{1}\otimes_{P}B
, where
P
is the standard polynomial resolution of
B_{0}
over
A_{0}
), the analytic cotangent complexes can also be represented by a canonical actual complex, defined by taking the colimit of the actual term-wise complete complexes and then invert by
p
.

Here we note that as there exists a canonical map from the algebraic cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{B_{0}/A_{0}}
to
\mathbb{L}_{B_{0}/A_{0}}^{\mathrm{an}}
, induced by the adjoint pair for the derived completion.
By inverting
p
we also have a canonical map from the algebraic cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{B/A}
to the analytic cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}
, for a map of algebras
A\rightarrow B
over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
.

We then give a simple description of the analytic cotangent complex for a smooth morphism.

Proposition 5.2.12
.

Let
A_{0}\rightarrow B_{0}
be a map of algebras in
{\mathrm{Alg}_{\mathrm{tfp},e}}
, and let
A=A_{0}[\frac{1}{p}]
and
B=B_{0}[\frac{1}{p}]
be their generic fibers respectively, with the induced map
f:A\rightarrow B
.
Assume the corresponding map of affinoid rigid spaces
\mathrm{Spa}(B)\rightarrow\mathrm{Spa}(A)
is smooth.
Then we have a natural isomorphism

\mathbb{L}_{B_{0}/A_{0}}^{\mathrm{an}}[\frac{1}{p}]\longrightarrow(\Omega_{B_{0}/A_{0}}^{1,{\mathrm{an}}}[\frac{1}{p}]).

Proof.

By the Corollary
5.1.5
,
B_{0}
is a topologically finitely presented
A_{0}
-algebra.
So we can write
B_{0}
as the quotient ring of the relative convergent power series ring
P_{0}=A_{0}\langle T_{1},\ldots,T_{m}\rangle
, by some finitely generated ideal
I_{0}\subset P_{0}
.
Denote by
P
and
I
the ring
P_{0}[\frac{1}{p}]
and the ideal
I_{0}[\frac{1}{p}]
respectively.
Then the surjection
P\rightarrow B
induces a closed immersion of
\mathrm{Spa}(B)
into the
m
-dimensional unit disc
\mathrm{Spa}(P)
over
\mathrm{Spa}(A)
.
Since both
\mathrm{Spa}(B)
and
\mathrm{Spa}(P)
are smooth over
\mathrm{Spa}(A)
, by the Jacobian criterion for the smoothness of adic spaces (
[
Hu96
]
, 1.6.9), for each maximal ideal
{\mathfrak{P}}
of
P
that contains
I
, we can always find generators
s_{1},\ldots,s_{l}
of
I_{\mathfrak{P}}
such that their derivatives
ds_{1},\ldots ds_{l}
can be extended to a basis of the continuous differential
\Omega_{P/A,{\mathfrak{P}}}^{1}
at
{\mathfrak{P}}
.
We denote by
\mathfrak{p}
the intersection
{\mathfrak{P}}\cap A
.
Then the above implies that the image of
s_{i}
in
P_{\mathfrak{P}}\otimes_{A}(A_{\mathfrak{p}}/\mathfrak{p}A_{\mathfrak{p}})
forms a regular sequence.
So by the flatness of
P_{\mathfrak{P}}
over
A_{\mathfrak{p}}
and
[
EGA IV
, Chap. 0, Proposition 15.1.16]
,
(s_{i})
forms a regular sequence in
P_{\mathfrak{P}}
.
Since this is true for every maximal ideal
{\mathfrak{P}}
of
P
containing
I
, we see
B
is a local complete intersection of
P
.

Now thanks to the surjectivity of
P_{0}\rightarrow B_{0}
, Proposition
5.2.5
implies that

\mathbb{L}_{B_{0}/P_{0}}^{\mathrm{an}}\simeq\mathbb{L}_{B_{0}/P_{0}}.

Moreover, by the flat base change formula for the algebraic cotangent complex (
[
Sta
,
Tag 08QQ
]
), there exits a canonical isomorphism of algebraic cotangent complexes

\mathbb{L}_{B_{0}/P_{0}}[\frac{1}{p}]\simeq\mathbb{L}_{B/P},

which by the local complete intersection of
P\rightarrow B
, is isomorphic to
I/I^{2}[1]
.
On the other hand, by Proposition
5.2.7
and Proposition
5.2.4
, we have a natural distinguished triangle

(\Omega_{P_{0}/A_{0}}^{1,{\mathrm{an}}}\otimes_{P_{0}}B_{0}[\frac{1}{p}])\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B_{0}/A_{0}}^{\mathrm{an}}[\frac{1}{p}]\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B_{0}/P_{0}}^{\mathrm{an}}[\frac{1}{p}].

Replacing the right side by the ideal
I/I^{2}[1]
in degree
1
, we get an isomorphism

\mathbb{L}_{B_{0}/A_{0}}^{\mathrm{an}}[\frac{1}{p}]\simeq\mathrm{cofib}(I/I^{2}\longrightarrow\Omega_{P_{0}/A_{0}}^{1,{\mathrm{an}}}\otimes_{P_{0}}B_{0}[\frac{1}{p}]).

Notice that by
[
Hu96
, Prop. 1.6.9.(ii)]
and the smoothness of
\mathrm{Spa}(B)\rightarrow\mathrm{Spa}(A)
, the above map in the right hand side is injective.
Hence the cofiber complex above lives in cohomological degree zero and is by definition equal to

(\Omega_{B_{0}/A_{0}}^{1,{\mathrm{an}}}[\frac{1}{p}])[0].

∎

As a quite useful upshot, to compute the analytic cotangent complex for affinoid rings, it suffices to use one single pair of rings of definition.

Proposition 5.2.13
.

Let
A_{0}\rightarrow B_{0}
be a map of algebras in
{\mathrm{Alg}_{\mathrm{tfp},e}}
, and let
A=A_{0}[\frac{1}{p}]
and
B=B_{0}[\frac{1}{p}]
be their generic fibers respectively, with the induced map
f:A\rightarrow B
.
Then the map below is an isomorphism

\mathbb{L}_{B_{0}/A_{0}}^{\mathrm{an}}[\frac{1}{p}]\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}.

Proof.

It suffices to show that for any commutative diagram of topologically finitely presented rings of definition

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 8.99307pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-8.99307pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A_{0}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.99307pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.99307pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{B_{0}^{\prime}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-8.99307pt\raise-31.67557pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-7.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.99307pt\raise-31.67557pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.99307pt\raise-31.67557pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{B_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 42.27954pt\raise-7.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

the induced morphism
\mathbb{L}_{B_{0}/A_{0}}^{\mathrm{an}}[\frac{1}{p}]\rightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B_{0}^{\prime}/A_{0}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{an}}[\frac{1}{p}]
is an isomorphism.
Moreover, using the distinguished triangles for
A_{0}\rightarrow A_{0}^{\prime}\rightarrow B_{0}^{\prime}
and
A_{0}\rightarrow B_{0}\rightarrow B_{0}^{\prime}
respectively, we can reduce to show that

\mathbb{L}_{A_{0}^{\prime}/A_{0}}^{\mathrm{an}}[\frac{1}{p}]=0.

Then we notice that since
A_{0}\rightarrow A_{0}^{\prime}
is an isomorphism after inverting by
p
, this satisfies the assumption of Proposition
5.2.12
.
So it suffices to prove that
\Omega_{A_{0}^{\prime}/A_{0}}^{1,{\mathrm{an}}}[\frac{1}{p}]=0
.
By the Corollary
5.1.5
,
A_{0}^{\prime}
is a topologically finitely presented algebra over
A_{0}
.
We pick a set of generators
x_{i}
of
A_{0}^{\prime}
over
A_{0}
.
Then by
A_{0}^{\prime}[\frac{1}{p}]=A_{0}[\frac{1}{p}]
, there exists a positive integer
N
such that
p^{N}x_{i}\in A_{0}
.
Note that the continuous differential forms
\Omega_{A_{0}^{\prime}/A_{0}}^{1,{\mathrm{an}}}
is generated by the
dx_{i}
.
So we get

p^{N}dx_{i}=d(p^{N}x_{i})=0.

This implies that
\Omega_{A_{0}^{\prime}/A_{0}}^{1,{\mathrm{an}}}
is
p^{\infty}
-torsion.
In particular, we have

\Omega_{A_{0}^{\prime}/A_{0}}^{1,{\mathrm{an}}}[\frac{1}{p}]=0.

So we are done.

∎

Here are some applications of the above result.

Corollary 5.2.14
.

Let
f:A\rightarrow B
be a map of topologically finite type algebras over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
, such that
\mathrm{Spa}(B)\rightarrow\mathrm{Spa}(A)
is smooth.
Then the projection onto the zero-th homotopy group induces natural isomorphism

\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}\longrightarrow\Omega_{B/A}^{1}[0],

where the right side is the modules of continuous differential forms.

Proof.

This follows from Proposition
5.2.13
and Proposition
5.2.12
.
∎

Similar to the integral case, when
B
is a quotient ring of
A
, the analytic cotangent complex coincides with the algebraic cotangent complex.

Corollary 5.2.15
.

Let
f:A\rightarrow B
ba a map of topologically finite type algebras over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
such that
B
is a finite
A
-module.
Then the natural map from the algebraic cotangent complex to the analytic cotangent complex below is an isomorphism

\mathbb{L}_{B/A}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}.

Proof.

Pick a ring of definition
A_{0}
of
A
that is topologically finitely presented over
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}
.
We first notice that under the assumption of
A\rightarrow B
, we can find a ring of definition
B_{0}
of
B
that contains
f(A_{0})
and is finite over
A_{0}
.
To find such
B_{0}
, we pick s set of
A
-module generators
x_{i}
of
B
over
A
.
Then each
x_{i}
satisfies a monic polynomial
f_{i}(X)=\sum_{j=0}^{r_{i}}a_{ij}X^{j}
with coefficients in
A
.
Since
A=A_{0}[\frac{1}{p}]
, we can pick a common integer
N\in\mathbb{N}
, such that coefficients
p^{N}a_{ij}
are inside of
A_{0}
for each
i
and
j
.
From this, we see the element
p^{N}x_{i}
satisfies a monic polynomial with coefficient in
A_{0}
.
In other words, the subring
B_{0}=f(A_{0})[p^{N}x_{i}]
of
B
is finite over
A_{0}
.

Now the corollary follows easily from
Proposition

5.2.13
and Proposition
5.2.5
, since
\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{L}_{B_{0}/A_{0}}^{\mathrm{an}}[\frac{1}{p}]
, while the latter is computed by inverting
p
at the algebraic cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{B_{0}/A_{0}}
.
Notice that thanks to the flat base change of the algebraic cotangent complex,
\mathbb{L}_{B_{0}/A_{0}}[\frac{1}{p}]
is exactly the algebraic cotangent complex of
B
over
A
.
So we get the result.

∎

As expected, we have the following simple description of the analytic cotangent complex for regular immersion.

Corollary 5.2.16
.

Let
f:A\rightarrow B
ba a surjective map of topologically finite type algebras over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
, such that the kernel
I
is a regular ideal in
A
.
Then we have a natural isomorphism

\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}\longrightarrow I/I^{2}[1].

Proof.

This follows from
Corollary

5.2.15
and
[
Sta
,
Tag 08SJ
]
.
∎

Another quick upshot is the pseudo-coherence of the analytic cotangent complex.

Corollary 5.2.17
.

Let
A\rightarrow B
be a map of topologically finite type algebras over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
.
Then the analytic cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}
is a pseudo-coherent complex of
B
-modules.

Proof.

This follows from Proposition
5.2.13
and the integral version of the result Proposition
5.2.7
.
∎

We also obtain the distinguished triangle for triples as follow.

Corollary 5.2.18
.

Let
A\rightarrow B\rightarrow C
be maps of topologically finite type algebras over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
.
Then there exists a distinguished triangle of analytic cotangent complexes of affinoid algebras

\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}\otimes_{B}^{L}C\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{C/A}^{\mathrm{an}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{C/B}^{\mathrm{an}}.

Proof.

Let
A_{0}\rightarrow B_{0}\rightarrow C_{0}
be an arbitrary choice of rings of definition of
A\rightarrow B\rightarrow C
that are topologically finitely presented over
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}
.
By Proposition
5.2.7
, we have a distinguished triangle

\mathbb{L}_{B_{0}/A_{0}}^{\mathrm{an}}\otimes_{B_{0}}^{L}C_{0}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{C_{0}/A_{0}}^{\mathrm{an}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{C_{0}/B_{0}}^{\mathrm{an}}.

Note that for the first term above we have the isomorphism

\displaystyle(\mathbb{L}_{B_{0}/A_{0}}^{\mathrm{an}}\otimes_{B_{0}}C_{0})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{L}\mathbb{Q}_{p}

\displaystyle\simeq(\mathbb{L}_{B_{0}/A_{0}}^{\mathrm{an}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{L}\mathbb{Q}_{p})\otimes_{B_{0}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{L}(C_{0}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}\otimes_{B}^{L}C.

So the corollary follows from Proposition
5.2.13
by the base change along
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.
∎

A quick consequence is the following change of base equality.

Corollary 5.2.19
.

Let
A\rightarrow A^{\prime}\rightarrow B
be maps of topologically finite algebras over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
, with
A\rightarrow A^{\prime}
being étale.
Then the natural map below is an isomorphism

\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{an}}_{B/A}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{an}}_{B/A^{\prime}}.

Proof.

By the distinguished triangle of the triple in the Corollary
5.2.18
, it suffices to show that
\mathbb{L}_{A^{\prime}/A}^{\mathrm{an}}
vanishes.
So this follows from the assumption and the Corollary
5.2.14
.
∎

As last, we have the étale base change formula as below.

Corollary 5.2.20
.

Let
A\rightarrow B\rightarrow B^{\prime}
be maps of topologically finite algebras over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
, with
B^{\prime}/B
being étale.
Then we have the following natural isomorphism

\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}\otimes_{B}B^{\prime}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B^{\prime}/A}^{\mathrm{an}}.

In particular, when there exists an étale morphism
A\rightarrow A^{\prime}
such that
B^{\prime}=A^{\prime}\otimes_{A}B
, we get the base change formula

\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}\otimes_{A}A^{\prime}\simeq\mathbb{L}_{B^{\prime}/A^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{an}}.

Proof.

The first isomorphism follows from the distinguished triangle (Corollary
5.2.18
) for
A\rightarrow B\rightarrow B^{\prime}
, and the étaleness of
B^{\prime}/B
(Corollary
5.2.14
).
The second isomorphism follows from the Corollary
5.2.19
and the isomorphism

\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}\otimes_{B}B^{\prime}=\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}\otimes_{B}B\otimes_{A}A^{\prime}.

∎

5.3.
Derived de Rham complex: affinoid case

In this subsection, we construct the derived de Rham cohomology for topologically finite type algebras over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
.

As a preparatory step, we first construct the rational analytic derived de Rham complex for a map
A_{0}\rightarrow B_{0}
of
{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}_{e}
-algebras, which is a complete filtered complex over
A_{0}[\frac{1}{p}]
, namely an object in
\widehat{\mathscr{DF}}(A_{0}[\frac{1}{p}])
.
We also refer the reader to
[
Bha12
, Const. 4.1]
for basics on the algebraic derived de Rham complex of algebras.

Construction 5.3.1
.

Let
A_{0}
be an
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}
-algebra in
{\mathrm{Alg}_{\mathrm{tfp},e}}
.
We want to build a functor
F:{\mathrm{Alg}_{\mathrm{tfp},e}}_{/A_{0}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathscr{DF}}({\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}),
sending
A_{0}\rightarrow B_{0}
to a filtered complete
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra over
A_{0}[\frac{1}{p}]
.

Step 1

Let
P
be the standard polynomial resolution of
B_{0}
over
A_{0}
.
The de Rham complex
\Omega_{P/A_{0}}^{\bullet}
of
P
over
A_{0}
is then a simplicial complex of
P
-modules.
Moreover, the (direct sum) totalization
{\mathrm{Tot}}(\Omega_{P/A_{0}}^{\bullet})
is a cochain complex of
A_{0}
-modules that comes with a canonical decreasing filtration, defined by
{\mathrm{Fil}}^{i}={\mathrm{Tot}}(\Omega_{P/A_{0}}^{\geq i})
.

Step 2

Now we take the derived
p
-adic completion of the filtered cochain complex
({\mathrm{Tot}}(\Omega_{P/A_{0}}^{\bullet}),{\mathrm{Fil}}^{i})
, to get an object
(E,{\mathrm{Fil}}^{i}E)
in the filtered derived category, such that
E
and
{\mathrm{Fil}}^{i}E
are all derived
p
-adic complete.
Then we invert
p
in
(E,{\mathrm{Fil}}^{i}E)
, to get an object
(E[\frac{1}{p}],{\mathrm{Fil}}^{i}E[\frac{1}{p}])
in the filtered derived category of
A_{0}[\frac{1}{p}]
-modules.

Step 3

At last, we denote
F(B_{0}/A_{0})
to be the filtered completion of
(E[\frac{1}{p}],{\mathrm{Fil}}^{i}E[\frac{1}{p}])
.

7
7

7

Roughly speaking, the filtered completion here means we are taking the derived inverse limit
R\varprojlim_{j}E[\frac{1}{p}]/{\mathrm{Fil}}^{j}E[\frac{1}{p}]
, together with the induced filtration with the
i
-th filtration being
R\varprojlim_{j\geq i}{\mathrm{Fil}}^{i}E[\frac{1}{p}]/{\mathrm{Fil}}^{j}E[\frac{1}{p}]
. This can be understood more canonically as the image of
(E[\frac{1}{p}],{\mathrm{Fil}}^{i}E[\frac{1}{p}])
along the symmetric monoidal
filtered completion functor

{\mathscr{DF}}(A_{0}[\frac{1}{p}])\to\widehat{\mathscr{DF}}(A_{0}[\frac{1}{p}])
.

Thus we get a functor from maps in
{\mathrm{Alg}_{\mathrm{tfp},e}}
to the filtered complete derived category of
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
-modules (even
A_{0}[\frac{1}{p}]
-modules), sending
A_{0}\rightarrow B_{0}
to
F(B_{0}/A_{0})
.

Remark 5.3.2
.

From the construction above, it is clear that the
i
-th graded piece of
F(B_{0}/A_{0})
is isomorphic to

\bigl(L\wedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{B_{0}/A_{0}}^{\mathrm{an}}[\frac{1}{p}]\bigr)[-i].

Remark 5.3.3
.

Recall given a complex of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-modules
C
, it admits a natural map onto its derived
p
-adic completion
\widetilde{C}
.
Applying this to the construction above (Step 2), we see there exists a natural filtered map from the algebraic derived de Rham complex
\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}_{B_{0}[\frac{1}{p}]/A_{0}[\frac{1}{p}]}
to
F(B_{0},A_{0})
.

Remark 5.3.4
.

From the construction above, the natural map from
P
to
B_{0}
induces a filtered map from
F(B_{0},A_{0})
to the continuous de Rham complex
\Omega_{B_{0}/A_{0}}^{\bullet,{\mathrm{an}}}[\frac{1}{p}]
, which is compatible with the differentials.
Here the filtration on the latter is the usual Hodge filtration.

Remark 5.3.5
.

As the de Rham complex is equipped with a structure of commutative differential graded algebra, and the above constructions are all lax-symmetric monoidal, the filtered complex
F(B_{0}/A_{0})
is also naturally a filtered
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra in
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
-modules.

Now we consider the constructions for affinoid rigid spaces.
Let
f:A\rightarrow B
be a map of topologically finite type algebras over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
.
Recall that the category
\mathcal{C}_{B/A}
is defined as pairs of rings
(B_{0},A_{0})
, where
A_{0}
and
B_{0}
are rings of definition of
A
and
B
separately, such that both of them are topologically finitely presented over
{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}
, and
f(A_{0})\subset B_{0}
.
The morphism among pairs is defined by the natural inclusion map of pairs.
Here we note that by the Corollary
5.1.5
,
B_{0}
is a topologically finitely presented algebra over
A_{0}
automatically.

Definition 5.3.6
.

Let
f:A\rightarrow B
be a map of topologically finite type algebras over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
, and let
\mathcal{C}_{B/A}
be the category of pairs of their rings of definitions as above.
The
analytic derived de Rham complex of
B
over
A
, denoted by
{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{B/A}
, is an object in
\widehat{\mathscr{DF}}({\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}})
, defined as

{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{B/A}:=filtered~completion~of~\underset{(B_{0},A_{0})\in\mathcal{C}_{B/A}}{{\mathrm{colim}}}F(B_{0}/A_{0}).

The filtration of
{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{B/A}
is called the
algebraic Hodge filtration
.
If we forget the filtered structure, we get the
underlying complex of
{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{B/A}
.
It is denoted as
\underline{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}}^{\mathrm{an}}_{B/A}
, and is defined as the 0-th filtration of
{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{B/A}
,
which is the image under the natural projection functor

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{DF}}({\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}})\subset\textrm{Fun}(\mathbb{N}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathscr{D}({\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathscr{D}({\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}});

\displaystyle C_{\bullet}

\displaystyle\longmapsto C_{0}.

Corollary 5.3.7
.

Let
A\rightarrow B
be a map of topologically finite type algebras over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
.
Then the
i
-th graded piece of
{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{B/A}
is naturally isomorphic to
L\wedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}[-i]
.
In particular, for any pair of rings of definition
(B_{0},A_{0})\in\mathcal{C}_{B/A}
, the natural map below is a filtered isomorphism

F(B_{0},A_{0})\longrightarrow{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{B/A}.

Proof.

By the construction above, the algebraic Hodge filtration
{\mathrm{Fil}}^{i}{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{B/A}
has the graded factor

{\mathrm{gr}}^{i}{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{B/A}=\underset{(B_{0},A_{0})\in\mathcal{C}_{B/A}}{{\mathrm{colim}}}L\wedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{B_{0}/A_{0}}^{\mathrm{an}}[\frac{1}{p}][-i].

By
Proposition

5.2.13
and the assumption on
(B_{0},A_{0})
, each
L\wedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{B_{0}/A_{0}}^{\mathrm{an}}[\frac{1}{p}]
is isomorphic to the
i
-th derived wedge product of the analytic cotangent complex for the affinoid algebras
B/A
.
In particular, the transition maps in the colimit above are all isomorphisms, and we can replace them by one single term.
Thus we get

{\mathrm{gr}}^{i}{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{B/A}\simeq L\wedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}[-i].

As a consequence, thanks to the filtered completeness, the filtered isomorphism can be checked on the graded pieces, and hence the natural map
F(B_{0},A_{0})\longrightarrow{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{B/A}
is a filtered isomorphism.

∎

Remark 5.3.8
.

By taking the colimit for the natural filtered map

\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}_{B_{0}[\frac{1}{p}]/A_{0}[\frac{1}{p}]}\longrightarrow F(B_{0},A_{0}),

we get a canonical filtered map from the algebraic derived de Rham complex
\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}_{B/A}
to the analytic derived de Rham complex
{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{B/A}
.
This is compatible with the canonical map of each graded factor

L\wedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{B/A}\longrightarrow L\wedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}.

Remark 5.3.9
.

As the colimit is a lax-symmetric monoidal functor, the analytic derived de Rham complex
{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{B/A}
is naturally a filtered
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra in
\mathscr{D}({\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}})
.

Here we provide a simple description of the analytic derived de Rham complex for two special cases: the smooth case and the complete intersections.

Proposition 5.3.10
.

Let
A\rightarrow B
be a smooth map of topologically finite type algebras over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
.
Then
the natural morphism below from the analytic derived de Rham complex to the continuous de Rham complex is a filtered isomorphism:

{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{B/A}\longrightarrow\Omega_{B/A}^{\bullet}.

Proof.

By the Remark
5.3.4
, there exists a natural filtered map from
{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{B/A}
to the continuous de Rham complex
\Omega_{B/A}^{\bullet}
, which is compatible with the differential maps.
By the assumption and Corollary
5.2.14
, the analytic cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}
is isomorphic to the module of continuous differential forms
\Omega_{B/A}^{1}[0]
, which is a free
B
-module whose rank is equal to the relative dimension
\dim_{A}(B)
.
On the other hand, the de Rham complex of affinoid algebras
B
over
A
is bounded above by the relative dimension and is thus complete under the Hodge filtration.
The derived wedge product
L\wedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}
is isomorphic to
\wedge^{i}\Omega_{B/A}^{1}[0]=\Omega_{B/A}^{i}[0]
, which vanishes when
i>\dim_{A}(B)
.
So by the Construction
5.3.1
above, the natural map from the analytic derived de Rham complex to the de Rham complex of
B/A
induces an isomorphism from the
i
-th graded factor
{\mathrm{gr}}^{i}{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{B/A}=L\wedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}[-i]
to the
i
-th continuous differential
\Omega_{B/A}^{i}[-i]
.
In this way, we get a filtered isomorphism from
{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{B/A}
to the de Rham complex
\Omega_{B/A}^{\bullet}
.
∎

Proposition 5.3.11
.

Let
A\rightarrow B
be a surjective map of topologically finite type algebras over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
. Then the canonical map below is a filtered isomorphism

\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}_{B/A}\longrightarrow{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{B/A}.

As an upshot, the underlying complex
\underline{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}}^{\mathrm{an}}_{B/A}
is isomorphic to the formal completion
\widehat{A}
for the surjection
A\rightarrow B
.

Proof.

As both
{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{B/A}
and
\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}_{B/A}
are filtered complete, it suffices to show that the induced map on each graded factor is an isomorphism.
For each
i\in\mathbb{N}
, the induced map
{\mathrm{gr}}^{i}\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}_{B/A}\rightarrow{\mathrm{gr}}^{i}{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{B/A}
is exactly the natural map induced from the derived
p
-completion integrally (Construction
5.2.9
).
So the first claim follows from the assumption and the Corollary
5.2.15
.

For the second claim, it follows from the isomorphism between the underlying complex
\underline{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}}_{B/A}
of the algebraic derived de Rham complex and the formal completion
\widehat{A}
, which is the main result in
[
Bha12
]
(see
[
Bha12
, 4.14, 4.16]
).
∎

Corollary 5.3.12
.

Let
A\rightarrow B
be a surjective map of topologically finite type algebras over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
, such that the kernel ideal
I
is regular in
A
.
Then for each
i\in\mathbb{N}
, we have a natural isomorphism

A/I^{i}[0]\longrightarrow{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{B/A}/{\mathrm{Fil}}^{i}.

In particular, by taking the derived limit, we get a filtered isomorphism of algebras

\widehat{A}\simeq{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{B/A},

where the left side is the (classical)
I
-adic completion of
A
.

Proof.

This follows from Proposition
5.3.11
, and the case for algebraic cotangent complex explained in the Example 4.5 in
[
Bha12
]
, originally proved in
[
Ill72
, Theorem 2.2.6]
.
∎

5.4.
Global constructions

In this subsection, we construct the global analytic cotangent complexes and the global analytic derived de Rham complexes.
Our strategy is to show that the affinoid constructions satisfy the hyperdescent for the rigid topology, thus can be extended to a complex of sheaves over the given rigid space.

Unfolding.

We first recall the unfolding of a hypersheaf in
\infty
-category.
We refer the reader to
[
Lu09
, §6.5.3]
for the more detailed discussion on hypercoverings, hypersheaves, and the hypercompletion.

Let
X
be a site that admits fiber products, and let
\mathcal{B}
be a basis of
X
,
namely
\mathcal{B}
is a subcategory of
X
such that for each object
U
in
X
,
there exists an object
U^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{B}
covering
U
.
So any hypercovering of an object in
X
can be refined to a hypercovering with each term in
\mathcal{B}
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a presentable
\infty
-category.
Consider a hypersheaf
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(\mathcal{B},\mathscr{C})
over
\mathcal{B}
.
We can then
unfold
the sheaf
\mathcal{F}
to a hypersheaf
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
on
X
,
such that its evaluation at any
V\in X
is given by

\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(V)=\underset{U^{\prime}_{\bullet}\rightarrow V}{{\mathrm{colim}}}\varprojlim_{[n]\in\Delta}\mathcal{F}(U^{\prime}_{n}),

where the colimit is indexed over all hypercoverings
U^{\prime}_{\bullet}\rightarrow V
with
U^{\prime}_{n}\in\mathcal{B}
for all
n
.
It follows from
[
Lu09
, Thm. 6.5.3.12]
(see also the discussion at the beginning of
[
Lu09
, §6.5.3]
) that one hypercovering suffices to compute the value of
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(V)
