in the above formula; in other words for a hypercovering
U^{\prime}_{\bullet}\rightarrow V
with each
U^{\prime}_{n}
in the basis
\mathcal{B}
,
we have a natural weak-equivalence

R\lim_{[n]\in\Delta}\mathcal{F}(U^{\prime}_{n})\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(V).

In particular for any
U\in\mathcal{B}
, the natural map
\mathcal{F}(U)\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(U)
is a weak-equivalence.

The above construction is functorial with respect to
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(\mathcal{B},\mathscr{C})
,
and we get a natural unfolding functor

\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(\mathcal{B},\mathscr{C})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X,\mathscr{C}),

which is in fact an equivalence, with the inverse given by the restriction functor

\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X,\mathscr{C})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(\mathcal{B},\mathscr{C})
.
Here we mention that the equivalence follows quickly from the limit formula in the last paragraph, which says that the restriction of
\mathcal{F^{\prime}}
onto the basis
\mathcal{B}
naturally coincide with the input sheaf
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(\mathcal{B},\mathscr{C})
.

Recall in the special case when
\mathscr{C}=\mathscr{D}(R)
is the derived
\infty
-category of
R
-modules, we have a natural equivalence

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}(X,R)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X,\mathscr{D}(R));

\displaystyle C

\displaystyle\longmapsto(V\mapsto R\Gamma(V,C)).

As an upshot, to define a complex of sheaves of
R
-modules over
X
,
it suffices to specify a contravariant functor from the basis
\mathcal{B}
to
\mathscr{D}(R)
,
such that it satisfies the hyperdescent condition within
\mathcal{B}
.

Hyperdescent of
\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}
and
{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{B/A}
.

We first consider the analytic cotangent complex.

Proposition 5.4.1
.

Let
A\rightarrow B
be a map of topologically finite type algebras over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
, and let
B\rightarrow B_{\bullet}
be a map from
B
to a cosimplicial algebras over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
, such that the associated map of rigid spaces
\mathrm{Spa}(B_{\bullet})\rightarrow\mathrm{Spa}(B)
is a rigid open hypercovering.
Then the induced map below is an isomorphism

\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}\longrightarrow R\lim_{[n]\in\Delta}\mathbb{L}_{B_{n}/A}^{\mathrm{an}}.

Proof.

We first notice that by the étaleness of the map
B\rightarrow B_{n}
and the Corollary
5.2.20
,
\mathbb{L}_{B_{n}/A}^{\mathrm{an}}
is naturally isomorphic to the base change
\mathbb{L}_{B/A}\otimes_{B}^{L}B_{n}
.
So it suffices to show the map below is an isomorphism

\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}\longrightarrow R\lim_{[n]\in\Delta}\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}\otimes_{B}^{L}B_{n}.

Note that since each
\mathrm{Spa}(B_{n})\rightarrow\mathrm{Spa}(B)
is an open covering of the rigid space
\mathrm{Spa}(B)
, the induced map
B\rightarrow B_{n}
is flat on structure sheaves (
[
Hu96
, Proposition 1.7.6]
).
In this way, by the surjectivity of
\mathrm{Spa}(B_{n})\rightarrow\mathrm{Spa}(B)
,
we see the map of affine schemes
\Spec(B_{\bullet})\rightarrow\Spec(B)
is a faithfully flat hypercover.

Finally, we recall from the faithfully flat descent of quasi-coherent sheaves over the affine scheme
\Spec(B)
that for a given
B
-module
M
, the canonical map
M\to R\lim_{[n]\in\Delta}M\otimes_{B}^{L}B_{n}
is an isomorphism.
This by induction implies that for any bounded complex of
B
-modules
C
, we have
C\simeq R\lim_{[n]\in\Delta}C\otimes_{B}^{L}B_{n}
.
As a consequence, since
\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}
is bounded above, by taking cohomological truncations of
\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}

\displaystyle=R\varprojlim_{i\leq 0}\tau^{\geq i}\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}

\displaystyle\simeq R\varprojlim_{i\leq 0}R\lim_{[n]\in\Delta}\bigl(\tau^{\geq i}\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}\otimes_{B}^{L}B_{n}\bigr)

\displaystyle\simeq R\lim_{[n]\in\Delta}R\varprojlim_{i\leq 0}\bigl(\tau^{\geq i}\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}\otimes_{B}^{L}B_{n}\bigr)

\displaystyle\simeq R\lim_{[n]\in\Delta}R\varprojlim_{i\leq 0}\tau^{\geq i}\bigl(\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}\otimes_{B}^{L}B_{n}\bigr)

\displaystyle=R\lim_{[n]\in\Delta}\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}\otimes_{B}^{L}B_{n}.

Here the second isomorphism uses the fact that limit functors commute to each other, and the third isomorphism uses the flatness of
B_{n}
over
B
.
∎

Using the unfolding technique in Paragraph
5.4
, we can extend the affinoid construction of the analytic cotangent complex to the global case.

Corollary 5.4.2
.

Let
X\rightarrow Y=\mathrm{Spa}(A)
be a map of rigid spaces over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
.
Then there exists a complex of sheaves of
A
-modules
\mathbb{L}_{X/Y}^{\mathrm{an}}
over
X
, such that for any affinoid open subset
U=\mathrm{Spa}(B)
of
X
,
we have a natural isomorphism

R\Gamma(U,\mathbb{L}_{X/Y}^{\mathrm{an}})=\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}.

The complex
\mathbb{L}_{X/Y}^{\mathrm{an}}
is called the
analytic cotangent complex of
X
over
Y
.

Similarly, we could unfold the construction of the analytic derived de Rham complex to an arbitrary rigid space.

Proposition 5.4.3
.

Let
A\rightarrow B
be a map of topologically finite type algebras over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
, and let
B\rightarrow B_{\bullet}
be a map from
B
to a cosimplicial algebras over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
, such that the associated map of rigid spaces
\mathrm{Spa}(B_{\bullet})\rightarrow\mathrm{Spa}(B)
is a rigid open hypercovering.
Then the induced filtered map below is an isomorphism

{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{B/A}\longrightarrow R\lim_{[n]\in\Delta}{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{B_{n}/A}.

Proof.

As a limit functor preserves the filtered completeness,
R\lim_{[n]\in\Delta}{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{B_{n}/A}
is an object in
\widehat{\mathscr{DF}}(A)
, and checking the isomorphism above can be reduced to their graded pieces.
Moreover, notice that the graded piece functor commutes with small limits and colimits (cf.
[
BMS2
, Lemma 5.2]
).
Thus we get

\displaystyle{\mathrm{gr}}^{i}{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{B/A}=L\wedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}[-i]

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\mathrm{gr}}^{i}(R\lim_{[n]\in\Delta}{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{B_{n}/A})

\displaystyle\simeq R\lim_{[n]\in\Delta}{\mathrm{gr}}^{i}{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{B_{n}/A}

\displaystyle=R\lim_{[n]\in\Delta}L\wedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{B_{n}/A}^{\mathrm{an}}[-i].

Notice that the wedge product functor commutes with the tensor product, and for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
we have

\displaystyle L\wedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{B_{n}/A}^{\mathrm{an}}

\displaystyle\simeq L\wedge^{i}(\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}}\otimes_{B}^{L}B_{n})

\displaystyle\simeq(L\wedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{B/A}^{\mathrm{an}})\otimes_{B}^{L}B_{n},

In this way, the natural map of graded pieces above is an isomorphism, by the similar fpqc hyperdescent for
B\rightarrow B_{\bullet}
as in the proof of Proposition
5.4.1
.
So we are done.

∎

Corollary 5.4.4
.

Let
X\rightarrow Y=\mathrm{Spa}(A)
be a map of rigid spaces over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
.
Then there exists a filtered complex of sheaves of
A
-modules
{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{X/Y}
over
X
, such that for any affinoid open subset
U=\mathrm{Spa}(B)
of
X
,
we have a natural isomorphism

R\Gamma(U,{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{X/Y})={\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{B/A}.

The complex
{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{X/Y}
is called the
analytic derived de Rham complex of
X
over
Y
.

5.5.
Infinitesimal cohomology and derived de Rham complex

In this subsection, we give a comparison theorem between the infinitesimal cohomology and (the underlying complex of) the analytic derived de Rham complex of a rigid space
X
over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
.

Affinoid comparison.

We first consider the affinoid case.
Our tool is the Čech–Alexander complex for the infinitesimal cohomology, and the structure of the analytic derived de Rham complex for closed immersions.

Theorem 5.5.1
.

Let
X=\mathrm{Spa}(A)
be an affinoid rigid space over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
.
Then there exists a natural isomorphism as below

\underline{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}}_{A/{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}}^{\mathrm{an}}\longrightarrow R\Gamma(X/\Sigma_{e\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma_{e}}).

Here
\underline{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}}_{A/{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}}^{\mathrm{an}}
is the underlying complex of the analytic derived de Rham complex.

Before the proof, we want to mention that in the proof below, we will see the isomorphism in the statement is induced from a chosen closed immersion
X\rightarrow Y
, where
Y
is a smooth rigid space.
Later on, we will use this observation to globalize a general comparison.

Proof.

Let
P={\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}\langle T_{1},\ldots,T_{m}\rangle\rightarrow A
be a surjection of topologically finite type algebras over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
.
By Proposition
4.1.3
for the crystal
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma_{e}}
, the infinitesimal cohomology of
X/\Sigma_{e\inf}
can be computed by the cosimplicial cochain complex

R\Gamma(X/\Sigma_{e\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma_{e}})\simeq(\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma_{e}}(D(0))\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma_{e}}(D(1))\longrightarrow\cdots),

where
D(\bullet)
is the cosimplicial object of sheaves over the infinitesimal site, produced by the envelope of
A
in
P^{\widehat{\otimes}_{{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}}\bullet+1}
(see the discussion before Theorem
4.1.1
).
Here we recall that by the definition of envelope (cf.
Definition

2.2.1
), the sheaf
D(m)
is the direct limit of all infinitesimal neighborhoods of
\mathrm{Spa}(A)
in
\mathrm{Spa}(P^{\widehat{\otimes}m+1})
.
In particular, we have the following isomorphism

\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma_{e}}(D(m))=\varprojlim_{i}P^{\widehat{\otimes}m+1}/I(m)^{i},

where
I(m)
is the kernel of the surjection
P^{\widehat{\otimes}_{{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}}m+1}\rightarrow A
.

Now by Proposition
5.3.11
, there exists a natural filtered morphism inducing an isomorphism of their underlying complexes

\underline{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}}^{\mathrm{an}}_{A/P^{\widehat{\otimes}m+1}}\longrightarrow\varprojlim_{i}P^{\widehat{\otimes}m+1}/I(m)^{i}.

By taking the cosimplicial version of the above isomorphism, we get isomorphisms of cosimplicial complexes

R\Gamma(X/\Sigma_{e\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma_{e}})\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma_{e}}(D(\bullet))\longleftarrow\underline{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}}^{\mathrm{an}}_{A/P^{\widehat{\otimes}\bullet+1}}.

So in order to prove the theorem, it suffices to show that the natural map
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}\rightarrow P^{\widehat{\otimes}_{{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}}\bullet+1}\rightarrow A
induces an isomorphism on analytic derived de Rham complexes.
Moreover, by the filtered completeness, it reduces to show the isomorphism

\mathbb{L}_{A/{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}}^{\mathrm{an}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{A/P^{\widehat{\otimes}\bullet+1}}^{\mathrm{an}}.

Note that as both
A
and
P^{\widehat{\otimes}\bullet+1}
are term-wise topologically finite type over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
, by the distinguished triangle of cotangent complexes for triples (Proposition
5.2.18
), it suffices to show the vanishing of the following

\mathbb{L}_{P^{\widehat{\otimes}\bullet+1}/{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}}^{\mathrm{an}}.

At last, we notice that by Proposition
5.2.13
, the complex
\mathbb{L}_{P^{\widehat{\otimes}\bullet+1}/{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}}^{\mathrm{an}}
can be computed by inverting
p
at the term-wise derived
p
-completion of the algebraic cotangent complex of the Čech nerve
{\mathrm{\check{C}ech}}({\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}\rightarrow{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}[T_{1},\ldots,T_{r}])
.
So the vanishing we want follows from the vanishing of the algebraic cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{{\mathrm{\check{C}ech}}({\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}\rightarrow{\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}}[T_{i}])/({\mathrm{A_{inf,e}}})}
, which is proved in the first part of the Corollary 2.7 in
[
Bha12
]
.
∎

In the special case when
A
is a complete intersection, the above can be improved into a filtered isomorphism.
Here we recall that the filtration structure on the infinitesimal cohomology, which is called
infinitesimal filtration
, is defined via
R\Gamma(X/\Sigma_{e\inf},\mathcal{J}_{X/\Sigma_{e}}^{\bullet})
, where
\mathcal{J}_{X/\Sigma_{e}}
is the kernel of the surjection
\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma_{e}}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}
over the infinitesimal site (cf. the discussion above
Remark

2.1.3
).

Corollary 5.5.2
.

Let
X=\mathrm{Spa}(A)
be an affinoid rigid space that admits a regular closed immersion into a smooth affinoid rigid space over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
.
Then there exists a natural filtered isomorphism as below

{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{A/{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}}\longrightarrow R\Gamma(X/\Sigma_{e\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma_{e}}).

Proof.

The proof is identical to the proof of Theorem
5.5.1
, with the use of the Corollary
5.3.12
.
∎

Comparison in general.

We are now ready to prove the comparison between the infinitesimal cohomology and (the underlying complex of) the analytic derived de Rham complex, for a general rigid space over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
.

We first introduce the category of smooth immersions.

Definition 5.5.3
.

Let
X
be a rigid space over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
.
We define the
site
{\mathrm{SE}}_{X}
of smooth immersions of
X
where:

•

objects of
{\mathrm{SE}}_{X}
consist of tuples
(U,Z,i:U\rightarrow Z)
with
U
being an affinoid open subset of
X
,
Z
a smooth affinoid rigid space over
\Sigma_{e}
, and
i:U\rightarrow Z
is a closed immersion;

•

morphisms from
(U_{1},Z_{1},i_{1}:U_{1}\rightarrow Z_{1})
and
(U_{2},Z_{2},i_{2}:U_{2}\rightarrow Z_{2})
consist of commutative diagrams as below

\textstyle{U_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{1}}
\textstyle{Z_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{2}}
\textstyle{Z_{2},}

where
U_{1}\rightarrow U_{2}
is an open immersion over
X
.

•

A collection of maps
\{(U_{j},Z_{j},i_{j})\rightarrow(U,Z,i)\}
is a covering if
\{U_{j}\rightarrow U\}
and
\{Z_{j}\rightarrow Z\}
are coverings of rigid spaces respectively.

There exists a natural projection functor from
{\mathrm{SE}}_{X}
to the category of affinoid open subsets
X_{\mathrm{aff}}
of
X
, by sending an object
(U,Z,i:U\rightarrow Z)
to the open subset
U
in
X
.
This functor is continuous under their topology.
Here the associated
push-forward functor

\pi_{*}
is the constant functor; namely for an ordinary sheaf
\mathcal{F}
in the topos
\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{aff}})
, the push-forward
\pi_{*}\mathcal{F}
satisfies

(\pi_{*}\mathcal{F})(U,Z,i)=\mathcal{F}(U).

The
pullback functor

\pi^{-1}
sends a sheaf
\mathcal{G}
in
\mathrm{Sh}({\mathrm{SE}}_{X})
to the sheaf associated with the presheaf

(\pi^{-1}\mathcal{G})(U)=\underset{(U,Z,i)}{{\mathrm{colim}}}\mathcal{G}(U,Z,i),~U\in X_{\mathrm{rig}}.

The colimit above is a filtered colimit, as given any two closed immersions
i_{1}:U\rightarrow Z_{1}
and
i_{2}:U\rightarrow Z_{2}
, we can find a common refinement of them by
i:U\rightarrow Z_{1}\times_{\Sigma_{e}}Z_{2}
, with natural projection maps
Z_{1}\times_{\Sigma_{e}}Z_{2}\rightarrow Z_{j}
for
j=1,2
.
In particular, the colimit (thus the inverse image functor
\pi^{-1}
) is exact.
So, by translating this into the language of sites (
[
Sta
, Tag 00X1]
), we get a natural morphism of sites

\pi:X_{\mathrm{aff}}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{SE}}_{X}.

Before we prove the main theorem, we first notice that to check two objects in
\mathscr{D}(X_{\mathrm{aff}})=\mathscr{D}(X_{\mathrm{aff}},\mathbb{Z})
are isomorphic, it suffices to do so by pulling back to the category of smooth immersions.
Precisely, we have the following general lemma.

Lemma 5.5.4
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{G}
be two objects in the derived category
\mathscr{D}(X_{\mathrm{aff}})
of sheaves of abelian groups over the site
X_{\mathrm{aff}}
.
Assume
f:R\pi_{*}\mathcal{F}\rightarrow R\pi_{*}\mathcal{G}
is an isomorphism in
\mathscr{D}({\mathrm{SE}}_{X})
.
Then the following natural arrows are all isomorphisms

\textstyle{\pi^{-1}R\pi_{*}\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{~~~~~~\widetilde{f}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}}
,

where both arrows are counit maps associated with
f:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}
and the identity
\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
respectively.
In particular, there exists a natural isomorphism
\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}
in the derived category
\mathscr{D}(X_{\mathrm{aff}})
.

Proof.

For each
U\in X_{\mathrm{aff}}
, we have

\displaystyle R\Gamma(U,\pi^{-1}R\pi_{*}\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle=\underset{(U,Z,i)}{{\mathrm{colim}}}~R\Gamma((U,Z,i),R\pi_{*}\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle=\underset{(U,Z,i)}{{\mathrm{colim}}}~R\Gamma(U,\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\simeq R\Gamma(U,\mathcal{F}),

where the last map is an isomorphism as the colimit above is filtered (thus the geometric realization of the index set is contractible).
In particular, this implies that the counit maps
\pi^{-1}R\pi_{*}\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
and
\pi^{-1}R\pi_{*}\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{G}
are isomorphisms.
Thus the claim follows from the following diagram of natural isomorphisms

\textstyle{\pi^{-1}R\pi_{*}\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{-1}f}
\textstyle{\pi^{-1}R\pi_{*}\mathcal{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{f}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}.}

∎

Now we are able to prove the comparison theorem.

Theorem 5.5.5
.

Let
X
be a rigid space over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
.
Then there exists a natural filtered morphism from the analytic derived de Rham complex to the Hodge-filtered infinitesimal cohomology sheaf as below

{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{X/\Sigma_{e}}\longrightarrow Ru_{X/\Sigma_{e}*}\mathcal{J}^{\ast}_{X/\Sigma_{e}}.

Moroever, the induced map between their underlying complexes is an isomorphism

\underline{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}}_{X/\Sigma_{e}}^{\mathrm{an}}\longrightarrow Ru_{X/\Sigma_{e}*}\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma_{e}}.

Proof.

We first notice that the isomorphism can be constructed using smooth embeddings.
To see this, we recall the equivalence of
\infty
-categories
\mathscr{D}(X,R)\simeq\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X,R)
(cf. Paragraph
5
), where the right side is the full sub-
\infty
-category of contravariant functors from
U\in X_{\mathrm{rig}}
to the derived category
\mathscr{D}(R)
.
As
X_{\mathrm{aff}}
is a basis of the rigid site
X_{\mathrm{rig}}
, we may use the equivalence
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X,R)=\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{\mathrm{aff}},R)
and regard objects in
\mathscr{D}(X,R)
as contravariant functors on affinoid open subsets of
X
.
So a map or an isomorphism of sheaves of complexes can be constructed via their evaluations at
X_{\mathrm{aff}}
.
Moreover, by Lemma
5.5.4
, by applying the constant functor
R\pi_{*}
to the given two objects, it suffices to show that there is an isomorphism between their images over the site of smooth immersions
{\mathrm{SE}}_{X}
.

Now for each smooth immersion
i:U=\mathrm{Spa}(B)\rightarrow Z=\mathrm{Spa}(P)
for affinoid open subset
U\subset X
, as in the proof of
Theorem

5.5.1
, we have the following maps of cosimplicial objects in
\widehat{\mathscr{DF}}({\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}})

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 25.54234pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-25.54234pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{B/{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 49.54234pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 49.54234pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{B/P^{\widehat{\otimes}\bullet+1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 129.31554pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 129.31554pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}_{B/P^{\widehat{\otimes}\bullet+1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 200.1859pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 200.1859pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 200.1859pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{D(\bullet)}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 255.56471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{R\Gamma(U/\Sigma_{e\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma_{e}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 231.56473pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

where
D(n)
is the envelope of the surjection

P^{\widehat{\otimes}(n+1)=}P^{\widehat{\otimes}_{{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}}(n+1)}\rightarrow B.

Here we recall that the first map above is induced from the map of rings
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}\to P^{\widehat{\otimes}\bullet+1}
, the second map is the inverse of the algebraicity isomorphism in Theorem
5.5.1
, the third map is the isomorphism in
[
Bha12
, Cor. 4.14]
, and the last map is induced from the limit presentation of the cohomology complex.
In particular, the above arrows are all functorial with respect to the smooth embedding
i:U\to P
, and the induced maps of their underlying complexes above are all isomorphisms, by the proof of the affinoid comparison in Theorem
5.5.1
.

At last, note that the above maps are functorial with respect to smooth immersions
i:U\rightarrow Z
, so we can improve the above map into the level of sheaves over
{\mathrm{SE}}_{X}

\textstyle{R\pi_{*}{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{X/\Sigma_{e}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}_{{\mathrm{SE}}_{X}}^{\mathrm{an}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{{\mathrm{SE}}_{X}}}
\textstyle{R\pi_{*}Ru_{X/\Sigma_{e}*},\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma_{e}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Here
where
\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}_{{\mathrm{SE}}_{X}}^{\mathrm{an}}
is the (cosimplicial) sheaf sending
i:U\rightarrow Z
to the filtered complex
{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}}_{B/P^{\widehat{\otimes}\bullet+1}}
, and
\mathcal{O}_{{\mathrm{SE}}_{X}}
is the (cosimplicial) sheaf sending
i:U\rightarrow Z
to the structure sheaves
\mathcal{O}_{D(\bullet)}
of envelopes
D(\bullet)
.
In this way, the isomorphism of the underlying complexes over the site
{\mathrm{SE}}_{X}
of smooth immersions

R\pi_{*}\underline{\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}}_{X/{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}}^{\mathrm{an}}\longrightarrow R\pi_{*}Ru_{X/\Sigma_{e}*},\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma_{e}}

follows from the above computation at the sections
i:U\rightarrow Z
, and thus we get the result by Lemma
5.5.4
.
So we are done.

∎

Remark 5.5.6
.

The above comparison map, though functorial with respect to the rigid space
X
, is constructed in an indirect way.
It is natural to ask if we can directly produce a natural morphism from the analytic derived de Rham complex to the infinitesimal cohomology sheaf.
Here we want to mention that for algebraic schemes over
\mathbb{C}
, this could be achieved by the universal property of the derived de Rham complex in the
\infty
-category of filtered
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebras.

6.
Éh descent

In this section, we prove the éh hyperdescent of the infinitesimal cohomology of crystals in vector bundles over
X/K
and
X/{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}_{\inf}
for a rigid space
X
.
Our goal is to show the comparison between the éh de Rham cohomology and the infinitesimal cohomology of a crystal.

In order to extend a crystal over
X
to any rigid space that admits a map to
X
(which in particular is not necessarily an open immersion), we will work with coherent crystals over the big site.
Here we note that this is only for the technical convenience, as crystals and their cohomology over
X/\Sigma_{e\inf}
or
X/\Sigma_{e{\mathrm{INF}}}
are equivalent via pullback and restrictions (Proposition
3.1.7
, Proposition
4.1.2
).

6.1.
Descent for blowup coverings

We first deal with the descent for the blowup covering over the base
\Sigma_{1}=\mathrm{Spa}(K)
, for an arbitrary complete non-archimedean
p
-adic field
K
, not necessarily algebraically closed.
The essential idea follows from Hartshorne’s proof for algebraic de Rham cohomology
[
Har75
, Chap II, Section 4]
, where he provides a long exact sequence of the algebraic de Rham cohomology for a blowup square.

We first give a Mayer-Vietories sequence for the infinitesimal cohomology:

Proposition 6.1.1

(Mayer-Vietories sequence)
.

Let
X
be a union of two closed analytic subspaces
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
over
K
, and let
\mathcal{F}
be a coherent crystal over
X/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}}
.
Then the map of rigid spaces
X_{1}\cap X_{2}\rightarrow X_{1}\cup X_{2}\rightarrow X
induces a natural distinguished triangle as below

Ru_{X/K*}\mathcal{F}\longrightarrow Ru_{X_{1}/K*}\mathcal{F}\moplus Ru_{X_{2}/K*}\mathcal{F}\longrightarrow Ru_{X_{1}\cap X_{2}/K*}\mathcal{F}.

Proof.

As the functor
u_{X/K*}
is the sheaf-version of the global section functor
\Gamma(X/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}},-)
(see Subsection
2.3
), it suffices to show that the maps in the statement above produce a natural distinguished triangle after applying
R\Gamma(U,-)
, for every
U\subset X
open affinoid.
So we may assume there exists a smooth affinoid rigid space
Z=\mathrm{Spa}(P)
over
\Sigma_{e}
, together with a closed immersion of
X=\mathrm{Spa}(P/I)
into
Z
, where
I
is the defining ideal.
Let
X_{i}
be the closed analytic subspace defined by the ideal
I_{i}
in
P
.
Then by assumption,
X
is defined by the ideal
I_{1}\cap I_{2}
, and the intersection
X_{3}:=X_{1}\cap X_{2}
is defined by
I_{3}:=I_{1}+I_{2}
.
We denote by
D
and
D_{i}
the envelope of
X
and
X_{i}
in
Z
respectively.
Here we regard
D
and
D_{i}
to be the ringed spaces, where the underlying topological spaces are
X
and
X_{i}
, and their (global sections of) structure sheaves are
\mathcal{O}_{D}=\varprojlim_{n}P/I^{n}
and
\mathcal{O}_{D_{i}}=\varprojlim P/I_{i}^{n}
respectively (cf. Remark
2.2.2
).

Now by Theorem
4.2.2
, the infinitesimal cohomology of the coherent crystal can be functorially identified as the derived global sections of the following sequence

\mathcal{F}_{D}\otimes\Omega_{D}^{\bullet}\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{D_{1}}\otimes\Omega_{D_{1}}^{\bullet}\moplus\mathcal{F}_{D_{2}}\otimes\Omega_{D_{2}}^{\bullet}\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{D_{3}}\otimes\Omega_{D_{3}}^{\bullet},

where
\mathcal{F}_{D}\otimes\Omega_{D}^{\bullet}
(resp.
\mathcal{F}_{D_{i}}\otimes\Omega_{D_{i}}^{\bullet}
) is the restriction of the de Rham complex of the coherent crystal
\mathcal{F}
on the envelope
D_{X}(Z)
(resp.
D_{X_{i}}(Z)
).
Moreover, by the crystal condition, the finite projective
\mathcal{O}_{D_{i}}
-module
\mathcal{F}_{D_{i}}
is equal to the tensor product
\mathcal{F}_{D}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{D}}\mathcal{O}_{D_{i}}
.
Combining with
Lemma

3.3.3
, we know that each term
\mathcal{F}_{D_{i}}\otimes\Omega_{D_{i}}^{n}
of the de Rham complex, regarded as a module over
\mathcal{O}_{D_{i}}
, is equal to the pullback of
\mathcal{F}_{D}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Z}}\Omega^{1}_{Z/K}
along
D_{i}\rightarrow D
.
As a consequence, by taking the naive truncations termwisely, to show the sequence above is distinguished, it suffices to show that the following natural sequence of rings is a short exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{D}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{D_{1}}\moplus\mathcal{O}_{D_{2}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{D_{3}}\longrightarrow 0.

And since each structure sheaf of envelopes are given by the formal completions of the ring
P
, we reduce the question to show that the following sequence of inverse systems is exact

0\longrightarrow\{P/(I_{1}\cap I_{2})^{n}\}_{n}\longrightarrow\{P/I_{1}^{n}\}_{n}\moplus\{P/I_{2}^{n}\}_{n}\longrightarrow\{P/(I_{1}+I_{2})^{n}\}_{n}\longrightarrow 0.

Notice that for fixed
n
, we always have the following short exact sequence

0\longrightarrow P/(I_{1}^{n}\cap I_{2}^{n})\longrightarrow P/I_{1}^{n}\moplus P/I_{2}^{n}\longrightarrow P/(I_{1}^{n}+I_{2}^{n})\longrightarrow 0.

The proof thus follows since the ring
P
is noetherian, and the inverse systems below are canonically isomorphic

\{P/(I_{1}^{n}+I_{2}^{n})\}_{n}\longrightarrow\{P/(I_{1}+I_{2})^{n}\}_{n},~\{P/(I_{1}\cap I_{2})^{n}\}_{n}\longrightarrow\{P/(I_{1}^{n}\cap I_{2}^{n})\}_{n}.

∎

Here is our main theorem in this subsection.

Theorem 6.1.2
.

Let
X
be a rigid space over
K
, and let
Y
be a smooth analytic closed subset of
X
over
K
, with the blowup map
f:X^{\prime}:=\mathrm{Bl}_{X}(Y)\rightarrow X
and the preimage
Y^{\prime}:=Y\times_{X}X^{\prime}
in
X^{\prime}
.
Then for any coherent crystal
\mathcal{F}
over
X/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}}
, the blowup square for
X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
induces the following distinguished triangle

Ru_{X/K*}\mathcal{F}\longrightarrow Rf_{*}Ru_{X^{\prime}/K*}\mathcal{F}\moplus Ru_{Y/K*}\mathcal{F}\longrightarrow Rf_{*}Ru_{Y^{\prime}/K*}\mathcal{F}.

In particular, by taking the derived global sections at
X
, we get a distinguished triangle of the infinitesimal cohomology

R\Gamma(X/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}},\mathcal{F})\longrightarrow R\Gamma(X^{\prime}/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}},\mathcal{F})\moplus R\Gamma(Y/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}},\mathcal{F})\longrightarrow R\Gamma(Y^{\prime}/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}},\mathcal{F}).

Before we prove the result, first we recall the formal function theorem for a proper map of rigid spaces.

Theorem 6.1.3
.

[
Kie67
, Thm. 3.7]

Let
f:X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
be a proper map of rigid space over
K
, and let
\mathcal{I}
be a sheaf of ideal over
X
, with
Y
the analytic closed subset of
X
defined by
\mathcal{I}
.
Assume
\mathcal{G}
is a coherent sheaf over
X^{\prime}
.
Then the following natural map is an isomorphism:

(R^{j}f_{*}\mathcal{G})^{\wedge}\longrightarrow R^{j}f_{*}(\widehat{\mathcal{G}}).

Here
(-)^{\wedge}
is the classical completion of a sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules (or
\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}
-modules) with respect to the ideal
\mathcal{I}
(resp.
f^{-1}\mathcal{I}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}
).

Remark 6.1.4
.

Here we note that we may get a more derived version of the above theorem using the derived completion, as in
[
Sta
, Tag 0A0H]
.
For our uses, we do not jump into this generality.

The rest of this subsection is devoted to prove Theorem
6.1.2
.

Special case:
X
is smooth.

First we deal with the special case, assuming
X
itself is smooth over
K
.

When
X
is smooth, as the blowup center
Y
is assumed to be smooth, the blowup
X^{\prime}
itself is also smooth over
K
.

8
8

8

To see this, one can use the algebraization argument in the proof of
[
Guo19
, Prop. 5.1.1]
to reduce to a blowup of a smooth closed subscheme in a smooth scheme, where the claim is clear.

By Theorem
4.2.2
, the derived direct image of the coherent crystal over
X/K_{\inf}
and
X^{\prime}/K_{\inf}
can be computed by their de Rham complexes
\mathcal{F}_{X}\otimes\Omega_{X/K}^{\bullet}
and
\mathcal{F}_{X^{\prime}}\otimes\Omega_{X^{\prime}/K}^{\bullet}=f^{*}\mathcal{F}_{X}\otimes\Omega_{X^{\prime}/K}^{\bullet}
respectively.
On the other hand, the derived direct image
Ru_{Y/K*}\mathcal{F}
and
Ru_{Y^{\prime}/K*}\mathcal{F}
are naturally isomorphic to the de Rham complex over the envelopes
D_{Y}(X)
and
D_{Y^{\prime}}(X^{\prime})
; namely the complexes

\mathcal{F}_{D}\otimes\Omega_{D_{Y}(X)}^{\bullet},~~~\mathcal{F}_{D^{\prime}}\otimes\Omega_{D_{Y^{\prime}}(X^{\prime})}^{\bullet},

which are compatible with the Hodge filtrations of
\Omega_{X/K}^{\bullet}
and
\Omega_{X^{\prime}/K}^{\bullet}
.
So the sequence
(\ast)
in Theorem
6.1.2
is naturally isomorphic to the following sequence of de Rham complexes

\mathcal{F}_{X}\otimes\Omega_{X/K}^{\bullet}\longrightarrow Rf_{*}(f^{*}\mathcal{F}_{X}\otimes\Omega_{X^{\prime}/K}^{\bullet})\moplus\mathcal{F}_{D}\otimes\Omega_{D_{Y}(X)}^{\bullet}\longrightarrow Rf_{*}(\mathcal{F}_{D^{\prime}}\otimes\Omega_{D_{Y^{\prime}}(X^{\prime})}^{\bullet}).

In fact, we want to show the following more general statement:

Proposition 6.1.5
.

Let
f:X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
be a proper morphism of smooth connected rigid spaces over
K
, and let
Y
be a closed analytic subset of
X
, with
Y^{\prime}=f^{-1}(Y)
.
Let
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{G}
be coherent sheaves over
X^{\prime}
and
X
separately, such that
f:X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
induces an injective map of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules
\mathcal{G}\rightarrow f_{*}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
.
Assume
f
induces an isomorphism between open subsets
X^{\prime}\backslash Y^{\prime}
and
X\backslash Y
, and also induces an isomorphism of the restriction of
\mathcal{G}\rightarrow f_{*}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
on
X\backslash Y
.
Then the following natural sequence is a distinguished triangle

\mathcal{G}\otimes\Omega_{X/K}^{i}\longrightarrow Rf_{*}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\otimes\Omega_{X^{\prime}/K}^{i})\moplus\mathcal{G}\otimes\Omega_{D_{Y}(X)/K}^{i}\longrightarrow Rf_{*}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\otimes\Omega_{D_{Y^{\prime}}(X^{\prime})/K}^{i}),~i\in\mathbb{N}.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{M}
be the coherent sheaf
\mathcal{G}\otimes\Omega_{X/K}^{i}
over
X
, and let
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
be the coherent sheaf
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\otimes\Omega_{X^{\prime}/K}^{i}
over
X^{\prime}
.
By the assumption of smoothness, both
\Omega_{X/K}^{i}
and
\Omega_{X^{\prime}/K}^{i}
are locally free, and the natural map
\mathcal{M}\rightarrow f_{*}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
is injective.
Furthermore, the restriction of the map
\mathcal{M}\rightarrow f_{*}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
on the open subset
X\backslash Y
is an isomorphism.

Recall the
i
-th differential sheaf
\Omega_{D_{Y}(X)/K}^{i}
of
D_{Y}(X)
, as a sheaf over
X
, is defined as the inverse limit
\varprojlim_{m}\Omega_{X_{m}/K}^{i}
, where
X_{m}
is the
m
-th infinitesimal neighborhood of
Y
in
X
.
As is shown in Lemma
3.3.3
, the sheaf
\Omega_{D_{Y}(X)/K}^{i}
is naturally isomorphic to the formal completion of the coherent sheaf
\Omega_{X/K}^{i}
along
Y\rightarrow X
, which is also equal to the tensor product
\Omega_{X/K}^{i}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{D_{Y}(X)}
.
Moreover, since
\mathcal{G}
is coherent over
X
, the tensor product
\mathcal{G}\otimes\Omega_{D_{Y}(X)}^{i}
is isomorphic to the formal completion
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}
of
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{G}\otimes\Omega_{X/K}^{i}
along
Y\rightarrow X
.
The same also holds for
X^{\prime},Y^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
.

We denote by
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
cones of the map
\mathcal{M}\rightarrow Rf_{*}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}\rightarrow Rf_{*}\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}
respectively.
Consider the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Rf_{*}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widehat{\mathcal{M}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Rf_{*}\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C_{2}.}

Here both rows are distinguished.

Now we make the following claim.

Claim 6.1.6
.

The natural map
C_{1}\rightarrow C_{2}
of cones above is an isomorphism.

Proof of Claim.

First we notice that since the map
f:X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
is an isomorphism on the open subsets
X^{\prime}\backslash Y^{\prime}\rightarrow X\backslash Y
and both
X^{\prime}
and
X
are smooth, the sheaves of differentials
\Omega_{X/K}^{i}
and
\Omega_{X^{\prime}/K}^{i}
are vector bundles, and the induced map
\Omega_{X/K}^{i}\rightarrow f_{*}\Omega_{X^{\prime}/K}^{i}
is injective.

9
9

9

For an open immersion
i:U\to X
, thanks to the smoothness, the restriction map
\Omega^{i}_{X/K}(X)\to\Omega^{i}_{X/K}(U)
is injective. When
U
is away from
Y
, the open immersion
U\to X
factors through
X^{\prime}
, and we get the injectivity of the composition
\Omega^{i}_{X/K}(X)\to\Omega^{i}_{X^{\prime}/K}(X^{\prime})\to\Omega^{i}_{X/K}(U)
as well, hence
\Omega^{i}_{X/K}(X)\to\Omega^{i}_{X^{\prime}/K}(X^{\prime})
is injective. The same applies when
X
and
X^{\prime}
are replaced by open subspaces, which justifies the injectivity in the level of sheaves.

On the other hand, the map
\mathcal{G}\rightarrow f_{*}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
is assumed to be an injective map of coherent sheaves in the Proposition.
Combining the above two conditions, we see the map
\mathcal{M}\rightarrow f_{*}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
is injective, and the cone lives in cohomological degree
[0,+\infty)
.

By the Formal Function Theorem
6.1.3
, the cohomology sheaf
R^{j}f_{*}\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}
is naturally isomorphic to the formal completion
(R^{j}f_{*}\mathcal{M}^{\prime})^{\wedge}
of
R^{j}f_{*}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
along
Y\rightarrow X
.
Moreover, by the exactness of the formal completion on coherent sheaves, the natural map
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}\rightarrow(f_{*}\mathcal{M}^{\prime})^{\wedge}
is injective, and we have a short exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{M}}\longrightarrow(f_{*}\mathcal{M}^{\prime})^{\wedge}\longrightarrow\mathcal{H}^{0}(C_{2})\longrightarrow 0.

This implies that
C_{2}
lives also in cohomological degree no smaller than zero.
Furthermore, by the exactness of the above sequence, the cohomology sheaf
\mathcal{H}^{0}(C_{2})
is isomorphic to the formal completion of
\mathcal{H}^{0}(C_{1})
at
Y
.
But since
\mathcal{H}^{0}(C_{1})
is coherent and is already supported at
Y
, we have

\mathcal{H}^{0}(C_{1})=\mathcal{H}^{0}(C_{1})^{\wedge}\simeq\mathcal{H}^{0}(C_{2}).

This finishes the degree zero part.

For the higher cohomology, we consider the following diagram of cohomologies

\textstyle{R^{j}f_{*}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{j}(C_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(R^{j}f_{*}\mathcal{M}^{\prime})^{\wedge}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{j}(C_{2}).}

As
\mathcal{M}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}
are living in cohomological degree zero, the horizontal maps above are isomorphisms, and it suffices to show for each
i>0
, the left vertical map above is an isomorphism.
But notice that since
f
induces an isomorphism between
\mathcal{M}
and
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
over
X\backslash Y
, the higher cohomology sheaf
R^{j}f_{*}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
is coherent and is supported over
Y
.
In particular, the formal completion of
R^{j}f_{*}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
along
Y\rightarrow X
is equal to itself; namely the natural map below is an isomorphism

R^{j}f_{*}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\longrightarrow(R^{j}f_{*}\mathcal{M}^{\prime})^{\wedge}.

This leads to the isomorphism

\mathcal{H}^{j}(C_{1})\simeq\mathcal{H}^{j}(C_{2}),~\forall j\geq 1,

and we finish the isomorphism between
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
.

∎

We change the notation back to Proposition
6.1.5
.
Then we get two rows of distinguished triangles

\textstyle{\mathcal{G}\otimes\Omega^{i}_{X/K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Rf_{*}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\otimes\Omega^{i}_{X^{\prime}/K})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}\otimes\Omega^{i}_{D_{Y}(X)/K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Rf_{*}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\otimes\Omega^{i}_{D_{Y^{\prime}}(X^{\prime})/K})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C_{2},}

the third vertical map is an isomorphism.

Finally, we consider the following two maps,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi:}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}\otimes\Omega_{X/K}^{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Rf_{*}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\otimes\Omega^{i}_{X^{\prime}/K})\moplus(\mathcal{G}\otimes\Omega_{D_{Y}(X)/K}^{i});}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi:}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Rf_{*}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\otimes\Omega^{i}_{X^{\prime}/K})\moplus(\mathcal{G}\otimes\Omega_{D_{Y}(X)/K}^{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Rf_{*}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\otimes\Omega_{D_{Y^{\prime}}(X^{\prime})/K}^{i}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(+,+)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-,+)}

where
+
and
-
are indicating the signs of the map.
As the composition of the above two maps is equal to zero, the map
\psi
factors though a morphism
\Cone(\phi)\rightarrow Rf_{*}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\otimes\Omega_{D_{Y^{\prime}}(X^{\prime})/K}^{i})
(
[
Sta
, Tag 08RI]
).
In this way, by chasing diagrams and the Claim above, the map
\Cone(\phi)\rightarrow Rf_{*}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\otimes\Omega_{D_{Y^{\prime}}(X^{\prime})/K}^{i})
is an isomorphism, and we get the distinguished triangle we want.
∎

General case.

We then deal with the general case of Theorem
6.1.2
.

Proof of Theorem
6.1.2
.

As the theorem is a local statement, by passing to an open covering if necessary it suffices to assume
X
is affinoid and admits a closed immersion into a smooth affinoid rigid space
Z
.
Moreover, as any coherent crystal over
X/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}}
is a crystal in vector bundles (Corollary
3.2.4
), by further taking open subsets we may assume
\mathcal{F}_{X}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\oplus m}
is trivial over
X
.

As
Y
is smooth over
K
, the blowup
Z^{\prime}=\mathrm{Bl}_{Z}(Y)
of the smooth rigid space
Z
at the center
Y
is also smooth.
Moreover, as
X\rightarrow Z
is a closed immersion while
X^{\prime}
is the blowup of
X
at
Y
, the natural map
X^{\prime}\rightarrow Z^{\prime}
is also a closed immersion, which is equal to the preimage of
X
along the blowup map
f:Z^{\prime}\rightarrow Z
.
So we get the following commutative diagrams of rigid spaces over
K
with both of the square being cartesian

\textstyle{Y^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Z^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Z.}

Since the restriction
\mathcal{F}_{D_{X}(Z)}
is a vector bundle over
\mathcal{O}_{D_{X}(Z)}
whose pullback along
X\rightarrow D_{X}(Z)
is trivial,
by Nakayama’s lemma we know
\mathcal{F}_{D_{X}(Z)}
is already a trivial bundle, and we may let
\mathcal{G}
be a trivial vector bundle over
Z
such that the tensor product
\mathcal{G}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Z}}\mathcal{O}_{D_{X}(Z)}
is equal to
\mathcal{F}_{D_{X}(Z)}
.
Let
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
be the pullback
f^{*}\mathcal{G}
, as a trivial bundle over
Z^{\prime}
.
Then by the crystal condition of
\mathcal{F}
we have

\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Z^{\prime}}}\mathcal{O}_{D_{X^{\prime}}(Z^{\prime})}=\mathcal{F}_{D_{X^{\prime}}(Z^{\prime})}.

Here we note that by our choices and the diagram above, the map
\mathcal{G}\rightarrow f_{*}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
is injective and is an isomorphism when restricted to open subsets
Z\backslash Y
and
Z\backslash X
.
Similar to the proof of Proposition
6.1.5
, we let
\mathcal{M}
and
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
be the tensor products
\mathcal{G}\otimes\Omega_{Z/K}^{i}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\otimes\Omega_{Z^{\prime}/K}^{i}
over
Z
and
Z^{\prime}
respectively.

Now by the proof of Proposition
6.1.5
, we have the following natural commutative diagrams, with each row being distinguished

\textstyle{\mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Rf_{*}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widehat{\mathcal{M}_{/X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Rf_{*}\widehat{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{/X^{\prime}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widehat{\mathcal{M}_{/Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Rf_{*}\widehat{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{/Y^{\prime}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C_{3}.}

Here the sheaf
\widehat{\mathcal{M}_{/X}}
and similar for the others is denoted to be the formal completion of
\mathcal{M}
along
X\rightarrow Z
.
Thanks to Claim
6.1.6
, the map
C_{1}\rightarrow C_{2}
and the
C_{1}\rightarrow C_{3}
are isomorphisms.
In particular, the map
C_{2}\rightarrow C_{3}
is an isomorphism.
In this way, as in the last part of the proof for Proposition
6.1.5
, the second and the third rows above produces the following distinguished triangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathcal{M}_{/X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Rf_{*}\widehat{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{/X^{\prime}}}\bigoplus\widehat{\mathcal{M}_{/Y}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Rf_{*}\widehat{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{/Y^{\prime}}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(+,+)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-,+)}

Hence by Lemma
3.3.3
, we may replace those formal completions by their corresponding sheaves over envelopes, and obtain the distinguished triangle below

\textstyle{\mathcal{F}_{D_{X}(Z)}\otimes\Omega_{D_{X}(Z)}^{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Rf_{*}(\mathcal{F}_{D_{X^{\prime}}(Z^{\prime})}\otimes\Omega_{D_{X^{\prime}}(Z^{\prime})}^{i})\moplus\mathcal{F}_{D_{Y}(Z)}\otimes\Omega_{D_{Y}(Z)}^{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Rf_{*}(\mathcal{F}_{D_{Y^{\prime}}(Z^{\prime})}\otimes\Omega_{D_{Y^{\prime}}(Z^{\prime})}^{i}),}

which implies the theorem by taking different
i
and Theorem
4.2.2
.

∎

Remark 6.1.7
.

With the help of the blowup triangle in Theorem
6.1.2
, we could show the following:
for a universal homeomorphism of rigid spaces
f:X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
over
K
and a coherent crystal
\mathcal{F}
over
X/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}}
,
there exists a natural isomorphism of cohomology sheaves as below

Ru_{X/K*}\mathcal{F}\longrightarrow Rf_{*}Ru_{X^{\prime}/K*}\mathcal{F}.

6.2.
Éh-hyperdescent in general

Now we are ready to prove the éh descent for a crystal over the infinitesimal site.
We first deal with the case for the infinitesimal cohomology over an arbitrary
p
-adic field
K
, where the strategy is to use the blowup square for the éh-topology in
[
Guo19
]
and the descent results in the first subsection.
After that, we generalize to the case over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
.

Recall that the éh site
X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}
is defined over the category
{\mathrm{Rig}}_{K}|_{X}
of all
K
-rigid spaces over
X
and is equipped with the éh-topology (cf.
[
Guo19
, Section 2]
).
For an object
X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
in
{\mathrm{Rig}}_{K}|_{X}
, we denote by
\pi_{X^{\prime}}:X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}\rightarrow X^{\prime}_{\mathrm{rig}}
the map from the éh site of
X
to the small rigid site of
X^{\prime}
, and denote by
\pi:X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to{\mathrm{Rig}}_{K}|_{X}
the map from the éh site of
X
to the big rigid site over
X
.

We first associate an infinitesimal crystal together with its de Rham complex an analogous construction over the éh topology.

Construction 6.2.1

(éh de Rham complex)
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be an infinitesimal sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{X/K}
-modules over the big infinitesimal site
X/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}}
.
We then associate a sheaf
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rig}}:=i_{X/K}^{-1}\mathcal{F}
of
\mathcal{O}_{X}=i_{X/K}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{X/K}
-modules over the big rigid site
{\mathrm{Rig}}_{K}|_{X}
, where
i_{X/K}:\mathrm{Sh}({\mathrm{Rig}}_{K}|_{X})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sh}(X/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}})
is the morphism of topoi as in Subsection
2.3
.
Here the section of
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rig}}
at an object
f:X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
in
{\mathrm{Rig}}_{K}|_{X}
is the
\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}(X^{\prime})
-module

\mathcal{F}(X^{\prime},X^{\prime}),

where
(X^{\prime},X^{\prime})\in X/\Sigma_{e{\mathrm{INF}}}
is the trivial thickening of
X^{\prime}
.
We could then sheafify it with the éh-topology, and thus get an éh-sheaf
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}
over the éh site
X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}
.

Now we specify
\mathcal{F}
to be a coherent crystal over big the infinitesimal site.
As in the discussion of Paragraph
3.1
, we could associate
\mathcal{F}
its de Rham complex over the big infinitesimal site

\mathcal{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X/K}}\Omega_{X/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}}}^{\bullet}.

This allows us to get a complex of sheaves over
{\mathrm{Rig}}_{K}|_{X}
and
X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}
respectively

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rig}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rig}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{rig}}}\Omega_{\mathrm{rig}}^{1}\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rig}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{rig}}}\Omega_{\mathrm{rig}}^{2}\longrightarrow\cdots;

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}}\Omega_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}^{1}\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}}\Omega_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}^{2}\longrightarrow\cdots.

Here the sheaf
\Omega_{\mathrm{rig}}^{i}
is the usual continuous Kähler differential sheaf over the rigid site, and
\Omega_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}^{i}
is the éh continuous Kähler differential, which is equal to the éh sheafification of the usual continuous differential.
The complex
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}\otimes\Omega_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}^{\bullet}
is called
éh de Rham complex associated with the crystal
\mathcal{F}
.

The Construction
6.2.1
produces two maps of objects in the derived category of the big rigid topos:

\textstyle{Ru_{X/K*}(\mathcal{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X/K}}\Omega_{X/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}}}^{\bullet})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rig}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{rig}}}\Omega_{\mathrm{rig}}^{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R\pi_{*}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}}\Omega_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}^{\bullet}).}

Formally the first map is given by the sheafified version of the natural transformation

{\mathrm{Rig}}_{K}|_{X}\ni X^{\prime}\longmapsto\left(R\Gamma(X^{\prime}/\Sigma_{e{\mathrm{INF}}},-)\rightarrow R\Gamma((X^{\prime},X^{\prime}),-)\right)

at the complex of infinitesimal sheaves
\mathcal{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X/K}}\Omega_{X/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}}}^{\bullet}
.
The second map above comes from the counit morphism for the adjoint pair
(\pi^{-1},\pi_{*})
, where
\pi^{-1}
is the éh-sheafification functor.
Moreover, the above map can be improved into the filtered derived category, where the left side is equipped with the infinitesimal filtration, and the rest two complexes are equipped with their Hodge filtrations.

We also note that the natural map from the de Rham complex
\mathcal{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X/K}}\Omega_{X/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}}}^{\bullet}
to the crystal
\mathcal{F}
itself induces a natural isomorphism as below

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ru_{X/K*}(\mathcal{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X/K}}\Omega_{X/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}}}^{\bullet})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ru_{X/K*}\mathcal{F},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

which is proved in Proposition
4.2.1
.

Now we can state the descent result.

Theorem 6.2.2
.

Let
X
be a rigid space over
K
, and let
\mathcal{F}
be a coherent crystal over the big infinitesimal site
X/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}}
.
Then the natural map of
K
-linear complexes below is an isomorphism.

Ru_{X/K*}(\mathcal{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X/K}}\Omega_{X/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}}}^{\bullet})\longrightarrow R\pi_{X*}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}}\Omega_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}^{\bullet}).

In particular, the infinitesimal cohomology of the coherent crystal
\mathcal{F}
satisfies the éh-hyperdescent.

Proof.

Step 1

In the Step 1, we show that by restricting to a smooth rigid space
X^{\prime}
over
K
that admits a map to
X
, the morphism in the statement is an isomorphism.
Namely the natural morphism below is an isomorphism

Ru_{X^{\prime}/K*}(\mathcal{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X/K}}\Omega_{X/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}}}^{\bullet})\longrightarrow R\pi_{X^{\prime}*}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}}\Omega_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}^{\bullet}).

We first apply
R\Gamma(X^{\prime},-)
for the smooth rigid space
X^{\prime}
.
On the one hand, we apply Theorem
4.2.2
to the trivial closed immersion
X^{\prime}\rightarrow X^{\prime}
to get

\displaystyle R\Gamma(X^{\prime}/K_{\mathrm{INF}},\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\simeq R\Gamma(X^{\prime}/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}},\mathcal{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X/K}}\Omega_{X/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}}}^{\bullet})

\displaystyle\simeq R\Gamma(X^{\prime},\mathcal{F}_{D}\otimes\Omega_{D}^{\bullet})

\displaystyle=R\Gamma(X^{\prime}_{\mathrm{rig}},\mathcal{F}_{X^{\prime}}\otimes\Omega_{X^{\prime}/K}^{\bullet}),

where the envelope
D
for the trivial closed immersion
X^{\prime}\rightarrow X^{\prime}
is just
X^{\prime}
itself.

On the other hand, the éh-cohomology
R\Gamma(X^{\prime}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}},\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}\otimes\Omega_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}^{\bullet})
is in fact isomorphic to the cohomology of the de Rham complex of
\mathcal{F}_{X^{\prime}}
given by the restriction of
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rig}}\otimes\Omega_{\mathrm{rig}}^{\bullet}
at
X^{\prime}
.
To see this, we notice that as the natural map of complexes
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rig}}\otimes\Omega_{\mathrm{rig}}^{\bullet}\rightarrow R\pi_{X^{\prime}*}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}\otimes\Omega_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}^{\bullet})
is filtered with respect to the Hodge filtrations, it suffices to show for each
i\in\mathbb{N}
the following map is an isomorphism

R\Gamma(X^{\prime}_{\mathrm{rig}},\mathcal{F}_{X^{\prime}}\otimes\Omega_{X^{\prime}/K}^{i})\longrightarrow R\Gamma(X^{\prime}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}},\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}\otimes\Omega_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}^{i}).

Here the restriction
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}|_{X^{\prime}}
at the éh site of
X^{\prime}
can be given by the éh-sheafification of the rigid sheaf
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rig}}|_{X^{\prime}}
over the big site
{\mathrm{Rig}}_{K}|_{X^{\prime}}
.
Moreover, as
\mathcal{F}
is a crystal in vector bundles (Corollary
3.2.4
) and the statement is local on
X^{\prime}
(namely both of the above two complexes satisfies the hyperdescent for rigid topology), by passing to an open rigid subspace of
X^{\prime}
if necessary we may assume the restriction
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{rig}}|_{X^{\prime}}
of
\mathcal{F}
at
X^{\prime}
is isomorphic to the direct sum
\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}^{m}
of structure sheaves.
Thus we reduce to show that the natural map of cohomology of differentials below for a smooth
K
-rigid space
X^{\prime}
is an isomorphism

R\Gamma(X^{\prime}_{\mathrm{rig}},\Omega_{X^{\prime}/K}^{i})\longrightarrow R\Gamma(X^{\prime}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}},\Omega_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}^{i}),

which is proved in
[
Guo19
, Theorem 4.0.2]
.

In this way, as the map in the statement is given by the composition

R\Gamma(X^{\prime}/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}},\mathcal{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X/K}}\Omega_{X/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}}}^{\bullet})\longrightarrow R\Gamma(X^{\prime}_{\mathrm{rig}},\mathcal{F}_{X^{\prime}}\otimes\Omega_{X^{\prime}/K}^{\bullet})\longrightarrow R\Gamma(X^{\prime}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}},\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}}\Omega_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}^{\bullet}),

we see both maps above are isomorphisms when
X^{\prime}
is smooth over
K
.

At last, notice that the cofiber
C
of the map
Ru_{X^{\prime}/K*}(\mathcal{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X/K}}\Omega_{X/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}}}^{\bullet})\longrightarrow R\pi_{X^{\prime}*}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}}\Omega_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}^{\bullet})
is a bounded below complex of sheaves over the small rigid site
X^{\prime}_{\mathrm{rig}}
.
If
C
is not acyclic, then there would exist an open subspace
U
of
X^{\prime}
such that the cohomology
R\Gamma(U_{\mathrm{rig}},C)
does not vanishes, which contradicts to the computation above.
So we get the isomorphism for smooth
K
-rigid space
X^{\prime}
that admits a map to
X
:

Ru_{X^{\prime}/K*}(\mathcal{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X/K}}\Omega_{X/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}}}^{\bullet})\longrightarrow R\pi_{X^{\prime}*}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}}\Omega_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}^{\bullet}).

Step 2

Now we prove the isomorphism of the derived push-forwards as in the statement.

Let
i:X_{\mathrm{red}}\rightarrow X
be the closed immersion by the reduced sub rigid space of
X
.
We first notice that the natural map below is an isomorphism

Ru_{X/K*}\mathcal{F}\longrightarrow i_{*}Ru_{X_{\mathrm{red}}/K*}(\mathcal{F}),

This is because locally both of them are computed using the de Rham complex
\mathcal{F}_{D}\otimes\Omega_{D}^{\bullet}
, where
D
is the envelope of
X
in a smooth rigid spaces (Theorem
4.2.2
).
The same isomorphism holds for the derived direct image of the infinitesimal de Rham complex
\mathcal{F}\otimes\Omega_{X_{\mathrm{red}}/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}}}^{\bullet}
by Proposition
4.2.1
.
On the other hand, We notice that as the closed immersion
i:X_{\mathrm{red}}\rightarrow X
is an éh-covering (
[
Guo19
, Section 2.4]
), which forms a constant éh-hypercovering as the product
X_{\mathrm{red}}\times_{X}X_{\mathrm{red}}
is equal to
X_{\mathrm{red}}
itself, we get

R\pi_{X*}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}\otimes\Omega_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}^{\bullet})\simeq i_{*}R\pi_{X_{\mathrm{red}}*}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}\otimes\Omega_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}^{\bullet}).

Thus the above two isomorphisms allow us to assume
X
is reduced, and by passing to an open subset if necessary we may assume
X
is quasi-compact.

Now we can do the induction on the dimension of
X
.
When
\dim(X)
is of dimension zero, as
X
is quasi-compact and reduced, it is then equal to a disjoint union of finite points and in particular is smooth over
K
, where the statement follows from the Step 1.

In general, by Temkin’s resolution of singularities for rigid spaces (
[
Tem12
, 1.2.1, 5.2.2]
), we can find a finite compositions
X_{n}\rightarrow\cdots X_{1}\rightarrow X_{0}=X
, where each
X_{i}\rightarrow X_{i-1}
is a blowup at a smooth nowhere dense closed subspace
Y_{i}\subset X_{i-1}
, such that in the last step
X_{n}
is smooth over
K
.
We denote by
Y_{i}^{\prime}
the preimage
Y_{i}\times_{X_{i-1}}X_{i}
in
X_{i}
, which is of dimension strictly smaller than
\dim(X_{i})=\dim(X)
, and we let
f_{i}
be the blowup map
X_{i}\rightarrow X_{i-1}
.
Then for each
1\leq i\leq n
, we get a natural distinguished triangle by Theorem
6.1.2

Ru_{X_{i-1}/K*}(\mathcal{F}\otimes\Omega_{{\mathrm{INF}}}^{\bullet})\longrightarrow Rf_{i*}Ru_{X_{i}/K*}(\mathcal{F}\otimes\Omega_{{\mathrm{INF}}}^{\bullet})\moplus Ru_{Y_{i}/K*}(\mathcal{F}\otimes\Omega_{{\mathrm{INF}}}^{\bullet})\longrightarrow Rf_{i*}Ru_{Y^{\prime}_{i}/K*}(\mathcal{F}\otimes\Omega_{{\mathrm{INF}}}^{\bullet}).

Again here we use Proposition
4.2.1
to replace the
\mathcal{F}
by its de Rham complex.
On the other hand, by the sheafified version of the blowup square in the éh-topology (
[
Guo19
, Proposition 5.1.4]
), we have a natural distinguished triangle

R\pi_{X_{i-1}*}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}\otimes\Omega_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}^{\bullet})\longrightarrow R\pi_{X_{i}*}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}\otimes\Omega_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}^{\bullet})\moplus R\pi_{Y_{i}*}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}\otimes\Omega_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}^{\bullet})\longrightarrow R\pi_{Y_{i}^{\prime}*}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}\otimes\Omega_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}^{\bullet}).

The functoriality of the map in the statement allows us to produce a map from the triangle
(\ast)
to the triangle
(\ast\ast)
.
Moreover, by the dimension assumption and the induction assumption, we know the statement in the Theorem is true for
X_{n}
and all of
Y_{i}
and
Y_{i}^{\prime}
.
In this way, since
X_{0}=X
, by a finite step of descending inductions via comparing the above two triangles
(\ast)
and
(\ast\ast)
, the natural map below is then an isomorphism

Ru_{X/K*}(\mathcal{F}\otimes\Omega_{X/K_{\mathrm{INF}}}^{\bullet})\longrightarrow R\pi_{X*}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}\otimes\Omega_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}^{\bullet}).

So we are done.

∎

Remark 6.2.3
.

As in
Remark

1.2.5
, by the construction of the map in Theorem
6.2.2
, we see that (the underlying complex of) the infinitesimal cohomology of a coherent crystal
\mathcal{F}
over
X/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}}
is a direct summand of the cohomology
R\Gamma(X_{\mathrm{rig}},\mathcal{F}_{X}\otimes\Omega_{X/K}^{\bullet})
of the usual de Rham complex over
X_{\mathrm{rig}}
.

Remark 6.2.4
.

The isomorphism in the Theorem above cannot always be improved into a filtered isomorphism.
The discrepancy already appears in the schematic theory (see
[
Bha12
, Example 5.6]
).

Now we are able to generalize the éh-hyperdescent to coherent crystals over
X/\Sigma_{e{\mathrm{INF}}}
for general
e
, not just
K
-linear crystals.
We assume
K
is complete and algebraically closed in the next Theorem, so
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
is well-defined for
K
.

Theorem 6.2.5
.

Let
X
be a rigid space over
X
, and let
\mathcal{F}
be a crystal in vector bundles over the big infinitesimal site
X/\Sigma_{e{\mathrm{INF}}}
.
Then the infinitesimal cohomology of
\mathcal{F}
over
X/\Sigma_{e{\mathrm{INF}}}
satisfies the éh-hyperdescent.
Namely for an éh-hypercovering
X_{\bullet}^{\prime}\rightarrow X^{\prime}
of
K
-rigid spaces over
X
, the following natural map is an isomorphism

R\Gamma(X^{\prime}/\Sigma_{e{\mathrm{INF}}},\mathcal{F})\longrightarrow R\lim_{\Delta}\left(R\Gamma(X^{\prime}_{\bullet}/\Sigma_{e{\mathrm{INF}}},\mathcal{F})\right).

Proof.

We prove the result by induction on
e
.
For
e=1
, it is Theorem
6.2.2
.
In general, we take the derived tensor product of short exact sequence
0\rightarrow K\rightarrow{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}\rightarrow\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR},e-1}^{+}\rightarrow 0
with the complex of sheaves
Ru_{X/\Sigma_{e}*}\mathcal{F}
.
By the big site version of the base change formula in Proposition
4.2.3
(cf. Corollary
2.2.8
), we get a natural distinguished triangle

Ru_{X/K*}\mathcal{F}_{1}\longrightarrow Ru_{X/\Sigma_{e}*}\mathcal{F}\longrightarrow Ru_{X/\Sigma_{e-1}*}\mathcal{F}_{e-1},

where
\mathcal{F}_{1}
and
\mathcal{F}_{e-1}
are pullbacks of
\mathcal{F}
along maps of sites
X/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}}\rightarrow X/\Sigma_{e{\mathrm{INF}}}
and
X/\Sigma_{e-1,{\mathrm{INF}}}\rightarrow X/\Sigma_{e{\mathrm{INF}}}
respectively.
In this way, applying the natural transformation
R\Gamma(X^{\prime},-)\rightarrow R\lim_{\Delta}R\Gamma(X^{\prime}_{\bullet},-)
to the above triangle, we get the result by induction.
∎

6.3.
Finiteness

With the use of the éh-hyperdescent, we show in this subsection the finiteness and the cohomological boundedness of the infinitesimal cohomology, assuming the properness of the rigid space.

Theorem 6.3.1
.

Let
X
be a proper rigid space over
K
, and let
\mathcal{F}
be a crystal in vector bundles over the big infinitesimal site
X/\Sigma_{e{\mathrm{INF}}}
.
Then the infinitesimal cohomology
R\Gamma(X/\Sigma_{e{\mathrm{INF}}},\mathcal{F})
is a bounded complex supported in the cohomological degrees
[0,2n]
, where each cohomology is a finite
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
-module.

Proof.

Smooth case

We first notice that when
X
is smooth, the infinitesimal cohomology
R\Gamma(X/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}},\mathcal{F})
is computed by the cohomology of the de Rham complex
\mathcal{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\Omega_{X/K}^{\bullet}
via Theorem
4.2.2
; namely we have

R\Gamma(X/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}},\mathcal{F})\simeq R\Gamma(X,\mathcal{F}\otimes\Omega_{X/K}^{\bullet}).

Moreover, each term
\mathcal{F}\otimes\Omega_{X/K}^{i}
of the de Rham complex is a coherent sheaf over
X
.
Notice that the cohomology of a coherent sheaf over a quasi-compact rigid space vanishes when the degree is above the dimension (
[
dJvdP
, Proposition 2.5.8]
).
Thus by the Hodge–de Rham spectral sequence for
\mathcal{F}\otimes\Omega_{X/K}^{\bullet}
, we get the result for
R\Gamma(X/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}},\mathcal{F})
with smooth proper
X
.

In general, we use the base change formula in Proposition
4.2.3
.
By taking the derived tensor product of
Ru_{X/\Sigma_{e}*}\mathcal{F}
with the short exact sequence
0\rightarrow K\rightarrow{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}\rightarrow\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR},e-1}\rightarrow 0
, we get a distinguished triangle

R\Gamma(X/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}},\mathcal{F})\longrightarrow R\Gamma(X/\Sigma_{e{\mathrm{INF}}},\mathcal{F})\longrightarrow R\Gamma(X/\Sigma_{e-1{\mathrm{INF}}},\mathcal{F}).

In this way, the claim for smooth proper
X
follows from the induction on
e
.

General case

In general, we prove by induction on the dimension of
X
.
When
X
is of dimension zero, it is equal to a nilpotent extension of several points
\mathrm{Spa}(K)
.
So the result follows from the éh-hyperdescent along closed immersions by reduced subspaces in Theorem
6.2.5
(in other words, we apply to the Čech nerve at closed immersion of the reduced subspace).

Now assume
X
is reduced of dimension
n
, and the claim is true for any rigid space of smaller dimension.
By the resolution of singularities of rigid space in
[
Tem18
]
, there exists a finite sequence of maps
X_{m}\rightarrow\cdots X_{1}\rightarrow X_{0}=X
, where
X_{m}
is smooth, and each map
X_{i}\rightarrow X_{i-1}
is a blowup at a closed analytic subspace
Y_{i-1}
of
X_{i-1}
, such that each
Y_{i-1}
is nowhere dense in
X_{i-1}
.
We denote
E_{i}
to be the exceptional locus
Y_{i-1}\times_{X_{i-1}}X_{i}
of the
i
-th blowup.
We could then apply the éh-hyperdescent in Theorem
6.2.5
to the Čech nerve associated with the blowup covering
X_{i}\coprod Y_{i-1}\rightarrow X_{i-1}
.
The limit
R\lim_{\Delta}
of the infinitesimal cohomology for the hypercovering is isomorphic to the fiber of the blowup square

R\Gamma(X_{i}/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}},\mathcal{F})\moplus R\Gamma(Y_{i-1}/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}},\mathcal{F})\longrightarrow R\Gamma(Y_{i}/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}},\mathcal{F}),

and thus we get a long exact sequence

\cdots\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{j}(X_{i-1}/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}},\mathcal{F})\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{j}(X_{i}/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}},\mathcal{F})\moplus\mathrm{H}^{j}(Y_{i-1}/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}},\mathcal{F})\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{j}(Y_{i}/K_{{\mathrm{INF}}},\mathcal{F})\longrightarrow\cdots.

In this way, with the help of the induction assumption for all
Y_{i}
, a further descending induction from
X_{m}
to
X_{0}=X
finishes the proof.

∎

6.4.
Algebraic and analytic infinitesimal cohomology

At the end of the section, we prove the comparison between the algebraic infinitesimal cohomology and the analytic infinitesimal cohomology, for a proper algebraic variety.

Recall that for an algebraic variety
10
10

10

For our purpose, a
variety
is defined to be a locally of finite type scheme over a field in the article.

\mathscr{X}
over a
p
-adic field
K
, we can define its
(algebraic) infinitesimal site
\mathscr{X}/K_{\inf}
, whose objects are schematic infinitesimal thickenings
(\mathscr{U},\mathscr{T})
, where
\mathscr{U}
is an Zariski open subset of
\mathscr{X}
.
The infinitesimal site
\mathscr{X}/K_{\inf}
is equipped with a structure sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}/K}
, and its cohomology is called the
algebraic infinitesimal cohomology
.
Moreover, the infinitesimal structure sheaf admits a surjection
\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}/K}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}}
to the Zariski structure sheaf, whose kernel
\mathcal{J}_{\mathscr{X}/K}
defines a natural filtration on
R\Gamma(\mathscr{X}/K_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}/K})
.
Similar to the analytic theory, we call this filtration the
(algebraic) infinitesimal filtration
.

Let
X=\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{an}}
be the rigid space over
K
defined as the analytification of a variety
\mathscr{X}
.
As the analytification functor
{\mathrm{Sch}}_{K}\rightarrow{\mathrm{Rig}}_{K}
preserves open and closed immersions, it induces a natural map of ringed sites

(X/K_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X/K})\longrightarrow(\mathscr{X}/K_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}/K}).

Moreover, as the surjection
\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}/K}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}}
is compatible with
\mathcal{O}_{X/K}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}
,
the natural map of infinitesimal structure sheaves above is then a filtered map.
As a consequence, by passing to their cohomology, we get a natural filtered morphism in derived category

R\Gamma(\mathscr{X}/K_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}/K})\longrightarrow R\Gamma(X/K_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X/K}).

Our main result in this subsection is the following.

Theorem 6.4.1
.

Let
\mathscr{X}
be a proper algebraic variety over
K
, and let
X
be its analytifiation.
Then the analytification functor induces a filtered isomorphism of the infinitesimal cohomology

R\Gamma(\mathscr{X}/K_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}/K})\longrightarrow R\Gamma(X/K_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X/K}).

Before the proof, we first recall that there is a natural map of ringed sites
(X_{\mathrm{rig}},\mathcal{O}_{X})\rightarrow(\mathscr{X}_{{\mathrm{Zar}}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}})
.
Here the rigid structure sheaf is flat over the Zariski structure sheaf,
and the pullback along the map induces a fully faithful functor from coherent
\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}}
-modules to coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules.

Moreover, the above map of sites is compatible with the infinitesimal topos.
Recall that there exists a natural map of topoi

\displaystyle u_{\mathscr{X}/K}:\mathrm{Sh}(\mathscr{X}/K_{\inf})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}(\mathscr{X}_{{\mathrm{Zar}}});

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}

\displaystyle\longmapsto(\mathscr{U}\mapsto\Gamma(\mathscr{U}/K_{\inf},\mathcal{F}|_{\mathscr{U}})).

By construction, this functor is compatible with its rigid version
u_{X/K}:\mathrm{Sh}(X/K_{\inf})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{rig}})
(cf. Subsection
2.3
).
Namely, the following diagram is commutative

\textstyle{\mathrm{Sh}(X/K_{\inf})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u_{X/K}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sh}(\mathscr{X}/K_{\inf})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u_{\mathscr{X}/K}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{rig}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sh}(\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{Zar}}).}

We then claim the following result.

Proposition 6.4.2
.

Let
\mathscr{X}
be an algebraic variety over
K
, and let
X
be its analytifcation.
Then the complex of coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules
Ru_{X/K*}(\mathcal{J}_{X/K}^{n}/\mathcal{J}_{X/K}^{n+1})
is naturally isomorphic to the analytification of the complex of coherent
\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}}
-modules
Ru_{\mathscr{X}/K*}(\mathcal{J}_{\mathscr{X}/K}^{n}/\mathcal{J}_{\mathscr{X}/K}^{n+1})
.

Proof.

We denote the complex of coherent
\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}}
-modules
Ru_{\mathscr{X}/K*}(\mathcal{J}_{\mathscr{X}/K}^{n}/\mathcal{J}_{\mathscr{X}/K}^{n+1})
by
C
, and
Ru_{X/K*}(\mathcal{J}_{X/K}^{n}/\mathcal{J}_{X/K}^{n+1})
by
C^{\prime}
.
Then it suffices to show that the natural map below induced from the pullback from
\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{Zar}}
to
X_{\mathrm{rig}}
is an isomorphism of complexes of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules

C\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}}}\mathcal{O}_{X}\longrightarrow C^{\prime}.

As the result is a local statement for
\mathscr{X}
, let us assume
\mathscr{X}=\Spec(A)
is a finite type affine scheme over
K
and
\mathscr{X}\rightarrow\mathscr{Y}=\Spec(A^{\prime})
be a closed immersion into an affine space over
K
.
Moreover, notice that the isomorphism could be checked locally on
X
, so we may take an open affinoid disc of certain radius
\mathrm{Spa}(B^{\prime})
in
\mathscr{Y}^{\mathrm{an}}
, with the open subset
X\cap\mathrm{Spa}(B^{\prime})=\mathrm{Spa}(B)
in
X
.
From our choices, we get a cartesian diagram as below, where horizontal maps are surjective and vertical maps are flat

\textstyle{B^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B}
\textstyle{A^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

So it suffices to show that

R\Gamma(\Spec(A)/K_{\inf},\mathcal{J}_{\mathscr{X}/K}^{n}/\mathcal{J}_{\mathscr{X}/K}^{n+1})\otimes_{A}B\simeq R\Gamma(\mathrm{Spa}(B)/K_{\inf},\mathcal{J}_{X/K}^{n}/\mathcal{J}_{X/K}^{n+1}).

We then recall from
[
BdJ
, Section 2]
that the algebraic infinitesimal cohomology can be computed by the Čech-Alexander complex as below

D\longrightarrow D(1)\longrightarrow D(2)\longrightarrow\cdots,

where
D(m)
is the formal completion of
A^{\prime}(m):={A^{\prime}}^{\underset{K}{\otimes}m+1}
along the surjection
A^{\prime}(m)\rightarrow A
.
We take the
n
-th graded piece for the algebraic infinitesimal filtration,
then the cohomology group
R\Gamma(\Spec(A)/K_{\inf},\mathcal{J}_{\mathscr{X}/K}^{n}/\mathcal{J}_{\mathscr{X}/K}^{n+1})
is isomorphic to the following map of
A
-linear cosimplicial complexes

J_{D}^{n}/J_{D}^{n+1}\longrightarrow J_{D(1)}^{n}/J_{D(n)}^{n+1}\longrightarrow J_{D(2)}^{n}/J_{D(2)}^{n+1}\longrightarrow\cdots,

where
J_{D(m)}
is the kernel of the surjection
A^{\prime}(m)\rightarrow A
.
On the other hand, by the Čech-Alexander complex for rigid spaces in Proposition
4.1.3
, we have

R\Gamma(\mathrm{Spa}(B)/K_{\inf},\mathcal{J}_{X/K}^{n}/\mathcal{J}_{X/K}^{n+1})\simeq\left(J_{\mathcal{D}}^{n}/J_{\mathcal{D}}^{n+1}\longrightarrow J_{\mathcal{D}(1)}^{n}/J_{\mathcal{D}(1)}^{n+1}\longrightarrow J_{\mathcal{D}(2)}^{n}/J_{\mathcal{D}(2)}^{n+1}\longrightarrow\cdots\right),

where
\mathcal{D}(m)
is the formal completion for the surjection
B^{\prime}(m):={B^{\prime}}^{\underset{K}{\widehat{\otimes}}m+1}\rightarrow B
, and
J_{\mathcal{D}(m)}
is the kernel of the map
B^{\prime}(m)\rightarrow B
.
Thus we are left to show the quasi-isomorphism for the canonical map of
B
-linear cosimplicial complexes below

\displaystyle\bigl(J_{D}^{n}/J_{D}^{n+1}\to J_{D(1)}^{n}/J_{D(n)}^{n+1}\to J_{D(2)}^{n}/J_{D(2)}^{n+1}

\displaystyle\to\cdots\bigr)\otimes_{A}B\longrightarrow

\displaystyle\bigl(J_{\mathcal{D}}^{n}/J_{\mathcal{D}}^{n+1}

\displaystyle\to J_{\mathcal{D}(1)}^{n}/J_{\mathcal{D}(1)}^{n+1}\to J_{\mathcal{D}(2)}^{n}/J_{\mathcal{D}(2)}^{n+1}\to\cdots\bigr).

At last notice that by our choices, the rigid space
\mathrm{Spa}(B^{\prime})
is an open disc of some radius in the affine space
\Spec(A^{\prime})^{\mathrm{an}}
.
In particular, the following map of rings is a cartesian diagram such that vertical maps are flat

\textstyle{B^{\prime}(m)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B^{\prime}}
\textstyle{A^{\prime}(m)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

In this way, combining this with the cartesian diagram in the first paragraph, we see the kernel
J_{\mathcal{D}(m)}
of the surjection
B^{\prime}(m)\rightarrow B
is equal to the base change of
J_{D(m)}
along the flat map
A^{\prime}(m)\rightarrow B^{\prime}(m)
.
Hence we get the natural equalities

\displaystyle J_{D(m)}B^{\prime}(m)=J_{D(m)}\otimes_{A^{\prime}(m)}B^{\prime}(m)=J_{\mathcal{D}(m)};

\displaystyle(J_{D(m)}^{n}/J_{D(m)}^{n+1})\otimes_{A}B=(J_{D(m)}^{n}/J_{D(m)}^{n+1})\otimes_{A^{\prime}(m)}B^{\prime}(m)=J_{\mathcal{D}(m)}^{n}/J_{\mathcal{D}(m)}^{n+1}.

So we are done.

∎

At last, we finish the proof of Theorem
6.4.1
.

Proof of Theorem
6.4.1
.

To show the natural map in the statement is a filtered isomorphism, it suffices to show the isomorphisms for their underlying complexes and each graded piece separately, as both of them are filtered complete.

11
11

11

For each affinoid infinitesimal thickening
(U,T)
, the kernel ideal
\mathcal{J}_{T}=\ker(\mathcal{O}_{T}\to\mathcal{O}_{U})
is nilpotent and hence
\mathcal{O}_{T}
is complete under
\mathcal{J}_{T}
-adic topology.
This in particular implies the inverse limit formula
\mathcal{O}_{X/K}=\varprojlim_{i}\mathcal{O}_{X/K}/\mathcal{J}_{X/K}^{i}
, and similarly for the algebraic version.

For the underlying complexes, this follows from the éh descent.
To see this, we first notice that when
\mathscr{X}
is smooth and proper over
K
, then the algebraic and the analytic infinitesimal cohomology are isomorphic to the algebraic and the analytic de Rham cohomology respectively (
[
Gr68
]
, Theorem
4.2.2
),
which are isomorphic to each other by applying the GAGA theorem to their Hodge-filtrations (cf.
[
Con06
, Appendix A.1]
).
In general, we may assume
\mathscr{X}_{\bullet}\rightarrow\mathscr{X}
is a simplicial smooth varieties by resolving singularities.
Then its analytification
X_{\bullet}\rightarrow X
is an éh-hypercovering by smooth rigid spaces, and we get the isomorphism

\displaystyle R\Gamma(\mathscr{X}/K_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}/K})

\displaystyle\simeq R\lim_{[n]\in\Delta}R\Gamma(\mathscr{X}_{n}/K_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}_{n}/K})

\displaystyle\simeq R\lim_{[n]\in\Delta}R\Gamma(X_{n}/K_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X_{n}/K})

\displaystyle\simeq R\Gamma(X/K_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X/K}),

where the first equality is the h-hyperdescent of algebraic de Rham cohomology for blowups in
[
Har75
]
, and the last is the éh-hyperdescent for the analytic infinitesimal cohomology in
6.2.2
.

For the graded pieces, by Proposition
6.4.2
we have

R\Gamma(X/K_{\inf},\mathcal{J}_{X/K}^{n}/\mathcal{J}_{X/K}^{n+1})=R\Gamma(X_{\mathrm{rig}},Ru_{X/K*}(\mathcal{J}_{X/K}^{n}/\mathcal{J}_{X/K}^{n+1}))\simeq R\Gamma(X_{\mathrm{rig}},\left(Ru_{\mathscr{X}/K*}(\mathcal{J}_{\mathscr{X}/K}^{n}/\mathcal{J}_{\mathscr{X}/K}^{n+1})\right)^{\mathrm{an}}).

We denote
C
to be the bounded below complex of coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules
