Ru_{\mathscr{X}/K*}(\mathcal{J}_{\mathscr{X}/K}^{n}/\mathcal{J}_{\mathscr{X}/K}^{n+1})
.
As
R\Gamma(X_{\mathrm{Zar}},\tau^{>n}C)
lives in cohomogical degree larger than
n
, we have the natural equalities

R\Gamma(X_{\mathrm{Zar}},C)={\mathrm{colim}}_{n}R\Gamma(X_{\mathrm{Zar}},\tau^{\leq n}C).

Similarly we have

R\Gamma(X_{\mathrm{rig}},C^{\mathrm{an}})={\mathrm{colim}}_{n}R\Gamma(X_{\mathrm{rig}},\tau^{\leq n}(C^{\mathrm{an}})).

On the other hand, as the rigid structure sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{X}
is flat over
\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}}
, the analytification functor
(-)^{\mathrm{an}}=-\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}}}\mathcal{O}_{X}
on coherent complexes is an exact functor.
So for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists a natural equality

\tau^{\leq n}C^{\mathrm{an}}=(\tau^{\leq n}C)^{\mathrm{an}}.

Notice that for each bounded complex
\tau^{\leq n}C
of coherent sheaves, by rigid GAGA theorem (
[
Con06
, Appendix A.1]
) we have

R\Gamma(X_{\mathrm{Zar}},\tau^{\leq n}C)\simeq R\Gamma(X_{\mathrm{rig}},(\tau^{\leq n}C)^{\mathrm{an}}).

In this way, combining all of the isomorphisms above, we get

\displaystyle R\Gamma(X_{\mathrm{Zar}},C)

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathrm{colim}}_{n}R\Gamma(X_{\mathrm{Zar}},\tau^{\leq n}C)

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathrm{colim}}_{n}R\Gamma(X_{\mathrm{rig}},(\tau^{\leq n}C)^{\mathrm{an}})

\displaystyle={\mathrm{colim}}_{n}R\Gamma(X_{\mathrm{rig}},\tau^{\leq n}(C^{\mathrm{an}}))

\displaystyle\simeq R\Gamma(X_{\mathrm{rig}},C^{\mathrm{an}}).

At last, substituting back the definition of
C
and Proposition
6.4.2
, we then obtain the formula for graded piece of infinitesimal filtrations:

R\Gamma(\mathscr{X}/K_{\inf},\mathcal{J}_{\mathscr{X}/K}^{n}/\mathcal{J}_{\mathscr{X}/K}^{n+1})\simeq R\Gamma(X/K_{\inf},\mathcal{J}_{X/K}^{n}/\mathcal{J}_{X/K}^{n+1}).

∎

As an application, we get the comparison with singular cohomology when
K
is abstractly isomorphic to the field of complex numbers, proving Theorem
1.2.1
.(v).

Corollary 6.4.3
.

Assume there exists an abstract isomorphism of fields
K\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
.
Then for any proper algebraic variety
\mathscr{X}/K
with its analytification
X
, there exists a filtered isomorphism of cohomology

\mathrm{H}^{i}(X/K_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X/K})\simeq\mathrm{H}^{i}_{\textrm{Sing}}(\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{C}),\mathbb{C}),

where singular cohomology of
\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{C})
is filtered by the algebraic infinitesimal filtration.

Proof.

This follows from Theorem
6.4.1
and the classical result of Hartshorne in
[
Har75
]
.
∎

Using the same idea of the proof for Proposition
6.4.2
and Theorem
6.4.1
, we can prove the base extension formula for the infinitesimal cohomology.

Corollary 6.4.4
.

Let
K_{0}
be a complete
p
-adic extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, and let
K
be a complete extension of
K_{0}
.
Assume
X
is a proper rigid space over
K_{0}
, and let
\mathcal{F}
be a coherent crystal over
X/K_{0,\inf}
.
Then the following natural map of filtered complexes is an isomorphism

R\Gamma(X/K_{0,\inf},\mathcal{F})\otimes_{K_{0}}K\longrightarrow R\Gamma(X_{K}/K_{\inf},\mathcal{F}_{K}).

7.
Cohomology over
{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}

In this section, we extend previous results to the infinitesimal site over
{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}
, for a rigid space
X
over
\Sigma_{r}
for some fixed
r\in\mathbb{N}
.
Our goal is to show
Theorem

1.2.7
from the introduction.

7.1.
Infinitesimal sites and topoi over
{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}

We fix a complete algebraic closed
p
-adic field
K
.
Let
X
be a rigid space over
{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}/\xi^{r}
for some fixed
r\in\mathbb{N}
.
To build an infinitesimal cohomology theory with the coefficient being
{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}=\varprojlim_{e\in\mathbb{N}}{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
, we construct an infinitesimal site
X/\Sigma_{\inf}
as a union of all
X/\Sigma_{e\inf}
for
e\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq r}
, and consider its relation to each infinitesimal site
X/\Sigma_{e\inf}
.

The site
X/\Sigma_{\inf}
.

We first give the definition of the infinitesimal site over
\Sigma=\varinjlim_{e\in\mathbb{N}}\Sigma_{e}
, where the latter is regarded as the ringed space whose underlying topological space is
\mathrm{Spa}(K)
with the structure sheaf given by
{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}
.

Definition 7.1.1
.

Let
X
be a rigid space over
\Sigma_{r}=\mathrm{Spa}({\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}/\xi^{r})
, for some fixed
r\in\mathbb{N}
.
The
infinitesimal site
X/\Sigma_{\inf}
over
{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}
is defined as follows:

•

The underlying category of
X/\Sigma_{\inf}
is the category of pairs
(U,T)
, for
(U,T)
being an thickening in
X/\Sigma_{e\inf}
for some
e\geq r
.

A morphism between
(U_{1},T_{1})
and
(U_{2},T_{2})
is a morphism of objects in
X/\Sigma_{e\inf}
, for
e
large enough such that both pairs are objects in
X/\Sigma_{e\inf}
.

•

A collection of morphism
(U_{i},T_{i})\rightarrow(U,T)
in
X/\Sigma_{\inf}
is a covering if
\{T_{i}\rightarrow T\}
is an open covering for the rigid space
T
.

As a category,
X/\Sigma_{\inf}
is the union of
X/\Sigma_{e\inf}
for all
e\geq r
.
It is clear that the topology is locally rigid over each object in
X/\Sigma_{\inf}
.
Thus the description of a sheaf over
X/\Sigma_{\inf}
is similar to that of a sheaf over
X/\Sigma_{e\inf}
as in Section
2
.

Remark 7.1.2
.

Similarly to the Discussion in Section
2
, we could define the big version infinitesimal site
X/\Sigma_{{\mathrm{INF}}}
, where the objects are infinitesimal thickenings
(U,T)
for
U
being a rigid space over
X
and
U\rightarrow T
a nil-extension over
{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}
.
The relation between the big infinitesimal sites
X/\Sigma_{{\mathrm{INF}}}
and the small one
X/\Sigma_{\inf}
, including the constructions in the rest of the subsection, are exactly identical to the case over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
in Paragraph
2.1
, and we will not duplicate again here.

Functoriality of
\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{\inf})
.

The infinitesimal topos
\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{\inf})
is functorial with respect to the rigid space
X
.
Namely, for a map of
{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}
-rigid spaces
f:X\rightarrow Y
where
\xi
is nilpotent in both
\mathcal{O}_{X}
and
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
, we have a natural map of topoi

f_{\inf}:\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{\inf})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}(Y/\Sigma_{\inf}).

The corresponding adjoint pair of functors are given by the following:

•

For a sheaf
\mathcal{G}\in\mathrm{Sh}(Y/\Sigma_{\inf})
, the inverse image
f_{\inf}^{-1}\mathcal{G}
is given by the restriction of
\mu_{Y}^{-1}\mathcal{G}
to the category
X/\Sigma_{\inf}
along the map
f
, and is equal to the sheaf associated with the presheaf

X/\Sigma_{\inf}\ni(U,T)\longmapsto\varinjlim_{\begin{subarray}{c}(U,T)\rightarrow(V,S)\\
(V,S)\in Y/\Sigma_{\inf},\\
U\rightarrow V~compatible~with~f\end{subarray}}\mathcal{G}(V,S).

•

The direct image functor
f_{\inf*}
sends a sheaf
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{\inf})
to the sheaf

f_{\inf*}\mathcal{F}(V,S)=\varprojlim_{\begin{subarray}{c}(U,T)\rightarrow(V,S)\\
(U,T)\in X/\Sigma_{{\mathrm{INF}}}\\
U\rightarrow V~compatible~with~f\end{subarray}}\mathcal{F}(U,T).

We want to remind the reader that the construction of those two functors are identical with the construction of the functoriality morphism
\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{e\inf})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sh}(Y/\Sigma_{e^{\prime}\inf})
for the map of rigid spaces

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Sigma_{e}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Sigma_{e^{\prime}},}

as in Subsection
2.4
.

Relation with
X/\Sigma_{e\inf}
.

Topologically, the infinitesimal site
X/\Sigma_{\inf}
is the limit of
X/\Sigma_{e\inf}
for
e\geq r
.
To make this precise, we consider the following morphism of sites:

u_{e}:X/\Sigma_{\inf}\longrightarrow X/\Sigma_{e\inf},

whose corresponding functor is the canonical inclusion functor that sends
(U,T)\in X/\Sigma_{e\inf}
to the object
(U,T)\in X/\Sigma_{\inf}
.
Note that by construction, this cocontinuous functor is a fully faithful embedding.

This morphism induces an adjoint pair of functors
(u_{e}^{-1},u_{e*})
given as follows:

•

The functor
u_{e*}
is the restriction functor, in a way that for a sheaf
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{\inf})
we have

(u_{e*}\mathcal{F})_{T}=\mathcal{F}_{T}.

•

For a sheaf
\mathcal{G}\in\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{e\inf})
, the sheaf
u_{e}^{-1}\mathcal{G}
is the sheaf associated with the presheaf

\displaystyle(V,S)

\displaystyle\mapsto\varinjlim_{\begin{subarray}{c}(V,S)\rightarrow(U,T)\\
(U,T)\in X/\Sigma_{e\inf}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{G}(U,T)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\varnothing,~S\notin{\mathrm{Rig}}_{\Sigma_{e}};\\
\mathcal{G}(V,S),~S\in{\mathrm{Rig}}_{\Sigma_{e}}.\end{cases}

So by the definition of the site
X/\Sigma_{\inf}
, the restriction of
u_{e}^{-1}\mathcal{G}
at
(V,S)
is

(u_{e}^{-1}\mathcal{G})_{S}=\begin{cases}\varnothing,~S\notin{\mathrm{Rig}}_{\Sigma_{e}};\\
\mathcal{G}_{S},~S\in{\mathrm{Rig}}_{\Sigma_{e}}.\end{cases}

Here we notice that when
\mathcal{G}=h_{(U,T)}
is the representable sheaf for some object
(U,T)\in X/\Sigma_{e\inf}
, the inverse image
u_{e}^{-1}h_{(U,T)}
is nothing but the representable sheaf
h_{(U,T)}
in
\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{\inf})
.

The morphism of site
u_{e}:X/\Sigma_{\inf}\rightarrow X/\Sigma_{e\inf}
induces a map of topoi

u_{e}:\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{\inf})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{e\inf}).

It admits a section
i_{e}:\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{e\inf})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{\inf})
, where the corresponding adjoint pair of functors is given as follows:

•

For a sheaf
\mathcal{G}\in\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{inf})
, the inverse image
i_{e}^{-1}\mathcal{G}
is the sheaf associated with the presheaf

X/\Sigma_{e\inf}\ni(U,T)\longmapsto\varinjlim_{\begin{subarray}{c}(U,T)\rightarrow(U,S)\\
(U,S)\in X/\Sigma_{\inf}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{G}(U,S)=\mathcal{G}(U,T).

Namely,
i_{e}^{-1}=u_{e*}
is the restriction functor.

•

The direct image functor
i_{e*}
sends a sheaf
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{e\inf})
to the sheaf

i_{e*}\mathcal{F}(V,S)=\varprojlim_{\begin{subarray}{c}(V,T)\rightarrow(V,S)\\
(V,T)\in X/\Sigma_{e\inf}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{F}(V,T)=\mathcal{F}(V,S\times_{\Sigma}\Sigma_{e}).

It is clear that the composition
u_{e}\circ i_{e}
is equal to the identity.
We also note that the above functors are functorial with respect to
e
.

Remark 7.1.3
.

Here we notice that the map
i_{e}
is in fact induced from a natural map of sites

\displaystyle i_{e}:X/\Sigma_{e\inf}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow X/\Sigma_{\inf};

\displaystyle(U,T\times_{\Sigma}\Sigma_{e})

\displaystyle\reflectbox{$\longmapsto$}(U,T).

This is analogous to the nilpotent bases situation, as in the Remark
2.4.3

Remark 7.1.4
.

We also want to reminder the reader that the construction of map
i_{e}
could be regarded as the functoriality morphism of infinitesimal topoi associated with the following diagram

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\id}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Sigma_{e}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Sigma.}

Remark 7.1.5
.

The construction of
u_{e}
and
i_{e}
is compatible with the functoriality morphism of infinitesimal topoi
f_{\inf}:\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{e\inf})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sh}(Y/\Sigma_{e^{\prime}\inf})
for a map of rigid spaces
f:X/\Sigma_{e}\rightarrow Y/\Sigma_{e^{\prime}}
.
Namely we have the following commutative diagrams among infinitesimal topoi

\textstyle{\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{\inf})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u_{e}}
\scriptstyle{f_{\inf}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{e\inf})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{\inf}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sh}(Y/\Sigma_{\inf})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u_{e^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sh}(Y/\Sigma_{e^{\prime}\inf});}

\textstyle{\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{e\inf})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{e}}
\scriptstyle{f_{\inf}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{\inf})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{\inf}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sh}(Y/\Sigma_{e^{\prime}\inf})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{e^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sh}(Y/\Sigma_{\inf}).}

Relation to the rigid topos
\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{rig}})
.

Analogous to Subsection
2.3
, there exists a natural map of topoi to the rigid site
X_{\mathrm{rig}}
as below

u_{X/\Sigma}:\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{\inf})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{rig}}).

The corresponding preimage and the direct image functors are given as below

•

\displaystyle u_{X/\Sigma*}:\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{\inf})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{rig}});

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}

\displaystyle\longmapsto(U\mapsto\Gamma(U/\Sigma_{\inf},\mathcal{F})).

•

\displaystyle u_{X/\Sigma}^{-1}:\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{rig}})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{\inf});

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}

\displaystyle\longmapsto((U,T)\mapsto\mathcal{E}(U)).

Namely the push-forward functor
u_{X/\Sigma*}
is the sheafified version of the infinitesimal global section functor.

Remark 7.1.6
.

The functor
u_{X/\Sigma}
is functorial with respect to the rigid space
X
.
Precisely, given a map of rigid spaces
f:X\rightarrow Y
over
\Sigma
where
\xi
is nilpotent in both
\mathcal{O}_{X}
and
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
, we have the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{\inf})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u_{X/\Sigma}}
\scriptstyle{f_{\inf}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{rig}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sh}(Y/\Sigma_{\inf})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u_{Y/\Sigma}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sh}(Y_{\mathrm{rig}}).}

Remark 7.1.7
.

The functor
u_{X/\Sigma}
is also compatible with
u_{e}:\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{\inf})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{e\inf})
and
i_{e}:\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{e\inf})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{\inf})
.
Namely, the following diagrams commute:

\textstyle{\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{\inf})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u_{e}}
\scriptstyle{u_{X/\Sigma}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{rig}})}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{e\inf})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u_{X/\Sigma_{e}}}
;

\textstyle{\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{\inf})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u_{X/\Sigma}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{rig}})}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{e\inf})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{e}}
\scriptstyle{u_{X/\Sigma_{e}}}
.

Here
u_{X/\Sigma_{e}}:\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{e\inf})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{rig}})
is the analogous functor of
u_{X/\Sigma}
onto the rigid site defined in Subsection
2.3
.

7.2.
Cohomology of crystals over
X/\Sigma_{\inf}

In this section, we consider the cohomology of a crystal
\mathcal{F}
over the infinitesimal site
X/\Sigma_{\inf}
.
Our strategy is to interpret the cohomology of
\mathcal{F}
as the derived inverse limit of the cohomology of the pullback
i_{e}^{*}\mathcal{F}
, where
i_{e}^{*}\mathcal{F}
is a crystal over the site
X/\Sigma_{e\inf}
.

To start with, we first describe a crystal over the infinitesimal site
X/\Sigma_{\inf}
.

Definition 7.2.1
.

Let
X
be a rigid space over
\Sigma_{\inf}
where
\xi
is nilpotent.

(i)

The
infinitesimal structure sheaf
over
X/\Sigma_{\inf}
, denoted as
\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma}
, is a sheaf over
X/\Sigma_{\inf}
sending a thickening
(U,T)\in X/\Sigma_{\inf}
onto the global section of
\mathcal{O}_{T}
at
T
as below

\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma}:(U,T)\longmapsto\mathcal{O}_{T}(T).

(ii)

The
infinitesimal ideal sheaf
over
X/\Sigma_{\inf}
, denoted as
\mathcal{J}_{X/\Sigma}
, is a sheaf over
X/\Sigma_{\inf}
sending a thickening
(U,T)\in X/\Sigma_{\inf}
onto the global section of
\ker(\mathcal{O}_{T}\to\mathcal{O}_{U})
at
T
as below

\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma}:(U,T)\longmapsto\ker(\mathcal{O}_{T}(T)\to\mathcal{O}_{U}(U)).

(iii)

A
coherent crystal
over
X/\Sigma_{\inf}
is a
\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma}
-coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}
over
X/\Sigma_{\inf}
satisfies the crystal condition as in Definition
3.1.2
.
It is called
a crystal in vector bundle
if the restriction
\mathcal{F}_{T}
at each infinitesimal thickening
(U,T)\in X/\Sigma_{\inf}
is a vector bundle over
\mathcal{O}_{T}
.

Here we mention that similarly to Proposition
3.1.7
, it can be shown the categories crystals over big and small sites are equivalent.

We notice that the morphism of sites
i_{e}:X/\Sigma_{e\inf}\rightarrow X/\Sigma_{\inf}
in the last subsection is naturally a morphism of ringed sites for their structure sheaves.
Moreover, since the preimage functor
i_{e}^{-1}
is equal to the restriction functor onto the subcategory
X/\Sigma_{e\inf}
, we get

i_{e}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma}=\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma_{e}},

and similarly for the infinitesimal ideal sheaves.
So we can define the
pullback functor

i_{e}^{*}\mathcal{F}:=i_{e}^{-1}\mathcal{F}\otimes_{i_{e}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma}}\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma_{e}}
, which is the same as the restriction functor
i_{e}^{-1}\mathcal{F}
itself; namely for an infinitesimal thickening
(U,T)\in X/\Sigma_{e\inf}
, we have

(i_{e}^{*}\mathcal{F})_{T}=(i_{e}^{-1}\mathcal{F})_{T}=\mathcal{F}_{T}.

Here we want to remark that the pullback functor
i_{e}^{*}=i_{e}^{-1}
is compatible with the pullback functor
f_{\inf}^{*}
of the morphism
f_{\inf}:\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{e\inf})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sh}(Y/\Sigma_{e^{\prime}\inf})
for a map of rigid spaces
f:X/\Sigma_{e}\rightarrow Y/\Sigma_{e^{\prime}}
.

The main tool of the subsection is the following lemma, relating a coherent crystal over
X/\Sigma_{\inf}
with those over
X/\Sigma_{e\inf}
of
\xi
-nilpotent coefficients.

Lemma 7.2.2
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a coherent crystal over the infinitesimal site
X/\Sigma_{\inf}
(resp.
X/\Sigma_{{\mathrm{INF}}}
), and let
X
be defined over
\Sigma_{r}
for some
r\in\mathbb{N}
.
Then we have the following.

(i)

The pullback
i_{e}^{*}\mathcal{F}
for each
e\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq r}
is a crystal over
X/\Sigma_{e\inf}
.
When
\mathcal{F}
is a crystal in vector bundles, so is
\mathcal{F}
over
X/\Sigma_{e\inf}
.

(ii)

The counit map for the adjoint pairs
(i_{e}^{*},i_{e*})
induces the following isomorphism

\mathcal{F}/\xi^{e}\longrightarrow Ri_{e*}i_{e}^{*}\mathcal{F}.

In particular, we have the natural equivalences as below

\mathcal{F}\longrightarrow R\varprojlim_{e\geq r}\mathcal{F}/\xi^{e}\longrightarrow R\varprojlim_{e\geq r}Ri_{e*}i_{e}^{*}\mathcal{F}.

Here the transition maps in the last limit are given by the map of infinitesimal sites
X/\Sigma_{e\inf}\rightarrow X/\Sigma_{e+1\inf}
(resp.
X/\Sigma_{e{\mathrm{INF}}}\rightarrow X/\Sigma_{e+1{\mathrm{INF}}}
) for the closed immersions of bases.

Proof.

(1)

The proof of the (i) follows from the definition of the crystal condition.

(2)

We recall from the last subsection that the push-forward functor
i_{e*}\mathcal{G}
is given by

(i_{e*}\mathcal{G})(U,T)=\mathcal{G}(U,T\times_{\Sigma}\Sigma_{e}),

for a sheaf
\mathcal{G}\in\mathrm{Sh}(X/\Sigma_{e\inf})
.
We denote the fiber product
T\times_{\Sigma}\Sigma_{e}
by
T_{e}
, which is an infinitesimal thickening of
U
that is defined over
\Sigma_{e}
.
Apply the above to the pullback
\mathcal{G}=i_{e}^{*}\mathcal{F}
of the crystal
\mathcal{F}
, and notice that
i_{e}^{*}
is the restriction functor, we get

\displaystyle(Ri_{e*}i_{e}^{*}\mathcal{F})(U,T)

\displaystyle=R\Gamma((U,T_{e}),\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle=R\Gamma(T_{e},\mathcal{F}_{T_{e}})

\displaystyle=R\Gamma(T_{e},\mathcal{F}_{T}/\xi^{e})

\displaystyle=R\Gamma(T,\mathcal{F}_{T}/\xi^{e}),

where the last equality follows from the observation that
T_{e}\rightarrow T
has the same underlying topological spaces.
Hence the cone of
\mathcal{F}/\xi^{e}\rightarrow Ri_{e*}i_{e}^{*}\mathcal{F}
, which is bounded below and has no cohomology, vanishes in the derived category.

At last, notice that for a coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}
of
\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma}
-modules over
X/\Sigma_{\inf}
, we always have

\mathcal{F}\simeq R\varprojlim_{e\geq r}\mathcal{F}/\xi^{e}\simeq\varprojlim_{e\geq r}\mathcal{F}/\xi^{e}.

So the last claim in
(ii)
follows.

∎

Now we are able to give the main result about the cohomology of crystals over the infinitesimal site
X/\Sigma_{\inf}
.
Analogous to the case over
\Sigma_{e}
, for a coherent crystal
\mathcal{F}
over
X/\Sigma_{\inf}
, we define a canonical filtration on it by
{\mathrm{Fil}}^{i}\mathcal{F}:=\mathcal{J}_{X/\Sigma}^{i}\mathcal{F}
, for
i\in\mathbb{N}
.
This then naturally induces a filtration on its derived direct image along the functor
u_{X/\Sigma}
and on its derived global section respectively.

Theorem 7.2.3
.

Let
X
be a rigid space over some
\Sigma_{r}
, and let
\mathcal{F}
be a coherent crystal over
X/\Sigma_{\inf}
.

(i)

There exists a natural filtered isomorphism of complexes of sheaves of
{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}
-modules as below:

Ru_{X/\Sigma*}\mathcal{F}\longrightarrow R\varprojlim_{e\geq r}Ru_{X/\Sigma_{e}*}(i_{e}^{*}\mathcal{F}).

In particular, by applying the derived global section functor, we get a filtered isomorphism

R\Gamma(X/\Sigma_{\inf},\mathcal{F})\simeq R\varprojlim_{e\geq r}R\Gamma(X/\Sigma_{e\inf},i_{e}^{*}\mathcal{F}).

(ii)

Let
\{Y_{e}\}_{e\geq r}
be a direct system of rigid spaces over
\Sigma_{e}
, such that each
Y_{e}
is smooth over
\Sigma_{e}
with
Y_{e+1}\times_{\Sigma_{e+1}}\Sigma_{e}\simeq Y_{e}
.
Assume
X
admits a closed immersions into
Y_{r}
.
Then we have natural filtered isomorphisms of complexes of sheaves of
{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}
-modules as below

Ru_{X/\Sigma*}\mathcal{F}\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{D}\otimes\Omega_{D}^{\bullet}\simeq R\varprojlim_{e\geq r}(\mathcal{F}_{D_{X}(Y_{e})}\otimes\Omega_{D_{X}(Y_{e})}^{\bullet}),

where
D=\varinjlim_{e\geq r}D_{X}(Y_{e})
is the colimit
12
12

12

As in
Definition

2.2.1
, we again regard
D=\varinjlim_{e\geq r}D_{X}(Y_{e})
as an ind-representable sheaf in the infinitesimal topos, where the colimit always exits.

of envelopes, and
\mathcal{F}_{D}\otimes\Omega_{D}^{\bullet}
is the filtered de Rham complex of
\mathcal{F}
over
D

13
13

13

Here the filtration on the de Rham complex is defined analogously to the discussion above
Lemma

4.1.4
.
.

(iii)

Suppose
\mathcal{F}
is a crystal in vector bundles over
X/\Sigma_{\inf}
.
We equip the rings
{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}
and
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
with their
\xi
-adic filtrations.
Then for each
e\geq r
, the natural maps below are filtered isomorphisms

\displaystyle(Ru_{X/\Sigma*}\mathcal{F})\otimes_{{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}}^{L}{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow Ru_{X/\Sigma_{e}*}(i_{e}^{*}\mathcal{F});

\displaystyle Ru_{X/\Sigma*}\mathcal{F}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow R\varprojlim_{e}\left((Ru_{X/\Sigma*}\mathcal{F})\otimes_{{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}}^{L}{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}\right).

In particular, when
X
is quasi-compact quasi-separated, by applying the derived global section functor we obtain the following canonical filtered equivalences

\displaystyle R\Gamma(X/\Sigma_{\inf},\mathcal{F})\otimes_{{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}}^{L}{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}

\displaystyle\simeq R\Gamma(X/\Sigma_{e\inf},i_{e}^{*}\mathcal{F});

\displaystyle R\Gamma(X/\Sigma_{\inf},\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\simeq R\varprojlim_{e}\left(R\Gamma(X/\Sigma_{\inf},\mathcal{F})\otimes_{{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}}{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}\right).

Before we prove, we want to remark that the result for crystals over the big site
X/\Sigma_{{\mathrm{INF}}}
are true and the proof is identical to the small site case.

Proof.

(1)

This follows from applying
Ru_{X/\Sigma*}
to the equivalences
\mathcal{F}\longrightarrow R\varprojlim_{e\geq r}Ri_{e*}i_{e}^{*}\mathcal{F}
in Lemma
7.2.2
.
Here we use the identity of maps of topoi in the last subsection

u_{X/\Sigma}\circ i_{e}=u_{X/\Sigma_{e}}.

(2)

For each
e\geq r
, by Theorem
4.2.2
there exists a natural filtered isomorphism of complexes of sheaves of
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
-modules

Ru_{X/\Sigma_{e}*}i_{e}^{*}\mathcal{F}\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{D_{X}(Y_{e})}\otimes\Omega_{D_{X}(Y_{e})}^{\bullet}.

So the map of ringed sites
X/\Sigma_{e\inf}\rightarrow X/\Sigma_{e+1\inf}
induced from the closed immersion of the bases
\Sigma_{e}\rightarrow\Sigma_{e+1}
together with (i) produces the inverse limits

\displaystyle Ru_{X/\Sigma*}\mathcal{F}

\displaystyle\simeq R\varprojlim_{e\geq r}(\mathcal{F}_{D_{X}(Y_{e})}\otimes\Omega_{D_{X}(Y_{e})}^{\bullet})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathcal{F}_{D}\otimes\Omega_{D}^{\bullet},

where we use the compatibility of the de Rham complexes
\mathcal{F}_{D_{X}(Y_{e})}\otimes\Omega_{D_{X}(Y_{e})}^{\bullet}
for different
e
, by our choices of the direct system of smooth rigid spaces
\{Y_{e}\}_{e}
.

(3)

We first notice that the second half of the statement follows from its sheaf version, by the following isomorphism

R\Gamma(U,(Ru_{X/\Sigma*}\mathcal{F})\otimes_{{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}}^{L}{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}})\simeq R\Gamma(U/\Sigma_{\inf},\mathcal{F})\otimes_{{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}}{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}.

Here the isomorphism follows by applying
R\Gamma(U,-)
at the distinguished triangle resolving
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
over
{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}
as below

\textstyle{Ru_{X/\Sigma*}\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cdot\xi^{e}}
\textstyle{Ru_{X/\Sigma*}\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(Ru_{X/\Sigma*}\mathcal{F})\otimes_{{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}}^{L}{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}.}

On the other hand, to check the sheaf level isomorphism, as the statement is rigid analytic local with respect to
X
, so it suffices to assume that
X
admits a closed immersion into a direct system of smooth rigid spaces
\{Y_{e}\}_{e}
over
\Sigma_{e}
, where the results follow from the explicit calculation of the completed de Rham complexes as in part (ii) and Theorem
4.2.2
.
So we are done.

∎

Remark 7.2.4
.

Recall that for a smooth affinoid rigid space
X=\mathrm{Spa}(R)
over
K
, the crystalline cohomology of
X
over
{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}
, introduced in
[
BMS
, Section 13]
, is defined as the inverse limit

\varprojlim_{e\in\mathbb{N}}\Omega_{D_{X}(Y_{e})}^{\bullet},

where
X\rightarrow Y_{e}=\mathrm{Spa}({\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}\langle T_{i}^{\pm 1}\rangle)
is a closed immersion.
So Theorem
7.2.3
implies that the infinitesimal cohomology
R\Gamma(X/\Sigma_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma})
coincides with the crystalline cohomology of
X
over
{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}
in the sense of
[
BMS
]
.

With the help of Theorem
7.2.3
, we can compare the infinitesimal cohomology of
X
over
{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}
with the derived de Rham complex.

Definition 7.2.5
.

Let
X
be a rigid space over
\Sigma_{r}
.
Then the
analytic derived de Rham complex of
X
over
{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}
, denoted as
\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}_{X/\Sigma}
, is defined to be the derived inverse limit of the filtered complexes

\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}_{X/\Sigma}:=R\varprojlim_{e\geq r}\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}_{X/\Sigma_{e}}.

Apply Theorem
7.2.3
.(i) to the infinitesimal structure sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma}
and the comparison in Theorem
5.5.5
,
we get the following.

Corollary 7.2.6
.

Let
X
be a rigid space over
\Sigma_{r}
.
There exists a natural filtered map between the analytic derived de Rham complex and the infinitesimal cohomology sheaves, inducing an isomorphism on the underlying complexes

\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}_{X/\Sigma}\longrightarrow Ru_{X/\Sigma*}\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma}.

In particular, applying the derived global section on the underlying complexes, we get the following comparison of cohomology

R\Gamma(X,\widehat{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{an}}_{X/\Sigma})\simeq R\Gamma(X/\Sigma_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma}).

The next result concerns the éh descent for cohomology of crystals over the big infinitesimal site
X/\Sigma_{{\mathrm{INF}}}
, where
X
is a rigid space over
K
.

Proposition 7.2.7
.

Let
X
be a rigid space over
K
, and let
\mathcal{F}
be a crystal in vector bundles over the big infiniteismal site
X/\Sigma_{{\mathrm{INF}}}
.
Then the cohomology sheaf
Ru_{X/\Sigma*}\mathcal{F}
(without the filtration) satisfies the éh-hyperdescent.
Namely for an éh-hypercovering
X_{\bullet}^{\prime}\rightarrow X^{\prime}
of
K
-rigid spaces over
X
, the following natural map is an isomorphism

R\Gamma(X^{\prime}/\Sigma_{{\mathrm{INF}}},\mathcal{F})\longrightarrow R\lim_{[n]\in\Delta}\left(R\Gamma(X^{\prime}_{n}/\Sigma_{{\mathrm{INF}}},\mathcal{F})\right).

Proof.

By Lemma
7.2.2
.(i), the pullback
i_{e}^{*}\mathcal{F}
over
X/\Sigma_{e{\mathrm{INF}}}
is a crystal in vector bundles.
Thanks to Theorem
6.2.5
, we know the natural map
X_{\bullet}^{\prime}\rightarrow X^{\prime}
induces a natural isomorphism as below

R\Gamma(X^{\prime}/\Sigma_{{\mathrm{INF}}},i_{e}^{*}\mathcal{F})\longrightarrow R\lim_{[n]\in\Delta}\left(R\Gamma(X^{\prime}_{n}/\Sigma_{{\mathrm{INF}}},i_{e}^{*}\mathcal{F})\right).

Thus the result we want follows from taking the derived limit over all
e
, by Theorem
7.2.3
.(i).
∎

We want to mention that thanks to the Corollary
2.2.8
, it is safe to replace the cohomology of
\mathcal{F}
over the big infinitesimal site by the cohomology
R\Gamma(X^{\prime}/\Sigma_{\inf},\iota^{-1}\mathcal{F})
of the restriction
\iota^{-1}\mathcal{F}
over the small infinitesimal site
X/\Sigma_{\inf}
.
In particular, by applying the above result to the infinitesimal structure sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma}
, we see the infinitesimal cohomology over
{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}
satisfies the éh-hyperdescent.

Corollary 7.2.8
.

Let
X
be a rigid space over
K
.
Then the infinitesimal cohomology
R\Gamma(X/\Sigma_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma})
satisfies the éh-hyperdescent.

Another quick upshot is the finiteness of the infinitesimal cohomology for a proper rigid space
X
.

Proposition 7.2.9
.

Let
X
be a proper rigid space of dimension
n
over
K
,
and let
\mathcal{F}
be a coherent crystal.
The infinitesimal cohomology
R\Gamma(X/\Sigma_{{\mathrm{INF}}},\mathcal{F})
is then a perfect
{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}
-complex in cohomological degrees
[0,2n]
.

Proof.

Thanks to Theorem
7.2.3
.(i), we can write
R\Gamma(X/\Sigma_{{\mathrm{INF}}},\mathcal{F})
as the derived limit of
R\Gamma(X/\Sigma_{e{\mathrm{INF}}},i_{e}^{*}\mathcal{F})
.
Here each
R\Gamma(X/\Sigma_{e{\mathrm{INF}}},i_{e}^{*}\mathcal{F})
is a bounded complex in cohomological degree
[0,2n]
such that each cohomology group is finite over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
(Proposition
6.3.1
).
So the result then follows from the short exact sequence

0\longrightarrow R^{1}\varprojlim_{e}\mathrm{H}^{i-1}(X/\Sigma_{e{\mathrm{INF}}},i_{e}^{*}\mathcal{F})\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{i}(X/\Sigma_{{\mathrm{INF}}},\mathcal{F})\longrightarrow\varprojlim_{e}\mathrm{H}^{i}(X/\Sigma_{e{\mathrm{INF}}},i_{e}^{*}\mathcal{F})\longrightarrow 0.

Here we note that the inverse system
\{\mathrm{H}^{2n}(X/\Sigma_{e{\mathrm{INF}}},i_{e}^{*}\mathcal{F})\}_{e}
satisfies the Mittag-Leffler condition,
by the finiteness of each
\mathrm{H}^{2n}(X/\Sigma_{e{\mathrm{INF}}},i_{e}^{*}\mathcal{F})
over
{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}
.
∎

7.3.
Comparison with pro-étale cohomology

In this subsection, we compare the infinitesimal cohomology of
X/\Sigma_{\inf}
with the pro-étale cohomology of the de Rham period sheaf
{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}}
.
As an application, we show the degeneracy of the Hodge–de Rham spectral sequence, together with a torsionfreeness of the infinitesimal cohomology
\mathrm{H}^{i}(X/\Sigma,\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma_{\inf}})
over
{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}
.
Throughout the section, we will assume the basics of the pro-étale topology defined in
[
Sch13
]
.

Comparison theorem.

Let
X
be a rigid space over
K
, and let
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
be the pro-étale site of
X
.
The pro-étale site admits a basis, which consists of affinoid adic spaces
U=\mathrm{Spa}(B,B^{+})
that are pro-étale over
X
and are
affinoid perfectoid
(namely, the Huber pair
(B,B^{+})
is a perfectoid algebra over
K
).
Over the pro-étale site, we can associate the complete structure sheaf
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
, whose section at an affinoid perfectoid space
U=\mathrm{Spa}(B,B^{+})
is the
K
-algebra
B
.
Denote
\nu:X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\rightarrow X_{{\mathrm{rig}}}
to be the canonical morphism from the pro-étale site to the rigid site of
X
.

We recall from
[
Sch13
]
that the
de Rham period sheaf
{\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}
, defined as a sheaf of
{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}
-algebras over
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
, sending an affinoid perfectoid space
U=\mathrm{Spa}(B,B^{+})
onto the ring

{\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}(B,B^{+}):=\varprojlim_{m}\left(W(\varprojlim_{x\mapsto x^{p}}B^{+}/p)[\frac{1}{p}]/\xi^{m}\right).

The sheaf
{\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}
admits a canonical surjection
\theta:{\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
that is compatible with the surjection map
\theta:{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}\rightarrow K
for the period ring
{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}
.
It can be shown that
\xi
is a nonzero-divisor in
{\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}
locally, and the ideal
\ker(\theta)\subset{\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}
is generated by
\xi\in{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}
.
So we could invert the element
\xi
to get a sheaf of
{\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}}={\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}[\frac{1}{\xi}]
-algebras over
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
, which we denote by
{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}}
.
The sheaf of rings
{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}}
then admits a natural descending filtration where the
i
-th filtration for
\forall i\in\mathbb{Z}
is defined by
{\mathrm{Fil}}^{i}{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}}:=\xi^{i}{\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}\subset{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}}
.
Each graded piece
{\mathrm{gr}}^{i}{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}}
, which is locally equal to
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\cdot\xi^{i}
, is canonically isomorphic to the pro-étale structure sheaf up to a twist.

We first recall the comparison between the infinitesimal cohomology and the pro-étale cohomology of
{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}}
for smooth rigid spaces.

Theorem 7.3.1

(
[
BMS
]
, Theorem 13.1)
.

Let
X
be a smooth rigid space over
K
.
Then there exists a natural map of complexes of sheaves of
{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}
-modules over
X

Ru_{X/\Sigma*}\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma}\longrightarrow R\nu_{*}{\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}.

It is an isomorphism after inverting
\xi
.

Proof.

This is essentially proved in the
[
BMS
]
, Theorem 13.1, and we explain here the relation of their result with our statement.

Let
X
be a smooth rigid space over
K
of dimension
d
.
Assume
U=\mathrm{Spa}(R)
is a
very small
affinoid open subset in
X
; namely it admits an étale morphism onto a torus
\mathbb{T}^{d}_{K}
, where the map can be extended to a closed immersion into a larger torus
\mathbb{T}^{n}=\mathrm{Spa}(K\langle T_{i}^{\pm 1}\rangle)
.
For any such closed immersion, we could associate the torus
\mathbb{T}^{n}
an affinoid perfectoid space
\mathbb{T}^{n,\infty}=\mathrm{Spa}(K\langle T_{i}^{\pm\frac{1}{p^{\infty}}}\rangle)
.
The canonical map
\mathbb{T}^{n,\infty}\rightarrow\mathbb{T}^{n}
is pro-étale, and its pullback along
U\rightarrow\mathbb{T}^{n}
produces a pro-étale morphism from an affinoid perfectoid space
\mathrm{Spa}(R_{\infty},R^{+}_{\infty})
over
U=\mathrm{Spa}(R)
.

We denote by
D
the envelope of
U
inside of the direct system
\{\mathbb{T}^{n}_{{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}}\}_{e}
of tori over
\{{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}\}_{e}
.
Then for any such choice of morphisms
(U\rightarrow\mathbb{T}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{T}^{n})
, we could construct two
{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}
-linear complexes

•

The de Rham complex
\Omega_{D}^{\bullet}
of
U
in
\{\mathbb{T}^{n}_{{\mathrm{B^{+}_{dR,e}}}}\}_{e}
, that computes the infinitesimal cohomology
R\Gamma(U/\Sigma_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma})
by Theorem
7.2.3
.

•

The Koszul complex
K_{{\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}(R_{\infty})}=K_{{\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}(R_{\infty})}(\gamma_{u_{i}}-1)
, that computes the pro-étale cohomology
R\Gamma(U_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},{\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}})
.

As in the proof of the
[
BMS
, Theorem 13.1]
, for any choice of
(U\rightarrow\mathbb{T}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{T}^{n})
, there exists a natural map of actual complexes

\Omega_{D}^{\bullet}\longrightarrow K_{{\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}(R_{\infty})},

which is functorial with respect to the choices of triples, such that it becomes an isomorphism after inverting
\xi
.
Notice that the set of triples for a fixed
U
is filtered, and the transition map of both complexes are isomorphisms.
In this way, the induced isomorphism

R\Gamma(U/\Sigma_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma})[\frac{1}{\xi}]\rightarrow R\Gamma(U_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}})

is independent of the triples
(U\rightarrow\mathbb{T}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{T}^{n})
.
Since the collection of very small open subsets of
X
form a basis in the rigid topology, we could then get a natural isomorphism as below

Ru_{X/\Sigma*}\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma}[\frac{1}{\xi}]\longrightarrow R\nu_{*}{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}}.

∎

Using the éh-hyperdescent, we could improve the above result into the non-smooth situations.

Theorem 7.3.2
.

Let
X
be a rigid space over
K
.
Then there exists a natural map of complexes of sheaves of
{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}
-modules over
X
as below

Ru_{X/\Sigma*}\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma}\longrightarrow R\nu_{*}{\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}.

It is an isomorphism after inverting
\xi
.

Proof.

In the proof, we use
\nu_{X}:X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\rightarrow X_{\mathrm{rig}}
to denote the natural map of sites associated with the rigid space
X
.

We first notice that the pro-étale cohomology sheaf
R\nu_{X*}{\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}
and
R\nu_{X*}{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}}
satisfy the éh-hyperdescent.
To see this, we recall from
[
Guo19
, Section 4]
that the derived push-forward
R\nu_{X*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
is naturally isomorphic to
R\pi_{X*}C
, where
C=R\alpha_{*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{v}\in D^{\geq 0}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}})
is the derived push-forward of the completed
v
-structure sheaf (see
[
Guo19
, Section 3.2]
), and
\pi_{X}:X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}\rightarrow X_{\mathrm{rig}}
is the natural map of sites.
As an upshot, since
C
is a bounded below complex of éh-sheaves, its direct image
R\pi_{X*}C
in the rigid site naturally satisfies the éh-hyperdescent; namely for an éh-hypercovering
\rho:X_{\bullet}\rightarrow X
over
K
, the induced map below is an isomorphism

R\pi_{X*}C\longrightarrow R\rho_{*}R\pi_{X_{\bullet}*}C.

We could then replace the above by the derived push-forward of the pro-étale structure sheaf to get a natural isomorphism

R\nu_{X*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\longrightarrow R\rho_{*}R\nu_{X_{\bullet}*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{\bullet}}.

On the other hand, notice the de Rham period sheaf
{\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}
is completed under the
\xi
-adic topology such that the
i
-th graded piece is equal to the complete structure sheaf
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\cdot\xi^{i}
up to a twist.
In this way, by the hyperdescent for graded pieces and the induction on
e
, we get

\displaystyle R\nu_{X*}{\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}

\displaystyle=R\varprojlim_{e\in\mathbb{N}}R\nu_{X*}{\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}/\xi^{e}

\displaystyle\simeq R\varprojlim_{e\in\mathbb{N}}R\rho_{*}R\nu_{X_{\bullet}*}{\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}/\xi^{e}

\displaystyle\simeq R\rho_{*}R\varprojlim_{e\in\mathbb{N}}R\nu_{X_{\bullet}*}{\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}/\xi^{e}

\displaystyle=R\rho_{*}R\nu_{X_{\bullet}*}{\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}.

Namely the pro-étale cohomology of
{\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}
hence
{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}}={\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}[\frac{1}{\xi}]
satisfies the éh-hyperdescent.

At last, notice that the collection of maps
f:X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
for smooth rigid spaces
X^{\prime}
form a basis of the éh-site
X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}
.
In this way, the natural comparison map
Ru_{X^{\prime}/\Sigma*}\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}/\Sigma}\rightarrow R\nu_{X^{\prime}*}{\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}
for smooth
X^{\prime}
extends to a map for
X
via the éh-hyperdescent (for the infinitesimal cohomology sheaf, this is Theorem
7.2.7
), and by inverting
\xi
we get the isomorphism

Ru_{X/\Sigma*}\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma}[\frac{1}{\xi}]\longrightarrow R\nu_{*}{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}}.

∎

Remark 7.3.3
.

The morphism between the infinitesimal cohomology and the pro-étale cohomology is constructed in an indirect way.
In fact, by enlarging the infinitesimal site
X/\Sigma_{\inf}
to a bigger site that allows all (adic spectra of) complete Huber rings as in
[
Yao19
, Construction 5.11]
,
the de Rham period ring
{\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}(R_{\infty})
for a perfectoid algebra
R_{\infty}
can then be regarded as a pro-thickening in this enlarged category.
In this way, the arrow from the associated ind-object to the final object in the enlarged infinitesimal topos will induce a map on their cohomology, and it can be checked via computations in smooth case and the éh-hyperdescent that this coincides with our morphism.

Below we consider a special case when
X
comes from a small subfield.
Precisely, let
K_{0}
be a discretely valued subfield of
K
such that the residue field of
K_{0}
is perfect.
Assume
Y
is a proper rigid space over
K_{0}
, and
X=Y\times_{K_{0}}K
is the base field extension of
X_{0}
.
We recall from
[
Guo19
, Theorem 8.2.2]
that there exists a
\Gal(K/K_{0})
-equivariant filtered comparison between the pro-étale cohomology
R\Gamma(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}})
and the tensor product

R\Gamma(Y_{{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}},\Omega_{{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}},/K_{0}}^{\bullet})\otimes_{K_{0}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}.

Here
\Omega_{{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}},/K_{0}}^{i}
is the éh-differential for rigid spaces over
K_{0}
, and the filtration is defined by the product filtration, where the éh de Rham cohomology
R\Gamma(Y_{{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}},\Omega_{{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}},/K_{0}}^{\bullet})
is equipped with a natural descending filtration by
{\mathrm{Fil}}^{i}=R\Gamma(Y_{{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}},\Omega_{{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}},/K_{0}}^{\geq i})
.
Moreover, by taking the zero-th graded pieces, we get

R\Gamma(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X})\simeq\moplus_{i}R\Gamma(Y_{{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}},\Omega_{{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}},/K_{0}}^{i})\otimes_{K_{0}}K(-i).

From this, we get the following:

Corollary 7.3.4
.

Let
Y
be a proper rigid space over the discretely valued subfield
K_{0}
of
K
as above, and let
X
be its base extension to
K
.
Then we have a canonical isomorphism

R\Gamma(X/\Sigma_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma})[\frac{1}{\xi}]\simeq R\Gamma(Y_{{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}},\Omega_{{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}},/K_{0}}^{\bullet})\otimes_{K_{0}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}.

In particular, the infinitesimal cohomology of
Y\times_{K_{0}}K
over
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}
admits a
\Gal(K/K_{0})
-equivariant filtration such that the zero-th graded factor is equal to

\moplus_{i}R\Gamma(Y_{{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}},\Omega_{{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}},/K_{0}}^{i})\otimes_{K_{0}}K(-i).

Torsion freeness and Hodge–de Rham degeneracy.

For the rest of the subsection, we prove the infinitesimal cohomology
\mathrm{H}^{n}(X/\Sigma_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma})
is torsionfree over
{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}
(namely
Theorem

1.2.7
.(vi)), and show the degeneracy for the éh Hodge–de Rham spectral sequence.

Theorem 7.3.5
.

Let
X
be a proper rigid space over
K
.
Then we have the following

(1)

the infinitesimal cohomology
\mathrm{H}^{n}(X/\Sigma_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma})
is torsionfree over
{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}
, for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

(2)

the éh Hodge–de Rham spectral sequence over
K
below degenerates at its first page:

E_{1}^{i,j}=\mathrm{H}^{j}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}},\Omega_{{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}},X/K}^{i})\Longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{i+j}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}},\Omega_{{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}},X/K}^{\bullet}).

Remark 7.3.6
.

Note that the part (ii) generalizes the degeneracy result in
[
Guo19
, Proposition 8.0.8]
, where the latter needs the assumption for
X
to be defined over a discretely valued subfield.

Proof.

Let
n
be any integer.
We first note that the pro-étale cohomology
\mathrm{H}^{n}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},{\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}})
is finite free over
{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}
:
To see this, we recall the Primitive Comparison Theorem over
{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}
as below (
[
Sch13b
, Thm. 3.17]
)

\mathrm{H}^{n}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Q}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}\simeq\mathrm{H}^{n}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},{\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}).

As the étale cohomology
\mathrm{H}^{n}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is a finite dimensional vector space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, the above implies the finite freeness of
\mathrm{H}^{n}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},{\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}})
over
{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}
.
In particular, by Theorem
7.3.2
and the finiteness in Proposition
7.2.9
, we get the following relations

\displaystyle\dim_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathrm{H}^{n}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Q}_{p})

\displaystyle=\rank_{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}\mathrm{H}^{n}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},{\mathbb{B}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}})

\displaystyle=\dim_{\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}}\mathrm{H}^{n}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}})

\displaystyle=\dim_{\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}}\mathrm{H}^{n}(X/\Sigma_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma})[\frac{1}{\xi}]

\displaystyle=\rank_{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}\mathrm{H}^{n}(X/\Sigma_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma})/torsion

\displaystyle\leq\dim_{K}\mathrm{H}^{n}(X/\Sigma_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma})/\xi,

where the last equality follows from the structure theorem of finite generated modules over the principal ideal domain
{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}
.

On the other hand, by the base change formula in Theorem
7.2.3
.(iii), for each
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, we get the following short exact sequence of
K
-vector spaces

0\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{n}(X/\Sigma_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma})/\xi\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{n}(X/K_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X/K})\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{n+1}(X/\Sigma_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma})[\xi]\longrightarrow 0,

which implies the inequalities

\dim_{K}\mathrm{H}^{n}(X/\Sigma_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma})/\xi\leq\dim_{K}\mathrm{H}^{n}(X/K_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X/K}),~\forall n\in\mathbb{Z}.

In addition, notice that the cohomology complex of a sheaf of abelian groups always lives in the non-negative cohomological degrees.
So both
\mathrm{H}^{n}(X/\Sigma_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma})
and
\mathrm{H}^{n}(X/K_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X/K})
vanish for
n\leq-1
, and the short exact sequence above for
n=-1
implies the vanishing of
\mathrm{H}^{0}(X/\Sigma_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma})[\xi]
.
In particular, we see the
{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}
-module
\mathrm{H}^{0}(X/\Sigma_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma})
is torsionfree.

Now using the comparison between the infinitesimal cohomology and the éh de Rham cohomology for the trivial crystal
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{O}_{X/K}
in Theorem
6.2.2
, we have

\dim_{K}\mathrm{H}^{n}(X/K_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X/K})=\dim_{K}\mathrm{H}^{n}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}},\Omega_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}^{\bullet}).

Note that the natural Hodge filtration on the éh de Rham complex
\Omega_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}^{\bullet}
induces the
E_{1}
spectral sequence

E_{1}^{i,j}=\mathrm{H}^{j}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}},\Omega_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}^{i})\Longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{i+j}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}},\Omega_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}^{\bullet}).

As a consequence, we get

\dim_{K}\mathrm{H}^{n}(X/K_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X/K})\leq\sum_{i+j=n}\dim_{K}\mathrm{H}^{j}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}},\Omega_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}^{i}).

However, by Hodge–Tate decomposition in
[
Guo19
, Theorem 1.1.3]
, we have

\dim_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathrm{H}^{n}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\sum_{i+j=n}\dim_{K}\mathrm{H}^{j}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}},\Omega_{\mathrm{\acute{e}h}}^{i}).

Hence combining all the relations of dimensions above, we see all the inequalities should be equalities.
The latter implies that the
E_{1}
spectral sequence degenerates at the first page, and for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{n+1}(X/\Sigma_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma})[\xi]=0.

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{n}(X/\Sigma_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma})/\xi=\mathrm{H}^{n}(X/\Sigma_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X/K}).

These, together with the torsionfreeness of the
{\mathrm{B_{dR}^{+}}}
-module
\mathrm{H}^{0}(X/\Sigma_{\inf},\mathcal{O}_{X/\Sigma})
, conclude the proof.
∎
